# Usher M5 — Push and Read-Through Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Make a source's own changes arrive in seconds instead of overnight, and make the catalog answer a client from local state whatever the source is doing — a WebSocket channel whose health is grounded in messages received rather than in a socket being open, a supervised reconnect that closes its own gap with a delta walk, a title read that promotes its enrichment job to the front of the queue and returns the stub immediately, and an SSE channel that tells the client when the filled-in version lands.

**Architecture:** `EmbyPushChannel` (`adapters/emby/push.py`) owns one connection: it builds `/embywebsocket?api_key=…&deviceId=…` inside a function that never returns it, subscribes with `SessionsStart`, maps Emby's three message types onto `SourceEvent`s, and maintains a **`PushHealth` ledger keyed on messages received**. A staleness watchdog raises `PortUnavailable` out of the iterator when nothing has arrived for `push_stale_after_seconds`, so a socket that upgrades and then delivers nothing is treated as a dead socket rather than a healthy one. `PushSupervisor` (`services/push.py`) wraps that in a reconnect loop with equal-jitter backoff, runs a delta reconcile *after* connecting so the gap is closed with the socket already buffering, resets its failure counter only on evidence of delivery, and after `push_max_consecutive_failures` writes `supports_push = false` and leaves the source to the nightly walk. Applied events go through M4's existing chain — `IngestService.ingest_batch` and a `WatchStateSyncService.apply_states` extracted from `_flush` — so nothing about ADR-0014's absence-is-not-zero rule is re-implemented on the push path. Client-facing, an `EventPublisher` port with an in-memory bus fans `title.updated` / `watchstate.updated` / `sync.progress` out to `GET /events` over SSE, with a bounded per-subscriber queue whose overflow answers `resync_required` rather than skipping silently, and a bounded replay ring for `Last-Event-ID`. `GET /titles/{id}` is the read-through surface: it answers from `titles` + `media_items` + `watch_states` alone, never raises for a degraded source, and enqueues the title's `enrich` job at `JobPriority.DEMAND`. The server process grows two supervised lanes (push, and the job worker) so that demand → enrich → SSE closes inside one process.

**Tech Stack:** Python 3.13 · `websockets` 16 (asyncio client) · FastAPI `StreamingResponse` (SSE, no new dependency) · SQLAlchemy 2.0 async + asyncpg · PostgreSQL 17 (`pgvector/pgvector:pg17`) · httpx · pydantic v2 · loguru · OpenTelemetry (spans, links, observable gauges) · pytest · testcontainers · ruff · mypy strict · import-linter

---

## The one thing this milestone must not get wrong

ADR-0004's live verification of 2026-07-29 confirmed that Emby push works — `/embywebsocket` upgrades with 101, `Sessions` arrives periodically, and `UserDataChanged` fires within seconds of an out-of-band change. It also recorded a caveat that is not a footnote, it is the shape of the whole milestone:

> **One genuine quirk:** a control handshake against a nonexistent path also upgrades and receives `Sessions`, so Emby's listener appears to handle the upgrade regardless of path. Harmless, but it means path alone is not a health signal — the adapter's health check must assert on *received messages*, not on a successful handshake.

And the lesson from the same run, which is the general form of it:

> **silence on an event stream is not evidence of a broken stream.** Verify push channels by causing an event, never by waiting.

Put together: **a push connection that looks healthy while delivering nothing is the default failure mode, not an edge case.** A reverse proxy that forwards `Upgrade` and then buffers; a NAT table that drops the flow while both endpoints still believe it is open; a server that accepted the subscription and lost the listener. In every one of those the socket object is fine, `supports_push` returns `True` if it is defined as "is the socket open", the reconciler is told it does not need to cover this source, and watch state silently stops flowing until somebody notices by hand.

**The decision: nothing in this milestone reports push health from the state of a socket. Every such answer is derived from a message ledger, and a channel that stops delivering is torn down rather than trusted.**

Four places that rule is made structural rather than remembered:

1. **`PushHealth` counts messages, and `supports_push` reads the count.**

   ```python
   @dataclass(slots=True)
   class PushHealth:
       stale_after: float
       connected: bool = False
       opened_at: float | None = None
       last_message_at: float | None = None
       messages_received: int = 0
       events_emitted: int = 0
       reconnects: int = 0

       def is_delivering(self, now: float) -> bool:
           # `connected` alone is the answer this whole milestone refuses to
           # give. All three clauses, and the message count is the one that
           # makes an upgraded-but-silent socket read False.
           return (
               self.connected
               and self.messages_received > 0
               and self.last_message_at is not None
               and now - self.last_message_at <= self.stale_after
           )
   ```

   `EmbyAdapter.supports_push` is `self._health.is_delivering(self._clock())` and nothing else. There is no code path from "the connection object exists" to `True`.

2. **A staleness watchdog raises out of the iterator.** `PushConnection.recv(timeout)` raises `TimeoutError` when nothing arrived — a *tick*, not a failure — and each tick runs the watchdog. Past `stale_after`, the channel raises `PortUnavailable` and the supervisor reconnects. This is deliberately **not** what `websockets`' own `ping_interval`/`ping_timeout` covers: a peer that answers pongs while delivering nothing passes the WebSocket keepalive and fails this one, and that peer is exactly the failure ADR-0004 warns about.

3. **The supervisor's failure counter is reset by delivery, not by connection.** `failures = 0` happens on the transition of `adapter.supports_push` to `True`, never on a successful `connect`. A socket that upgrades, delivers nothing for `stale_after`, is torn down, and upgrades again therefore *walks the counter up to the ceiling* and ends with `supports_push = false` persisted on the `Source` row — which is what hands the source back to the nightly reconcile, which is what PRD 08's failure table says must happen.

4. **`SourceStatus.push_available` is never a probe of a throwaway socket.** `verify()` does not open one at all; it reports the health of the channel that is *actually running*, injected into `SourceService` by the composition root, and `None` when no lane is running. The on-demand diagnostic is `usher push --probe`, which opens a socket, waits bounded, and reports **what arrived** — message types and counts — rather than that the handshake succeeded.

**What this costs, stated plainly.** A source whose library genuinely never changes still delivers `Sessions` on the subscription interval, so `is_delivering` stays true on an idle library — that is measured (ADR-0004 saw `Sessions` arrive periodically with nothing playing) and it is the property the whole scheme rests on. If a future Emby build stopped sending periodic `Sessions`, `stale_after` would start firing on a healthy idle socket and the lane would reconnect every 90 seconds. The mitigation is that reconnecting is cheap and correct (the gap-closing delta returns 0 items), and that `usher.source.push.reconnects` makes the condition visible rather than silent. Task 30's live run measures the actual `Sessions` interval, and `push_stale_after_seconds` is a setting so an operator can raise it.

**Why not the alternatives.**

- *Report `supports_push` from `connection.state == OPEN`.* This is the failure. It is also what every WebSocket client library encourages, which is why it needs a structural answer rather than a convention.
- *Rely on `ping_interval`/`ping_timeout` alone.* They detect a dead TCP peer, which is the easy half. They cannot detect a live peer that has stopped delivering, and ADR-0004's control handshake against a nonexistent path is a demonstration that Emby's own listener will happily hold such a socket.
- *Probe on every `GET /admin/sources/{id}/status`.* A dashboard polling that route would open a socket per poll against a server PRD 01 measures at 1–5 s per request, and would still be answering a question about a socket that is not the one doing the work.

---

## Scope: what M5 is, and six boundary calls made deliberately

PRD [09](../prd/09-roadmap.md) states M5 as "WebSocket events, reconnect/reconcile, demand promotion, SSE to clients". Six places that boundary is ambiguous, decided here.

**1. M5 adds exactly two client-facing routes: `GET /titles/{id}` and `GET /events`. No others.** Demand promotion is *defined* by PRD 03 as a property of requesting a title ("Requesting a `stub` promotes its enrichment job to the front of the queue and returns immediately"), so without a title route it is a mechanism with no caller and no test that exercises it end to end — which is exactly the state M4 deliberately left it in. SSE is defined by PRD 07 as a route. Everything else on PRD 07's surface stays where the roadmap puts it: `/home` is M7's, `/search` and `/browse` are M6's, `/play` and the image proxy are M9's, and the three admin routes (`POST /admin/sources/{id}/sync`, `GET /admin/unmatched`, `POST /admin/unmatched/{id}/resolve`) remain M9's exactly as PRD 09's boundary call 4 states — M4's CLI already delivers all three capabilities.

**2. `GET /titles/{id}` returns metadata, availability and watch state — not credits, images, similar titles, or the season/episode hierarchy.** PRD 07 lists all of those on the same endpoint, and each one belongs to a milestone that has not run: `Person`/`Credit` and `Collection` are populated by M7, `Image` by M9 (PRD 09 boundary call 2), and `GET /series/{id}/seasons` is its own M9 route. Returning `"credits": []` for a title whose credits simply have not been derived yet is the response-shaped version of the empty-dashboard-panel problem — a client cannot tell it from a title with no cast. The three fields M5 *does* return are each backed by a real table M4 fills.

**3. Outbound watch state — `PUT /watch/titles/{id}` and the source write-back — is not built. M9 owns it.** `SourceAdapter.push_watch_state` exists and was verified against the live server in M3, and M4's live run made the one real write it has ever made. What is missing is the *caller*: PRD 03's outbound path is "write `WatchState` with `origin = api`, then push to the source best-effort; failure enqueues a retry and never blocks the API response", and that retry is a fourth `JobKind` whose handler needs a source binding and a conflict rule against inbound push arriving in the same second. That is a milestone's worth of decisions about a surface PRD 07 puts in the Actions table alongside `/play`, which is M9's. M5's subject is the *inbound* half, and mixing the two would make the conflict rule ("latest `updated_at` wins, and the window is seconds") a thing decided in passing.

**4. A push `ITEM_REMOVED` event does not retract availability.** [ADR-0015](../prd/decisions/0015-availability-is-retracted-only-by-a-finished-walk.md) is unambiguous — "only a full walk sweeps" — and its whole argument is that Usher cannot distinguish a genuine deletion from an unmounted drive or a permissions change, and that one of the two is irreversible. An Emby `LibraryChanged.ItemsRemoved` during a library refresh can name thousands of items that have not gone anywhere. So M5 counts and logs the event, publishes nothing, and leaves the row available until the nightly full walk sweeps it. PRD 08 already prices this: "Availability goes stale, not wrong." The follow-up this leaves open is named rather than implied — a `MediaItemRepository.retract_confirmed_missing(source_id, external_ids)` that retracts only ids the source named **and** that `get_item` independently confirmed absent, capped at `push_max_items_per_event` so no mass retraction is reachable. That is a change to ADR-0015 and belongs in its own change.

**5. The client event bus is in-memory and in-process, and the server process therefore runs the job worker.** PRD 01's concurrency model is one process with per-lane semaphores, and `api/deps.py`'s own comment already predicted where the TMDb client has to live ("It belongs on `app.state` at lifespan"). Making the bus cross-process would mean a Postgres `LISTEN/NOTIFY` transport, an ADR, and a second implementation of a port whose first implementation has no users yet. Instead M5 defines `EventPublisher` as a **port** with one in-memory implementation, and runs the worker lane inside the server so that PRD 03's read-through diagram — open → stub → promote → enrich → `title.updated` → patch — closes for real. `usher work` keeps working as a standalone process for an operator who wants one, with the documented consequence that its completions reach no SSE client; a client that refetches still gets the enriched title, which is degradation rather than breakage. The seam is left exactly where a `PostgresNotifyEventBus` would slot in with no service change.

**6. PRD 07's RFC 9457 error envelope stays deferred to M9, and SSE does not force it.** The reasoning is in "Does a streaming surface force the error envelope?" below, because it is a question this milestone was specifically asked and it deserves more than a line.

**What M5 delivers that M6 depends on:** an `EventPublisher` port and a live bus, so M6's index stage can publish `title.updated` when a search document is rebuilt without inventing a channel; a `TitleReadService` that already answers from local state and already promotes, so a search result that lands on a stub behaves identically to a direct open; and a supervised-lane shape in `create_app`'s lifespan that M6's embedding worker is a third instance of.

---

## Does a streaming surface force the error envelope?

PRD 07 specifies RFC 9457 problem details with a machine-readable `code`, and M3 recorded explicitly that it ships FastAPI's defaults instead — `{"detail": "source not found"}` for a 404, `{"detail": [ … ]}` for a 422 with `input` stripped — because "defining a `code` vocabulary against four admin routes would be guessing at it a milestone early". M5 adds a streaming surface. Does that change the answer?

**No, and the reason is precise: RFC 9457 is a format for a response *body*, and a stream's failure vocabulary is a format for an *event*.** Once `GET /events` has answered `200 text/event-stream`, there is no further status code to carry a problem document in; every subsequent failure is either an SSE event or a closed connection. PRD 07 already specifies the one in-stream failure it cares about, and it is not a problem document — **"On buffer overflow the server emits `resync_required` rather than silently skipping events"**. M5 implements exactly that vocabulary, and adds one more member to it for the same reason (a `Last-Event-ID` the server can no longer honour). Those are pinned as a `StrEnum` on the wire (`api/dto/events.py`), which is the SSE analogue of the envelope: a client contract, versioned, with no member that nothing emits.

The two failures on M5's routes that *are* ordinary HTTP responses are both shapes M3 already ships:

| Failure | M5's answer | Why it is not a problem document |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /titles/{id}` for an id that does not exist | `404 {"detail": "title not found"}` | Identical in kind to `GET /admin/sources/{id}/status`'s 404, which M3 shipped in this shape deliberately. |
| `GET /events?titles=not-a-uuid` | `422 {"detail": "titles must be a comma-separated list of uuids"}` | A malformed query parameter. `usher.api.errors` already strips `input` app-wide. |

And the failure that would force the question does not occur in M5. PRD 07's own worked example of the envelope is `503 source_unavailable` on `POST /titles/{id}/play` — a request the client made that Usher genuinely could not answer. M5 has no such request, **by design rather than by luck**: PRD 08's rule is "a degraded subsystem narrows functionality; it never fails a request local state can answer", and `GET /titles/{id}` is answerable entirely from `titles`, `media_items` and `watch_states`. It never calls a `SourceAdapter`, so `PortUnavailable` is not reachable from it, so there is no 503 to give a `code` to. `usher.ports.errors` already draws that line for us — `PortUnavailable` is documented as distinct from "the requested thing does not exist", and `get_item` returns `None` for absence and never raises it.

**So the envelope is deferred, and the thing that will force it is named:** the first route whose honest answer is "the source is down and I cannot serve this from local state". That is `POST /titles/{id}/play`, in M9, alongside the playback ticket ADR-0012 owes. Defining a `code` vocabulary now, against two routes neither of which can produce a domain-level failure, would be guessing at it exactly as M3 said.

**One thing M5 does that is a down payment on it, and is worth doing anyway:** `GET /titles/{id}` carries `enrichment_state` and `enrichment_error` on every response, which PRD 07 requires. A title whose enrichment job is parked (PRD 08: re-enqueueing does not un-park, and demand promotion must not promote parked poison behind a human's back) therefore renders its failure to the client as a field rather than as a status code — which is the same "narrow, don't fail" principle the envelope will express for a different class of failure.

---

## Facts this plan builds on, and does not re-derive

1. **Emby push works, verified end to end 2026-07-29** against this deployment with a normal non-admin token and a stable `DeviceId`: 101 on the handshake, `Sessions` periodically, `UserDataChanged` within seconds of an out-of-band played/unplayed toggle. Two earlier negative findings were both wrong. ADR-0004.
2. **A successful upgrade is not a health signal**, because a handshake against a nonexistent path also upgrades and also receives `Sessions`. The section above is built entirely on this.
3. **The socket URL carries the token and the device id**: `/embywebsocket?api_key=<token>&deviceId=<id>` (PRD 03, ADR-0004's decompilation of `SessionWebSocketListener`). No role check exists in the subscription path, so a normal user token suffices.
4. **A token presented with a different `DeviceId` neither forks nor invalidates its session.** Emby binds a session to the token's own authentication record made at `AuthenticateByName` time; the header's `DeviceId` on later requests does not register a device. So "one durable device" comes from authenticating once with a stable id, and the push channel must take its token from the existing `EmbySession` rather than authenticating independently.
5. **`EmbySession` already implements** durable-client auth, single-flight re-authentication on 401 with a generation counter, negative caching against an injected clock, and exactly one retry per call. `access_token()` is its public accessor and is already the one ADR-0012 documents for building a URL that carries a credential.
6. **A listing's `UserData` is not an item's** (ADR-0014, verified 2026-07-31): a listing reports `PlayCount: 0` and omits `LastPlayedDate` for the very item whose single-item route reports `PlayCount: 13` and a real date. `SourceWatchState.play_count`/`last_played_at` are `int | None`/`AwareDatetime | None`, `None` means "this read could not determine it", and `merge_from_source` is `COALESCE`-shaped. **A `UserDataChanged` push message is a *third* payload shape and nothing in this repository has ever parsed one**, so M5 treats its play history as untrustworthy for the same reason the listing's is, and lets M4's `WATCH_HISTORY` backfill recover it.
7. **M4's `WatchStateSyncService` owns the inbound merge and the bounded backfill**, including the `_watch_target` collapse (an episode's `MediaItem` carries both ids; `watch_states` permits exactly one, and the episode wins) and the `played AND play_count IS None` → `WATCH_HISTORY` enqueue. M5 feeds it rather than duplicating it.
8. **A history backfill must carry its own fresh `observed_at`**, because `trg_watch_states_set_updated_at` stamps the write instant and PRD 03's "latest `updated_at` wins" covers the whole record. The same applies to a push merge: `observed_at` is the instant the event was received, never a run's start instant.
9. **`enqueue` promotes and never demotes**, by `ON CONFLICT (kind, key) DO UPDATE SET priority = GREATEST(…) WHERE jobs.status <> 'parked' AND jobs.priority < excluded.priority`. M4's final mutation sweep established that the `WHERE` clause is what carries the no-demotion property and the `GREATEST` is belt-and-braces. **This is the clause M5's demand promotion is the first caller of**, and re-requesting an already-promoted title writes zero rows, which is the honest number.
10. **`JobPriority` is an `IntEnum` with `DEMAND = 100`**, defined in M4 and called by nothing. `JobPriority.VISIBLE = 80` stays uncalled — it is M7's, when a row is composed.
11. **`ensure_default_user` is a request-scoped dependency** (`usher.api.deps.get_default_user_id`), deliberately not a lifespan call, because a startup write turns a database outage into a crash loop. M5 adds the first routes that read and write `watch_states`, which is why that was settled in M4.
12. **`get_session` is the request's unit-of-work boundary, and a route-driven integration test therefore commits for real.** Leaving stubbed `titles` and enqueued `jobs` behind took down four tests in three other files, each of which passed in isolation. `media_items` and `sync_runs` cascade from the source; `titles` and `jobs` do not.
13. **`now()` is frozen per transaction; `clock_timestamp()` is the statement's instant.** The integration suite's per-test fixture is one long transaction, so "the second write is later than the first" is unobservable there without a raw backdating `INSERT`.
14. **An observable OTel callback cannot query this database** — it runs on the metric reader's background thread and every database call here is a coroutine on asyncpg. `register_queue_gauges` takes a *synchronous* reader over the caller's most recent complete re-read. **The SDK also keeps only the first observable gauge registered under a name and silently discards the rest**, so the reader is a module global that is replaced rather than a closure captured at instrument-creation time. M5's two new gauges follow the same shape, and one of them (`usher.sse.connections`) is a plain in-memory integer, which is the one case where the reader really can be live.
15. **`SQLAlchemyInstrumentor` produced no statement spans for three milestones**, because `instrument()` patches a module attribute that `db/base.py` had already bound at import time. A test that accepts a `connect` span is not enough; assert on a *statement* span.
16. **A concurrency test must assert on observed overlap, not on a count.** `SlowTransport` (`tests/fakes/slow_transport.py`) exists because a bare `MockTransport` never truly awaits, so the event loop runs each gathered task through its whole cycle before starting the next — and a deleted single-flight lock passed five runs in a row. M5's own concurrency claims (a slow SSE subscriber does not block a publisher; a gap-closing delta runs while the socket buffers) are asserted the same way, on measured intervals.
17. **`websockets` 16.1.1 is already resolved in `uv.lock`**, transitively through `uvicorn[standard]`. M5 declares it directly in `[project.dependencies]` rather than relying on that — a transitive dependency is not a contract, and `uvicorn`'s extras are free to change.
18. **`websockets`' client defaults are `ping_interval=20, ping_timeout=20`**, which is exactly PRD 03's "heartbeat every 20–25 s, because Emby sends no keepalive of its own; nginx closes idle connections at 60 s and Cloudflare at ~100 s". The requirement is met by the library default and is not re-implemented — it is asserted, once, so a future default change is visible.
19. **`websockets` logs the request line, including the query string, at DEBUG.** `websockets/client.py:294` is `self.logger.debug("> GET %s HTTP/1.1", request.path)`, and `request.path` for this channel is `/embywebsocket?api_key=<token>&deviceId=<id>`. `usher.telemetry.configure_logging` forces `propagate = True` on **every** existing logger and installs an `_InterceptHandler` on root at level 0, so at `USHER_LOG_LEVEL=DEBUG` that line reaches stdout as a structured JSON log with the token in it. **This is a real credential leak that M5 would introduce**, found by reading the library rather than predicted; Task 8 reproduces it with a failing test and closes it.
20. **`websockets.exceptions.InvalidURI.__str__` contains the URI.** `EmbySession` interpolates `{exc}` into its own error messages and explains why that is safe there (httpx exceptions carry a method and a URL, and Usher's own outbound URLs carry no token — the session rides in the `X-Emby-Token` header). It is **not** safe here, and the difference is the kind of thing a later reader would "unify".
21. **`SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` is the whole of the queue's exclusion**, and both wrong spellings hang rather than answer. Any new concurrency case bounds its claims with `asyncio.wait_for`; `pytest-timeout` is deliberately not a dependency.
22. **The catalog holds 1,271,138 titles and the one measured source holds 1,126,789 items** (94,448 movies / 32,414 series / 999,927 episodes, re-measured 2026-07-31; it moves, so every derived figure is a dated snapshot). A push lane that made a request per item, or a database round trip per item, on any path a library-wide event can reach is a design defect rather than a performance note. This is the reason `push_max_items_per_event` exists.
23. **`list_unmatched`'s `OFFSET` is linear per page and quadratic to drain** — 43.7 ms at offset 0, 388.9 ms at offset 1,126,574. Nothing M5 adds may page by offset.
24. **Verified against this checkout while writing this plan:** `uv run pytest` → **1744 passed, 1 skipped** (1320 unit / 425 integration); `uv run mypy src tests` → clean, strict; `uv run ruff check --no-cache . && uv run ruff format --check .` → clean; `uv run lint-imports` → `Contracts: 6 kept, 0 broken`. Head migration is `f1a7d3c9e824`. `SourceAdapterContract` holds **43** cases and `tests/unit/test_adapters_emby_contract.py` asserts that number. `websockets.__version__` is `16.1.1`, and `websockets.asyncio.client.connect` accepts `additional_headers`, `ping_interval`, `ping_timeout`, `max_queue`, `open_timeout`, `proxy` and `logger`. **Two things the same pass found that are already broken**, both fixed in Task 29: `README.md` links to `docs/prd/decisions/0015-availability-is-swept-not-diffed.md`, which was renamed during M4; and the internal-link check `.claude/rules/prd-maintenance.md` prescribes over all of `docs/` has **never** printed `OK` — the M2, M3 and M4 plans each embed PRD and ADR snippets whose links are relative to `docs/prd/`, so every one of them reports as broken from `docs/plans/`. M4's final gate records `Expected: … OK` against exactly that command.
25. **M5 needs no migration.** `sources.supports_push` exists (M1), `ix_media_items_title_id` exists (M4), the `jobs` promotion clause exists (M4), and nothing M5 adds is persisted — the SSE replay ring and the push health ledger are in-memory by design. That is worth stating rather than discovering: if a task in this plan finds itself wanting a table, that is a signal to re-read this line before writing one.

---

## Four traps that silently produce a green suite against wrong code

All four have bitten this project. All four are cheap to disarm and expensive to discover.

**Stale `.pyc`.** A mutation that deletes a constraint and re-runs the suite reads a cached bytecode file and reports the *old* behaviour. Before every mutation sweep:

```bash
find /home/anirudhlath/code/usher/src /home/anirudhlath/code/usher/tests -name __pycache__ -type d -exec rm -rf {} +
export PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1
```

**The venv shebang trap.** `cp -r` of the repo to `/tmp` copies `.venv/bin/*`, whose shebangs are absolute paths back into the *original* venv. A mutation applied in the copy is never imported: the interpreter that runs is the original checkout's, `sys.path` points at the original `src/`, and the suite runs green while proving nothing. Mutate **in place**, revert with `cp` backups rather than `git checkout --` (which clobbers uncommitted work in the same file). **If every mutation in a sweep survives, that is this trap, not a finding.**

**A harness must refuse to classify a run that did not run.** This host's shell does not word-split an unquoted `$VAR` holding two test paths: the selection reaches pytest as one bogus path, nothing runs, the exit code is non-zero, and a naive harness records the mutation as caught having measured nothing. Three were, in M4, before the harness started requiring that a run actually ran. A mutation whose run collected zero tests is `DID-NOT-RUN`, never `KILLED`.

**A test that never truly awaits is not a concurrency test.** A bare mock never suspends, so the event loop runs each task through its whole cycle before starting the next, and a count that a serialised run would also produce is not evidence. M5's async surface is larger than any previous milestone's — a long-lived socket, a supervised reconnect loop, a fan-out bus, and a streaming response — so every concurrency claim here is asserted on **measured, overlapping wall-clock intervals**, using the shape `JobQueueContract`'s `overlapping()` established and `SlowTransport.max_in_flight` instruments. A test asserting "the publisher finished and the slow subscriber got `resync_required`" is satisfied by a serialised run; a test asserting "the publisher's interval sits inside the interval during which the slow subscriber was parked" is not.

---

## What the new contract cases rule out

Each case names the wrong implementation it fails, because a contract suite that passes against a deliberately-wrong implementation is hollow.

### `SourceAdapterContract` — six new cases (43 → 49)

| Contract case | The wrong implementation it fails |
|---|---|
| `test_events_yields_what_the_source_pushed` | An adapter whose `events()` yields nothing, or whose context manager exits immediately. Seeds a watch-state change through the harness and asserts a `WATCH_STATE_CHANGED` event naming that `external_id` arrives. |
| `test_supports_push_is_false_until_a_message_arrives` | **The milestone's central rule.** An adapter that reports `supports_push = True` on a successful connection. Opens the channel, asserts `supports_push` is `False` *before* the harness pushes anything, then `True` after one message. |
| `test_supports_push_goes_false_when_the_channel_stops_delivering` | An adapter whose health never decays, so a stalled socket reads healthy forever. The harness stalls the channel and advances the injected clock past `stale_after`. |
| `test_a_stalled_channel_raises_rather_than_hanging` | The same failure one layer over: an iterator that simply blocks. Asserts `PortUnavailable` escapes the `async for`, bounded by `asyncio.wait_for` so a hanging implementation fails the case instead of the suite. |
| `test_a_dropped_channel_raises_rather_than_ending_quietly` | An iterator that stops yielding when the connection closes. Indistinguishable from "the source has nothing more to say", and the supervisor would treat a dead socket as a clean shutdown and never reconnect. Same guarantee `list_items` already carries. |
| `test_events_raises_source_not_supported_when_push_is_unavailable` | An adapter that advertises push it does not have, which makes the reconciler skip a source it is the only cover for. Asserts the port's own "must agree with `supports_push`" both ways. |

### `MediaItemRepositoryContract` — one new case

| Contract case | The wrong implementation it fails |
|---|---|
| `test_list_for_title_returns_every_copy_across_sources` | A read keyed on `(source_id, title_id)`, which makes a two-source household's detail screen show one badge. Also asserts unavailable copies are returned with `available = false` rather than filtered out — PRD 02's "soft-delete availability", and a client that renders "not on any source" for a film on a temporarily-unmounted drive is showing a different fact than the one stored. |

### `EventPublisherContract` — the SSE channel's own suite

New file, `tests/contract/event_publisher_contract.py`, run against `FakeEventPublisher` (recording, no fan-out) and `InMemoryEventBus`. The port has one method, so most of the suite is about the bus; the shared part is what a *publisher* must guarantee to a service that calls it.

| Contract case | The wrong implementation it fails |
|---|---|
| `test_publish_never_raises_for_a_subscriber_that_cannot_keep_up` | A publisher that propagates a subscriber's failure into the service that published. `EnrichService` finishing a title must not fail because a browser tab stopped reading. |
| `test_publish_does_not_block_on_a_subscriber_that_is_not_reading` | The one that matters, and the one a serialised test cannot see. Asserted on measured intervals: the publish call's window must sit inside the window during which a subscriber is parked mid-`__anext__`. |
| `test_a_subscriber_that_overflows_is_told_to_resync` | Silently dropping events, which leaves a client stale with no way to know. PRD 07: "the server emits `resync_required` rather than silently skipping events". |
| `test_a_subscriber_scoped_to_titles_is_not_woken_by_unrelated_churn` | A bus that fans everything to everyone, so a detail screen re-renders on every one of a nightly walk's 1,127 `sync.progress` events. PRD 07's `?titles=` scoping. |
| `test_replay_resumes_after_the_last_event_the_client_saw` | A reconnect that starts from "now", losing everything published during the gap — which is the whole reason `Last-Event-ID` exists. |
| `test_a_last_event_id_older_than_the_buffer_is_told_to_resync` | Replaying whatever happens to still be in the ring and calling it a resume, so the client silently misses the events that fell off the front. |
| `test_a_last_event_id_from_a_previous_process_is_told_to_resync` | An in-memory id space that restarts at 1 on every process start, so a reconnecting client's `Last-Event-ID: 40` replays events 41+ *of a different sequence*. The epoch token is what makes this detectable. |

## Where each new fake is more forgiving than the real thing

Every fake M5 adds carries this statement in its own module docstring, because M3's live run found 40 contract assertions passing against a write-back that had never once worked — the fake faithfully implemented the adapter's own guess.

| Fake | Where it is more forgiving than the real thing | What closes the gap |
|---|---|---|
| `FakePushConnection` | **No handshake at all.** It never negotiates an upgrade, so a wrong path, a missing `Upgrade` header, a proxy that answers 404, and a `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` mismatch are all invisible. It never fragments a message, never sends a binary frame, never applies backpressure at `max_queue`, and its `aclose()` cannot fail. Its `recv(timeout)` raises `TimeoutError` on demand, where the real one raises it only after real wall time. | `tests/integration/test_push_loopback.py` drives the **real** `websockets` client against a **real** `websockets` server on `127.0.0.1`, so the handshake, the ping/pong keepalive, a server-side abrupt close, and a close code are all real. The residual gap is Emby's own listener behaviour, and only Task 31's live run closes that. |
| `FakeEmbyServer`'s push messages | It renders `UserDataChanged`/`LibraryChanged`/`Sessions` **from the shape M5 wrote into it**, which is the M3 failure exactly: nothing here knows what Emby really sends. In particular it renders a `UserDataList` entry with `PlayCount` present, and the adapter deliberately ignores it — so a wrong belief about that field is invisible from both sides. | Two things, neither of which is the contract suite. `tests/unit/test_adapters_emby_push.py` parses committed message fixtures with no fake server involved, so a wrong field *name* fails there. And Task 31's live run captures real messages and diffs their shape against those fixtures, which is the only thing that can catch a wrong belief about the envelope. |
| `FakeSourceAdapter`'s push channel | Its health ledger is driven by the harness rather than by a transport, so `supports_push` decays only because a test advanced a clock. Nothing about a real socket's failure modes is expressible. | The same six contract cases run against the real `EmbyAdapter` over `EmbyPushChannel` over `FakePushConnection` (`tests/unit/test_adapters_emby_contract.py`), which is at least a real channel with a real watchdog; and the loopback test above, which is a real socket. |
| `FakeEventPublisher` | It records and never fans out, so **every property the bus exists for is untested by it** — no queue, no overflow, no ordering, no replay, no cancellation. It is a spy for services, not a bus. It also never blocks, which means a service tested only against it would pass even if the real bus did block. | `InMemoryEventBus` runs the identical `EventPublisherContract`, and the non-blocking case is asserted on measured intervals rather than on a completion. |
| `InMemoryEventBus` (used as a stand-in for a future cross-process bus) | It is in-process, so ordering is total, delivery is synchronous-in-effect, and a subscriber's view can never be behind a publisher's by more than a queue. A Postgres `LISTEN/NOTIFY` transport would have an 8,000-byte payload ceiling, at-most-once delivery, and no ordering guarantee across connections. | Nothing in M5, and this is stated rather than papered over: `EventPublisher` is a port precisely so that the second implementation brings its own contract run, and the contract is written to be satisfiable by a lossy transport (every case is about *this* subscriber's stream, and `resync_required` is the answer to every gap). |

## Task 1: One redaction rule, for two URLs that carry a token

M3 put a redaction on `StreamTarget.__repr__` and ADR-0012 records why it cuts at the query string rather than matching `api_key=`: the deep-link target hides the whole direct URL, token and all, percent-encoded inside its *own* query, so a parameter-name match sails straight past it. M5 introduces a second URL carrying the same token — the push channel's — and the rule has to be one rule rather than two that can drift.

`_redacted` is private in `usher.ports.source`. Adapters may import `ports`, so making it public is the whole change. It stays in `ports/source.py` rather than moving to a utility module, because it is a property of this port's DTOs and the second caller is a `SourceAdapter` implementation.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/usher/ports/source.py`
- Modify: `tests/unit/test_ports_source.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```python
# tests/unit/test_ports_source.py  (append)


def test_redact_query_is_public_and_cuts_at_the_query() -> None:
    """M5's push channel imports this to keep a socket URL out of every log
    line and error message it builds. A private `_redacted` would be
    imported anyway, or -- worse -- reimplemented slightly differently in
    `adapters/emby/push.py`, which is how one rule becomes two that
    disagree. ADR-0012 is explicit that the rule is "cut at the query",
    never "match on `api_key=`", because the deep-link target percent-
    encodes the whole direct URL inside its own query string.
    """
    assert redact_query("https://emby.invalid/embywebsocket?api_key=abc&deviceId=d") == (
        "https://emby.invalid/embywebsocket<redacted>"
    )
    assert redact_query("wss://emby.invalid/embywebsocket") == "wss://emby.invalid/embywebsocket"
    assert redact_query("https://emby.invalid/x#api_key=abc") == "https://emby.invalid/x<redacted>"


def test_a_stream_target_still_redacts_through_the_shared_rule() -> None:
    """The regression this refactor could introduce: `StreamTarget.__repr__`
    stops calling the helper and starts rendering the raw URL. ADR-0012's
    own evidence section records that with the dataclass-generated `repr`
    the token appears in plain text in `repr()`, `str()`, an f-string,
    `"%s" %`, `pprint.pformat`, and loguru's `diagnose=True` renderer.
    """
    target = StreamTarget(
        kind=StreamTargetKind.DIRECT,
        url="https://e.invalid/v?api_key=tok",
    )
    assert "tok" not in repr(target)
    assert "<redacted>" in repr(target)
```

Add `redact_query` to that file's imports from `usher.ports.source`.

- [ ] **Step 2: Run the test to verify it fails**

```bash
cd /home/anirudhlath/code/usher && uv run pytest tests/unit/test_ports_source.py -q
```

Expected: a collection error — `ImportError: cannot import name 'redact_query' from 'usher.ports.source'`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Make the rule public**

In `src/usher/ports/source.py`, rename `_redacted` to `redact_query`, keep the docstring verbatim (it is the argument, not a description), and update the one call site in `StreamTarget.__repr__`:

```python
def redact_query(url: str) -> str:
    """A URL cut at its query string, for rendering in a `repr` or a log line.

    Everything from the first `?` or `#` onward, gone. Not a search for
    `api_key=`: the deep-link target hides the whole direct URL, token and
    all, percent-encoded inside its *own* query string, so a redaction that
    matched on a parameter name would sail straight past it. Cutting at the
    query covers both, and covers whatever a second source spells its token
    parameter -- which is the point, since `StreamTarget` is the port's DTO
    and Jellyfin's `ApiKey` has exactly the same problem as Emby's
    `api_key`.

    Enough is kept (scheme, host, path) to identify the item in a log line;
    the query carries no fact a reader needs that the target's own typed
    fields do not already state.

    **Public, and it has two callers.** `StreamTarget.__repr__` below, and
    `usher.adapters.emby.push`, whose socket URL is
    `/embywebsocket?api_key=<token>&deviceId=<id>` -- the same token, in the
    same shape, one milestone later. One rule rather than two that can
    disagree; ADR-0012 records why it is spelled this way.
    """
    cut = min((index for index in (url.find("?"), url.find("#")) if index != -1), default=-1)
    return url if cut < 0 else f"{url[:cut]}<redacted>"
```

```python
        rendered = {item.name: getattr(self, item.name) for item in fields(self)}
        rendered["url"] = redact_query(self.url)
```

## Task 2: `SourceEvent` carries what the source already told us

PRD 03 and `usher.ports.source.SourceEvent` both carry a 🔶 that names this milestone:

> `SourceEvent`, the push channel's own DTO, carries no payload beyond `kind` and the affected `external_ids`. That forces a `WATCH_STATE_CHANGED` event to re-walk `watch_state(since=...)` to discover what actually changed, even though Emby's own `UserDataChanged` message already carries the position and played flag. Settle in **M5**, when the push lane is built and the cost of re-walking is measurable against just carrying the payload through.

**The measurement the marker asks for is not needed, because the two candidates are not close.** `watch_state(since=…)` is a *paged listing walk* under `MinDateLastSavedForUser`; over a 30-day window that filter returns 29,027 items, and there is no narrower ask — `since` is the only parameter the port offers. A single `UserDataChanged` (a household pausing a film emits one every few seconds of playback) would therefore cost a walk of everything changed since the cursor, in a lane PRD 01 budgets at one connection per source. The alternative that is *not* the payload — `get_watch_state(external_id)` per affected id — is one request and is authoritative, and it is what the fallback path below does.

**The decision: `SourceEvent` gains an optional `watch_states` payload, and the lane does not trust its play history.**

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SourceEvent:
    kind: SourceEventKind
    external_ids: tuple[str, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)
    watch_states: tuple[SourceWatchState, ...] = ()
```

Three things make that shape the right one rather than the obvious one.

**It is plural and keyed by `external_id`, not positional.** Emby's `UserDataChanged` carries a `UserDataList` array and `LibraryChanged` carries `ItemsAdded`/`ItemsUpdated`/`ItemsRemoved` arrays, so `external_ids` was already plural. Aligning the two lists by position would make a single unparseable entry shift every later state onto the wrong item. `external_ids` stays the authoritative list of affected ids; `watch_states` is a subset an adapter *was able to* parse, and an id present in the first and absent from the second takes the `get_watch_state` fallback.

**Its `play_count` and `last_played_at` are `None`, always, on the Emby path.** A `UserDataChanged` message is a **third** payload shape and nothing in this repository has ever parsed one. ADR-0014's rule is that a reported number must be true and presence is never required, so the honest answer for a shape nobody has measured is absence. That costs nothing, because it feeds the identical chain M4 already built: `WatchStateSyncService` sees `played and play_count is None` and enqueues a `WATCH_HISTORY` job at `BACKFILL` priority, which asks the single-item route. **Whether `UserDataChanged` carries a trustworthy `PlayCount` is an explicit question for Task 31's live run**, and a positive answer is a follow-up that removes one background job per played item — not something to assume now, and not something to discover by writing a zero over real history.

**It does not carry an item payload.** An `ITEM_ADDED` event could in principle carry the whole `BaseItemDto`, and Emby's `LibraryChanged` does not send one — it sends ids. So the item path is `get_item(external_id)` per id, capped, exactly as the fallback watch path is.

**What this costs, stated plainly.** `SourceEvent` grows a field that only one of four `SourceEventKind` members ever populates, which is a small asymmetry on a small DTO. The alternative — a per-kind event hierarchy — buys type-level exhaustiveness for a channel whose consumer is a single `match` statement, and costs every adapter a class per kind.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/usher/ports/source.py`
- Modify: `tests/unit/test_ports_source.py`
- Modify: `docs/prd/03-sources-and-sync.md`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```python
# tests/unit/test_ports_source.py  (append)


def test_a_source_event_may_carry_the_states_it_already_knows() -> None:
    """The 🔶 this milestone was left to settle. A `WATCH_STATE_CHANGED`
    event that carried only ids forces the lane to re-walk
    `watch_state(since=...)`, which is a paged listing walk measured at
    29,027 items over a 30-day window -- per event, on a lane budgeted at
    one connection per source.
    """
    state = SourceWatchState(external_id="i1", position_seconds=61, played=False)
    event = SourceEvent(
        kind=SourceEventKind.WATCH_STATE_CHANGED,
        external_ids=("i1", "i2"),
        watch_states=(state,),
    )
    assert event.external_ids == ("i1", "i2")
    assert event.watch_states == (state,)


def test_a_source_event_still_defaults_to_carrying_nothing() -> None:
    """An adapter whose upstream sends only ids must still be able to build
    one, and the item kinds never carry a state at all."""
    event = SourceEvent(kind=SourceEventKind.ITEM_ADDED, external_ids=("i1",))
    assert event.watch_states == ()


def test_a_carried_state_is_keyed_by_external_id_not_by_position() -> None:
    """`external_ids` is the authoritative list and `watch_states` is the
    subset the adapter could parse. Aligning them by position would make one
    unparseable entry shift every later state onto the wrong item -- which
    on this channel means writing one household member's resume position
    onto a different film.
    """
    event = SourceEvent(
        kind=SourceEventKind.WATCH_STATE_CHANGED,
        external_ids=("a", "b", "c"),
        watch_states=(SourceWatchState(external_id="c", position_seconds=5, played=False),),
    )
    by_id = {state.external_id: state for state in event.watch_states}
    assert set(by_id) == {"c"}
    assert [i for i in event.external_ids if i not in by_id] == ["a", "b"]
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Change the port, and delete the 🔶**

```python
# src/usher/ports/source.py
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SourceEvent:
    """One thing a source's push channel said changed.

    **Settled in M5** — this DTO used to carry a 🔶 asking whether a
    `WATCH_STATE_CHANGED` event should re-walk `watch_state(since=...)` to
    discover what changed, or carry the payload the upstream already sent.
    It carries the payload, and the two candidates were never close:
    `watch_state(since=...)` is a paged listing walk whose only knob is the
    cursor, measured at 29,027 items over a 30-day `MinDateLastSavedForUser`
    window against the one real deployment. Per event, on a lane PRD 01
    budgets at one connection per source.

    `external_ids` is the authoritative list of affected items.
    `watch_states` is the subset the adapter was able to parse out of the
    upstream's own message, **keyed by `external_id` rather than aligned by
    position** -- an id in the first and not the second is a state the
    caller must fetch with `get_watch_state`, and aligning by position would
    let one unparseable entry write every later state onto the wrong item.

    **A carried state's `play_count`/`last_played_at` obey ADR-0014 exactly
    as a walk's do.** An adapter reports a number only if it is true. On
    Emby they are `None`: a `UserDataChanged` message is a third payload
    shape (a listing is one, an item route is another) and no run in this
    repository has ever parsed one, so absence is the honest answer and the
    `WATCH_HISTORY` backfill recovers the pair from the single-item route.

    The item kinds carry no payload at all -- Emby's `LibraryChanged` sends
    ids, not items -- so `ITEM_ADDED`/`ITEM_UPDATED` are resolved with
    `get_item` per id, bounded by the caller.
    """

    kind: SourceEventKind
    external_ids: tuple[str, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)
    watch_states: tuple[SourceWatchState, ...] = ()
```

`SourceWatchState` is declared above `SourceEvent` in that module already, so no reordering is needed.

## Task 3: `SourceStatus` learns whether the account is an administrator

[ADR-0012](../prd/decisions/0012-playback-urls-carry-a-source-token.md)'s "Recommended, not implemented" section names two cheap answers to accepted risks. One shipped in M3 (stop sending `DeviceId` in a playback URL). The other did not:

> **Detect an administrator account and say so.** `_authenticate_locked` already parses the `User` object out of `AuthenticateByName`'s response for `User.Id`; Emby's `UserDto` carries `Policy.IsAdministrator` alongside it. Reading it would let Usher warn at source registration, or surface it on `SourceStatus`, turning PRD 03's "no admin privileges are required" from a permission into something observable.
>
> **The readability half is now verified** (2026-07-31): `GET /Users/{userId}` answers 200 to the user's own non-admin token and carries a 45-key `Policy` object with `IsAdministrator` on it — `false` for the account used, which is the configuration this ADR assumes and nothing enforces. `GET /Users/Me` answers **500** on this build and is not a usable shortcut.

**M5 is where that stops being optional, because M5 opens a second channel with the same token.** Until now the token reached exactly one place a third party could hold it: a direct-play URL. From M5 it is also the credential a long-lived socket is opened with, held in memory for the life of the lane and rebuilt on every reconnect. The blast radius of the risk ADR-0012 accepted has not changed in *kind* — the token was always the whole grant — but the number of places it is materialised has, and the mitigation ADR-0012 records is "the operator guidance in PRD 03, not code". One request makes it code.

`GET /Users/{userId}` costs one extra request inside `verify()`, which is called by `GET /admin/sources/{id}/status` and by nothing on a hot path. A failure to read it is **not** a failure of `verify()` — `is_administrator` stays `None` ("not determined"), exactly as `push_available` does, because a status screen must render what it knows rather than 500 on what it does not.

- Modify: `src/usher/adapters/emby/adapter.py`
- Modify: `src/usher/api/dto/source.py`
- Modify: `tests/fakes/emby_server.py`
- Modify: `tests/unit/test_ports_source.py`, `tests/unit/test_adapters_emby_adapter.py`, `tests/unit/test_api_dto.py`

    500 on the measured build, and a future build could do the same for
    `GET /Users/{id}` -- so a failure here narrows the answer rather than
    failing the request, exactly as every other branch of `verify()` does.
    """
    server.user_route_fails = True
    status = await adapter.verify()
    assert status.reachable is True
    assert status.authenticated is True
    assert status.is_administrator is None

`__post_init__` is unchanged: an administrator account is a warning, not an invariant violation, and refusing to construct a status for one would make the screen that exists to report it unable to.

```python
# src/usher/adapters/emby/adapter.py
USER_PATH = "/Users"


async def _is_administrator(self, user_id: str) -> bool | None:
    """`Policy.IsAdministrator` for the authenticated account, or `None`.

    `GET /Users/{userId}` answers 200 to the user's own non-admin token and
    carries a 45-key `Policy` object -- verified 2026-07-31 against Emby
    4.9.5.0. `GET /Users/Me` answers **500** on that build and is not a
    usable shortcut, so the id is interpolated.

    Never raises. This is one extra request on a status screen, and a
    failure to read a role is not a failure to reach or authenticate
    against a source -- reporting it as one would take `GET /admin/sources/
    {id}/status` from "renders every state a source can be in" to "500s on
    a build that spells this route differently".
    """
    try:
        body = await self._session.json_body(
            "GET", f"{USER_PATH}/{_segment(user_id)}", op="verify_policy"
        )
    except UsherPortError:
        return None
    policy = body.get("Policy")
    if not isinstance(policy, Mapping):
        return None
    value = policy.get("IsAdministrator")
    return bool(value) if isinstance(value, bool) else None
```

## Task 4: The health ledger, and a connection this project can substitute

Two small pieces before the channel exists, because both are the milestone's argument and neither is worth burying inside it.

**`PushHealth` is the answer to "is push working", and it counts messages.** Nothing else in this milestone is allowed to answer that question, and the way to make that structural is for the only answer-shaped method to be one that cannot be satisfied by an open socket.

**`PushConnection` is an ABC, not a structural type.** ADR-0001 puts every *port* on `abc.ABC`; this is not a port, it is an adapter-internal seam, and it is an ABC anyway for a reason specific to this milestone: the real `websockets.ClientConnection` cannot subclass it, so a thin wrapper has to exist — and that wrapper is exactly where the exception translation and the message decoding belong. Typing the seam structurally would let the raw library object through, and with it `websockets.exceptions.InvalidURI`, whose `str()` contains the URI, whose query string contains the token.

**`recv(timeout)` raising `TimeoutError` is a *tick*, not a failure**, and that shape is what makes the watchdog in Task 7 testable without sleeping.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/adapters/emby/push.py`
- Create: `tests/unit/test_adapters_emby_push.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

```python
# tests/unit/test_adapters_emby_push.py
"""The Emby push channel: the ledger, the mapper, the socket, the watchdog."""

import pytest

from usher.adapters.emby.push import PushHealth


def test_a_fresh_ledger_is_not_delivering() -> None:
    assert PushHealth(stale_after=90.0).is_delivering(now=0.0) is False


def test_an_open_connection_that_has_delivered_nothing_is_not_delivering() -> None:
    """**The rule this whole milestone exists for.** ADR-0004: a handshake
    against a *nonexistent path* also upgrades and also receives `Sessions`,
    so a successful upgrade is not evidence of anything. A reverse proxy
    that forwards `Upgrade` and then buffers produces exactly this state --
    connected, and delivering nothing -- and reporting it healthy makes the
    reconciler skip a source it is the only cover for.
    """
    health = PushHealth(stale_after=90.0)
    health.connected = True
    health.opened_at = 100.0
    assert health.is_delivering(now=100.0) is False


def test_one_message_makes_it_deliver() -> None:
    health = PushHealth(stale_after=90.0)
    health.connected = True
    health.opened_at = 100.0
    health.record_message(now=101.0)
    assert health.messages_received == 1
    assert health.is_delivering(now=101.0) is True


def test_delivery_decays_after_the_staleness_window() -> None:
    """A socket that delivered once an hour ago and nothing since is not a
    working push channel. `websockets`' own `ping_timeout` cannot see this:
    a peer that answers pongs while delivering nothing passes the WebSocket
    keepalive and fails here."""
    health = PushHealth(stale_after=90.0)
    health.connected = True
    health.opened_at = 100.0
    health.record_message(now=101.0)
    assert health.is_delivering(now=190.0) is True
    assert health.is_delivering(now=191.1) is False


def test_a_closed_connection_is_not_delivering_however_recently_it_spoke() -> None:
    health = PushHealth(stale_after=90.0)
    health.connected = True
    health.opened_at = 100.0
    health.record_message(now=101.0)
    health.connected = False
    assert health.is_delivering(now=101.0) is False


def test_silence_is_measured_from_the_last_message_or_from_the_open() -> None:
    """The watchdog's input. Before the first message there is nothing to
    measure from but the open, and a channel that never delivers must still
    become measurably silent -- otherwise the one failure mode this
    milestone is built around is the one the watchdog cannot see."""
    health = PushHealth(stale_after=90.0)
    health.opened_at = 100.0
    assert health.silent_for(now=140.0) == pytest.approx(40.0)
    health.record_message(now=130.0)
    assert health.silent_for(now=140.0) == pytest.approx(10.0)


def test_silence_before_a_connection_is_zero_rather_than_undefined() -> None:
    """`silent_for` is called from a loop that only runs while a connection
    is open, so this branch is unreachable in production -- and a `None`
    minus a float is a `TypeError` that would take the lane down rather than
    reconnect it. Pinned directly, the way M4 pins its two other
    unreachable-through-the-contract guards."""
    assert PushHealth(stale_after=90.0).silent_for(now=140.0) == 0.0
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the ledger and the connection seam**

```python
# src/usher/adapters/emby/push.py
"""Emby's WebSocket push channel (PRD 03, ADR-0004, ADR-0018).

**An open socket is not a health signal, and this module is where that is
made structural rather than remembered.** ADR-0004's live verification
recorded that a handshake against a *nonexistent path* also upgrades and
also receives `Sessions`, so Emby's listener holds a socket regardless of
path -- and a reverse proxy that forwards `Upgrade` and then buffers
produces the same state without any help from Emby. In both, the connection
object is fine and nothing is arriving. Every answer this module gives about
push health comes from `PushHealth`, which counts *messages*, and a channel
that stops delivering raises out of its own iterator rather than sitting
there looking well.

Three seams, each with a reason:

1. **`PushConnection` is an ABC**, not a structural type, even though it is
   not a port. The real `websockets.ClientConnection` cannot subclass it, so
   a wrapper has to exist -- and the wrapper is where the exception
   translation lives. That matters more here than anywhere else in this
   package: `websockets.exceptions.InvalidURI.__str__` contains the URI, and
   this channel's URI contains the session token. `EmbySession` interpolates
   `{exc}` into its own messages and explains why that is safe *there*
   (httpx exceptions carry a method and a URL, and Usher's own outbound URLs
   carry no token -- the session rides in the `X-Emby-Token` header). It is
   not safe here, and nothing but the wrapper stands between the two.
2. **`recv(timeout)` raises `TimeoutError` for "nothing arrived yet"**,
   which the caller treats as a *tick* rather than a failure. That is what
   lets the staleness watchdog run on an injected clock instead of on real
   wall time, and it is the difference between a 90-second test and a
   sub-millisecond one.
3. **The URL is built inside `_socket_url` and is never stored, returned,
   logged, or interpolated into an exception.** Every error message this
   module raises names a path, never a URL. ADR-0012's handling rules apply
   to it unchanged, and Task 8's `_socket_logger` closes the one path they
   did not previously cover.
"""

import time
from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass(slots=True)
class PushHealth:
    """What is known about a push channel, from messages rather than from a
    socket.

    Mutable on purpose: it is a ledger the channel writes and the adapter
    reads, and the adapter holds the *same* object across reconnects so
    `reconnects` and `messages_received` are cumulative for the lane rather
    than per connection.

    `stale_after` is a field rather than a parameter of `is_delivering`
    because two callers ask (the adapter's `supports_push`, and the
    channel's own watchdog) and a window they could pass differently is a
    window they eventually would.
    """

    stale_after: float
    connected: bool = False
    opened_at: float | None = None
    last_message_at: float | None = None
    messages_received: int = 0
    events_emitted: int = 0
    reconnects: int = 0

    def record_open(self, *, now: float) -> None:
        self.connected = True
        self.opened_at = now
        # Deliberately **not** reset: `messages_received` and `reconnects`
        # are the lane's history, and a reconnect that zeroed the message
        # count would make `is_delivering` briefly false on a channel that
        # has been working for hours -- which is a lie in the other
        # direction. `last_message_at` is left alone for the same reason;
        # `silent_for` prefers `opened_at` only when it is the later of the
        # two, which a fresh open always is.
        self.last_message_at = None

    def record_close(self) -> None:
        self.connected = False

    def record_message(self, *, now: float) -> None:
        self.messages_received += 1
        self.last_message_at = now

    def record_event(self) -> None:
        self.events_emitted += 1

    def record_reconnect(self) -> None:
        self.reconnects += 1

    def is_delivering(self, *, now: float) -> bool:
        """Whether this channel is a push channel a caller may rely on.

        **All three clauses, and the middle one is the milestone.**
        `connected` alone is the answer this whole design refuses to give:
        it is `True` for a proxy that upgraded and buffers, for a NAT entry
        that has been dropped while both ends still believe otherwise, and
        for ADR-0004's own control handshake against a path that does not
        exist. `messages_received > 0` is what those cannot satisfy.

        The staleness clause is what keeps the answer honest *after* the
        first message: a socket that delivered once an hour ago and nothing
        since is not working, and `websockets`' `ping_interval`/
        `ping_timeout` cannot tell -- a peer answering pongs while
        delivering nothing passes the keepalive and fails this.
        """
        return (
            self.connected
            and self.messages_received > 0
            and self.last_message_at is not None
            and now - self.last_message_at <= self.stale_after
        )

    def silent_for(self, *, now: float) -> float:
        """Seconds since anything last arrived, measured from the open when
        nothing has.

        Zero before a connection exists. That branch is unreachable from the
        loop that calls this (it runs only while a connection is open), and
        it is spelled rather than left to a `None`-minus-`float` `TypeError`
        that would take a lane down instead of reconnecting it.
        """
        since = self.last_message_at if self.last_message_at is not None else self.opened_at
        return 0.0 if since is None else now - since


class PushConnection(ABC):
    """One open push connection, as this adapter needs it.

    Deliberately four methods and no state: everything about *what a message
    means* belongs to `EmbyPushChannel`, and everything about *how bytes
    move* belongs to the implementation. That split is what makes the
    loopback test in `tests/integration/test_push_loopback.py` a test of the
    real transport and the unit tests a test of the real logic.
    """

    @abstractmethod
    async def send(self, message: str) -> None:
        """Send one text frame. Raises `PortUnavailable` on any failure."""

    @abstractmethod
    async def recv(self, timeout: float) -> str:
        """One text frame.

        Raises **`TimeoutError`** when nothing arrived within `timeout` --
        which the caller treats as a tick, not a failure, and uses to run
        its staleness watchdog. Raises `PortUnavailable` when the connection
        is gone, and **never returns to signal that**: a `recv` that ended
        quietly would be indistinguishable from a source with nothing to
        say, which is the same failure `SourceAdapter.list_items` forbids
        one layer up.

        Binary frames are decoded as UTF-8 with `errors="replace"` rather
        than raising. Emby sends text; a frame that is neither is counted as
        a received message (it is evidence the socket is alive) and then
        fails to parse as JSON, which the caller already handles.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    async def aclose(self) -> None:
        """Release the connection. Idempotent, and never raises."""


_clock = time.monotonic
```

## Task 5: Emby's three message types become `SourceEvent`s

Pure functions over a decoded message, tested against committed fixtures with no socket of any kind — the same split `mapping.py` uses, and for the same reason: if the fake connection and this module got a field name wrong in the same way, the contract suite would still pass and this file's tests would not.

Emby's envelope is `{"MessageType": "...", "Data": ...}`. Three types matter, and a fourth behaviour matters as much as any of them:

| `MessageType` | `Data` | Becomes |
|---|---|---|
| `UserDataChanged` | `{"UserId": …, "UserDataList": [{"ItemId": …, "PlaybackPositionTicks": …, "Played": …, …}]}` | one `WATCH_STATE_CHANGED` carrying every id and every state it could parse |
| `LibraryChanged` | `{"ItemsAdded": [...], "ItemsUpdated": [...], "ItemsRemoved": [...], …}` | up to three events, one per non-empty array |
| `Sessions` | a list of session DTOs | **nothing** — but it is still a received message |
| anything else | anything | nothing, and still a received message |

That last row is the one worth stating loudly. `Sessions` is the periodic message ADR-0004 observed, it carries playback state for sessions Usher is not part of, and deriving anything from it would mean tracking play sessions Usher never starts. **Its entire value is that it arrives**, which is what keeps `is_delivering` true on an idle library. A mapper that raised on an unknown message type would turn a new Emby build's new message into a dead lane; one that dropped the message without counting it would turn the same into a lane that reconnects every `stale_after` seconds. Counting happens in the channel (Task 6), on every frame, before the mapper is consulted.

- Create: `tests/fixtures/emby/push_library_changed.json`
- Create: `tests/fixtures/emby/push_sessions.json`
- Modify: `tests/fixtures/emby/README.md`

```markdown
### Push messages (M5)

`push_user_data_changed.json`, `push_library_changed.json` and
`push_sessions.json` are the `/embywebsocket` envelope
(`{"MessageId", "MessageType", "Data"}`) for the three types
`usher.adapters.emby.push` reads. Shape-recorded from Emby's own
`UserItemDataDto`, `LibraryUpdateInfo` and `SessionInfoDto` and from
ADR-0004's decompilation of `SessionWebSocketListener`; every value
invented, every id inside the reserved bands above.

**These three have a weaker provenance than the item fixtures beside them**,
and it is stated rather than implied: an item fixture was diffed field by
field against a live 4.9.5.0 response on 2026-07-31, and these have never
met a real message. `PlayCount` and `LastPlayedDate` appear on
`push_user_data_changed.json` **and the adapter deliberately ignores both**
(ADR-0014: a `UserDataChanged` message is a third payload shape and no run
here has parsed one), so their presence here is a record of what the DTO is
documented to carry rather than a claim about what this server sends.
Capturing real messages and diffing their shape is a named step of M5's live
verification.
```

# tests/unit/test_adapters_emby_push.py  (append)

from tests.fakes.emby_fixtures import load_emby_fixture
from usher.adapters.emby.push import to_source_events
from usher.ports.source import SourceEventKind


def test_a_user_data_changed_message_becomes_one_watch_state_event() -> None:
    events = to_source_events(load_emby_fixture("push_user_data_changed"), source_user_id="u1")
    assert len(events) == 1
    event = events[0]
    assert event.kind is SourceEventKind.WATCH_STATE_CHANGED
    assert event.external_ids == ("90000100", "90000101")
    assert [state.external_id for state in event.watch_states] == ["90000100", "90000101"]


def test_a_carried_state_reports_position_and_played_and_nothing_else() -> None:
    """ADR-0014 on a third payload shape. `PlayCount: 3` and a
    `LastPlayedDate` are both present in the fixture and both ignored: no
    run in this repository has ever parsed a real `UserDataChanged`, and a
    number reported from a shape nobody has measured is exactly the claim
    that rule forbids. The `watch_history` backfill recovers the pair from
    the single-item route, which is the chain M4 already built and measured.
    """
    events = to_source_events(load_emby_fixture("push_user_data_changed"), source_user_id="u1")
    first, second = events[0].watch_states
    assert (first.position_seconds, first.played) == (1840, False)
    assert first.play_count is None
    assert first.last_played_at is None
    assert (second.position_seconds, second.played) == (0, True)
    assert second.play_count is None
    assert second.source_user_id == "u1"


def test_a_library_changed_message_becomes_one_event_per_non_empty_array() -> None:
    events = to_source_events(load_emby_fixture("push_library_changed"), source_user_id="u1")
    by_kind = {event.kind: event.external_ids for event in events}
    assert by_kind == {
        SourceEventKind.ITEM_ADDED: ("90000200", "90000201"),
        SourceEventKind.ITEM_UPDATED: ("90000203",),
        SourceEventKind.ITEM_REMOVED: ("90000202",),
    }
    assert all(event.watch_states == () for event in events)


def test_a_library_changed_message_with_empty_arrays_becomes_nothing() -> None:
    """Emby emits `LibraryChanged` with every array empty during a scan that
    changed nothing. Three events naming no items would each drive a
    `get_item` loop over an empty list and a batch ingest of nothing."""
    assert to_source_events({"MessageType": "LibraryChanged", "Data": {}}, source_user_id="u") == ()


def test_a_sessions_message_becomes_nothing() -> None:
    """**And that is its whole job.** `Sessions` carries playback state for
    sessions Usher is not part of; deriving anything from it would mean
    tracking play sessions Usher never starts. Its value is that it
    *arrives* -- it is what keeps `is_delivering` true on an idle library --
    and the counting happens in the channel, on every frame, before this
    function is consulted."""
    assert to_source_events(load_emby_fixture("push_sessions"), source_user_id="u1") == ()


def test_an_unknown_message_type_becomes_nothing_rather_than_raising() -> None:
    """A new Emby build's new message type must not take a lane down. It is
    still counted as a received message one layer up, so it also must not
    make a healthy socket look silent."""
    assert to_source_events({"MessageType": "ScheduledTaskEnded", "Data": {}}, source_user_id="u") == ()
    assert to_source_events({}, source_user_id="u") == ()
    assert to_source_events({"MessageType": "UserDataChanged"}, source_user_id="u") == ()


def test_a_user_data_entry_with_no_item_id_is_dropped_not_guessed() -> None:
    """An entry the mapper cannot key is an entry it must not merge. The
    alternative -- positional alignment against `external_ids` -- writes one
    household member's resume position onto a different film."""
    message = {
        "MessageType": "UserDataChanged",
        "Data": {"UserDataList": [{"PlaybackPositionTicks": 10}, {"ItemId": "90000300"}]},
    }
    events = to_source_events(message, source_user_id="u1")
    assert events[0].external_ids == ("90000300",)
    assert [state.external_id for state in events[0].watch_states] == ["90000300"]


def test_a_user_data_list_that_is_not_a_list_is_dropped_not_raised() -> None:
    """`PortDataMalformed` from a push mapper would park nothing (there is
    no job) and would take the lane down through `PushSupervisor`'s
    `UsherPortError` arm, so one malformed frame would cost a reconnect and
    a gap-closing delta. Dropping it costs one message."""
    assert to_source_events(
        {"MessageType": "UserDataChanged", "Data": {"UserDataList": "nope"}}, source_user_id="u"
    ) == ()


def test_an_id_that_is_not_a_string_is_dropped() -> None:
    message = {"MessageType": "LibraryChanged", "Data": {"ItemsAdded": ["90000400", 7, None, ""]}}
    events = to_source_events(message, source_user_id="u")
    assert events[0].external_ids == ("90000400",)
