```python
# tests/contract/source_adapter_contract.py  (a new "# --- push ---" section,
# between "# --- watch state ---" and "# --- status ---")

    # --- push ---

    async def test_events_yields_what_the_source_pushed(self, harness: SourceHarness) -> None:
        """PRD 03's fast path, at its narrowest: something changed on the
        source and the channel said so, naming the item."""
        await harness.given_item(MOVIE, changed_at=T0)
        await harness.given_watch_state(
            SourceWatchState(external_id="movie-1", position_seconds=91, played=False)
        )
        async with harness.adapter.events() as events:
            await harness.push_event(
                SourceEvent(
                    kind=SourceEventKind.WATCH_STATE_CHANGED, external_ids=("movie-1",)
                )
            )
            event = await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(events)), timeout=2.0)
        assert event.kind is SourceEventKind.WATCH_STATE_CHANGED
        assert event.external_ids == ("movie-1",)
        carried = {state.external_id: state for state in event.watch_states}
        if carried:
            # An adapter is permitted to carry the payload its upstream sent
            # and permitted not to; what it may not do is report a *wrong*
            # position. Same shape as the walk's play-history assertion.
            assert carried["movie-1"].position_seconds == 91

    async def test_supports_push_is_false_until_a_message_arrives(
        self, harness: SourceHarness
    ) -> None:
        """**The rule this milestone exists for**, stated where every future
        adapter has to satisfy it. ADR-0004: a WebSocket handshake against a
        *nonexistent path* also upgrades and also receives `Sessions`, so an
        open connection is not evidence of anything -- and PRD 03's
        reconciler skips a source whose adapter says `True` here.
        """
        assert harness.adapter.supports_push is False
        async with harness.adapter.events() as events:
            assert harness.adapter.supports_push is False, (
                "the channel is open and nothing has arrived; an adapter that "
                "reports push available here is reporting a socket, not a channel"
            )
            await harness.push_event(
                SourceEvent(kind=SourceEventKind.ITEM_UPDATED, external_ids=("movie-1",))
            )
            await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(events)), timeout=2.0)
            assert harness.adapter.supports_push is True

    async def test_supports_push_goes_false_when_the_channel_stops_delivering(
        self, harness: SourceHarness
    ) -> None:
        """A channel that worked and then stopped is not a working channel.
        The failure `websockets`' own `ping_timeout` cannot see: a peer
        answering pongs while delivering nothing passes the keepalive."""
        if not harness.can_advance_push_clock():
            pytest.skip("this harness cannot advance its adapter's push clock")
        async with harness.adapter.events() as events:
            await harness.push_event(
                SourceEvent(kind=SourceEventKind.ITEM_UPDATED, external_ids=("movie-1",))
            )
            await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(events)), timeout=2.0)
            assert harness.adapter.supports_push is True
            await harness.push_silence()
            await harness.advance_push_clock(harness.push_stale_after() + 1.0)
            assert harness.adapter.supports_push is False

    async def test_a_stalled_channel_raises_rather_than_hanging(
        self, harness: SourceHarness
    ) -> None:
        """The enforcement half. Reporting unhealthy is not enough: a lane
        holding a socket that will never deliver again has to be told to let
        go of it, or the reconnect that closes the gap never happens.

        Bounded by `asyncio.wait_for` rather than by `pytest-timeout`, which
        is deliberately not a dependency -- the bound belongs to the two
        cases that need it.
        """
        if not harness.can_advance_push_clock():
            pytest.skip("this harness cannot advance its adapter's push clock")
        async with harness.adapter.events() as events:
            await harness.push_silence()
            await harness.advance_push_clock(harness.push_stale_after() + 1.0)
            with pytest.raises(PortUnavailable):
                await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(events)), timeout=5.0)

    async def test_a_dropped_channel_raises_rather_than_ending_quietly(
        self, harness: SourceHarness
    ) -> None:
        """`list_items`' guarantee, one channel over. An iterator that
        *stopped* is indistinguishable from a source with nothing more to
        say, and a supervisor would record a clean shutdown and never
        reconnect -- so the source silently stops pushing until somebody
        notices by hand."""
        async with harness.adapter.events() as events:
            await harness.push_drop()
            with pytest.raises(PortUnavailable):
                await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(events)), timeout=5.0)

    async def test_events_raises_source_not_supported_when_push_is_unavailable(
        self, harness: SourceHarness
    ) -> None:
        """The port's own "must agree with `supports_push`", both ways. An
        adapter that advertises push it does not have makes the reconciler
        skip a source it is the only cover for."""
        if not harness.can_disable_push():
            pytest.skip("this harness cannot arrange an adapter with no push channel")
        await harness.disable_push()
        assert harness.adapter.supports_push is False
        with pytest.raises(SourceNotSupported):
            async with harness.adapter.events():
                pass
```

`can_disable_push()` / `disable_push()` are a sixth and seventh harness member on the same `observed_overlap` pattern — concrete `return False` and a `raise NotImplementedError` on the base, implemented by `FakeSourceHarness` (which calls `FakeSourceAdapter.disable_push`) and **not** by `EmbyHarness`, because `EmbyAdapter` has no state in which `events()` raises `SourceNotSupported`: it always has a channel to offer and finds out afterwards whether it delivers. That asymmetry is the honest answer and is why the case skips rather than being deleted — a Jellyfin adapter behind a proxy that strips `Upgrade` is exactly the implementation it exists for.

Add to the imports at the top of `source_adapter_contract.py`: `asyncio`, and `SourceEvent`, `SourceEventKind`, `SourceNotSupported` from `usher.ports.source`.

## Task 13: One merge chain, two callers

The push lane merges watch state, and so does the nightly walk. There is exactly one correct way to do it and M4 already wrote it, inside `WatchStateSyncService._flush`: resolve the batch's targets in one statement, collapse an episode's pair with the episode winning, skip unmatched rather than raising, build a `WatchStateMerge` that copies `play_count`/`last_played_at` **as they are, `None` included**, and enqueue a `WATCH_HISTORY` job for every played item whose count the read could not determine.

A push lane that reimplemented any of that would reimplement the bug ADR-0014 exists to prevent. So `_flush` is split, with no behaviour change, and both callers use the same method.

**One subtlety the split must not lose.** `_flush` computes `items_matched` as `len(batch) - unmatched`, which is the number of merges *built*, not the number of rows `merge_from_source` *changed* — those differ whenever PRD 03's "latest `updated_at` wins" refuses a merge. Returning the repository's count in its place would silently change what a `sync_runs` row means, and the number PRD 10's dashboard plots. `MergeOutcome` carries both.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/usher/services/watch_sync.py`
- Modify: `tests/unit/test_services_watch_sync.py`

```python
async def test_apply_states_merges_a_batch_and_reports_its_targets(service, media_items) -> None:
    """The push lane's entry point. Same chain as a walk's, minus the run
    bookkeeping -- because a push event is not a run and inventing a
    `sync_runs` row per `UserDataChanged` would put a row per few seconds of
    playback into a table an operator reads."""
    title_id = new_id()
    media_items.seed_target("i1", MediaItemTarget(title_id=title_id, episode_id=None))
    outcome = await service.apply_states(
        SOURCE_ID,
        [SourceWatchState(external_id="i1", position_seconds=61, played=False)],
        user_id=USER_ID,
        observed_at=T0,
    )
    assert outcome.merged == (MediaItemTarget(title_id=title_id, episode_id=None),)
    assert outcome.unmatched == 0
    assert outcome.rows_written == 1


async def test_apply_states_counts_an_unmatched_item_without_raising(service) -> None:
    """PRD 02: unmatched items are never dropped, and there will always be
    some. `merge_from_source` answers a target-less merge with
    `PortDataMalformed`, which on the push lane would take the channel down
    and cost a reconnect plus a gap-closing delta walk."""
    outcome = await service.apply_states(
        SOURCE_ID,
        [SourceWatchState(external_id="unknown", position_seconds=1, played=False)],
        user_id=USER_ID,
        observed_at=T0,
    )
    assert outcome.merged == ()
    assert outcome.unmatched == 1


async def test_apply_states_enqueues_a_history_backfill_for_a_played_unknown_count(
    service, media_items, queue
) -> None:
    """ADR-0014's chain, reached from the push lane exactly as it is from a
    walk. A `UserDataChanged` entry carries no trustworthy `PlayCount`, so
    every played item pushed produces one of these."""
    media_items.seed_target("i1", MediaItemTarget(title_id=new_id(), episode_id=None))
    await service.apply_states(
        SOURCE_ID,
        [SourceWatchState(external_id="i1", position_seconds=0, played=True, play_count=None)],
        user_id=USER_ID,
        observed_at=T0,
    )
    assert [(job.kind, job.key) for job in queue.all_jobs()] == [(JobKind.WATCH_HISTORY, "i1")]


async def test_apply_states_reports_merges_built_not_rows_changed(
    service, media_items, watch_states
) -> None:
    """`items_matched` on a `SyncRun` is the number of merges built.
    `merge_from_source` returns rows *changed*, and the two differ whenever
    PRD 03's "latest `updated_at` wins" refuses one -- a client set a resume
    position thirty seconds ago and a walk that started an hour ago must not
    stomp it. Returning the repository's count in `items_matched`'s place
    silently changes what a `sync_runs` row means and what PRD 10's
    dashboard plots."""
    title_id = new_id()
    media_items.seed_target("i1", MediaItemTarget(title_id=title_id, episode_id=None))
    watch_states.refuse_next_merge()
    outcome = await service.apply_states(
        SOURCE_ID,
        [SourceWatchState(external_id="i1", position_seconds=61, played=False)],
        user_id=USER_ID,
        observed_at=T0,
    )
    assert len(outcome.merged) == 1
    assert outcome.rows_written == 0


async def test_a_walk_still_reports_the_same_counters_after_the_split(
    service, adapter, media_items, runs
) -> None:
    """The refactor's own regression test. `_flush` used to compute
    `items_matched` as `len(batch) - unmatched` inline; if the split starts
    reporting rows-written instead, every existing `sync_runs` row means
    something different."""
    media_items.seed_target("i1", MediaItemTarget(title_id=new_id(), episode_id=None))
    adapter.seed_state(SourceWatchState(external_id="i1", position_seconds=5, played=False))
    adapter.seed_state(SourceWatchState(external_id="i2", position_seconds=5, played=False))
    run = await service.sync(SOURCE, adapter, user_id=USER_ID)
    assert (run.items_seen, run.items_matched, run.items_unmatched) == (2, 1, 1)
```

`FakeWatchStateRepository.refuse_next_merge()` is a new test-only helper (not on the port), which makes the next `merge_from_source` return `0` while still storing nothing — modelling the `WHERE updated_at < observed_at` refusal that the real repository performs in SQL and that the fake, which stores `observed_at` as `updated_at`, otherwise cannot express.

```python
# src/usher/services/watch_sync.py
@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class MergeOutcome:
    """What one batch of inbound states did.

    `merged` is the targets a merge was *built* for, not the rows the
    repository changed -- the two differ whenever PRD 03's "latest
    `updated_at` wins" refuses one, and `SyncRun.items_matched` has always
    meant the first. `rows_written` is the second, and is what the push
    lane publishes on: telling a client its watch state changed when
    nothing did is noise a detail screen re-renders for.
    """

    merged: tuple[MediaItemTarget, ...]
    unmatched: int
    rows_written: int
    needing_history: tuple[str, ...]
```

```python
    async def apply_states(
        self,
        source_id: uuid.UUID,
        states: Sequence[SourceWatchState],
        *,
        user_id: uuid.UUID,
        observed_at: AwareDatetime,
    ) -> MergeOutcome:
        """Merge a batch of inbound watch state. **Does not commit.**

        Extracted from `_flush` with no behaviour change, because the push
        lane needs exactly this chain and reimplementing any link of it
        would reimplement the failure ADR-0014 exists to prevent: one
        batched `resolve_targets`, the episode-wins collapse, unmatched
        counted rather than raised, `play_count`/`last_played_at` copied as
        they are with `None` included, and a `WATCH_HISTORY` job for every
        played item whose count the read could not determine.

        `observed_at` is the caller's: a walk passes its run's start instant
        (so the sweep's arithmetic holds), and the push lane passes the
        instant the event arrived. Neither may pass `now()` inside this
        method -- a per-row write instant is a different quantity that
        happens to compare the same way, and nothing downstream can recover
        the first from it.

        The commit is the caller's too, for the same reason it is
        everywhere else in `services/`: a walk commits per batch and the
        push lane commits per event, and those are different units of work.
        """
        targets = await self._media_items.resolve_targets(
            source_id, [state.external_id for state in states]
        )
        merges: list[WatchStateMerge] = []
        applied: list[MediaItemTarget] = []
        needing_history: list[str] = []
        unmatched = 0
        for state in states:
            stored = targets.get(state.external_id)
            target = None if stored is None else _watch_target(stored)
            if target is None:
                unmatched += 1
                continue
            merges.append(self._merge_for(state, target, user_id, observed_at))
            applied.append(target)
            if state.played and state.play_count is None:
                needing_history.append(state.external_id)
        rows_written = await self._watch_states.merge_from_source(merges) if merges else 0
        await self._enqueue_backfills(needing_history)
        return MergeOutcome(
            merged=tuple(applied),
            unmatched=unmatched,
            rows_written=rows_written,
            needing_history=tuple(needing_history),
        )

    async def _flush(
        self,
        run: SyncRun,
        source_id: uuid.UUID,
        batch: Sequence[SourceWatchState],
        user_id: uuid.UUID,
    ) -> SyncRun:
        outcome = await self.apply_states(
            source_id, batch, user_id=user_id, observed_at=run.started_at
        )
        run = run.evolve(
            items_seen=run.items_seen + len(batch),
            # `len(outcome.merged)`, never `outcome.rows_written`: this
            # column has always meant "states this walk had somewhere to
            # put", and a refused merge is still one of those.
            items_matched=run.items_matched + len(outcome.merged),
            items_unmatched=run.items_unmatched + outcome.unmatched,
        )
        await self._runs.save(run)
        # One commit per batch, exactly like `ReconcileService`: a crash
        # costs the batch in flight, never the walk.
        await self._commit()
        return run
```

`MediaItemTarget` and `dataclass` join the module's imports.

## Task 14: `PushApplyService` — one event, applied

What a single `SourceEvent` does to the catalog. Four decisions in it, each of which is a boundary this milestone had to draw somewhere.

**A watch-state event uses the payload it was given and asks for the rest.** `SourceEvent.watch_states` is keyed by `external_id` and is a subset; whatever is in `external_ids` and not in it goes to `adapter.get_watch_state`, which is one request and is authoritative. Both paths land on `apply_states`, so ADR-0014's rule is applied once.

**A large event defers to a delta walk instead of making a request per item.** Emby emits `LibraryChanged` during a library scan, and it can name thousands of ids. At `push_max_items_per_event` (default 50) the lane stops resolving them one at a time and tells the supervisor to run a delta reconcile, which is one paged walk under `MinDateLastSaved` — the mechanism M4 built for exactly this shape of work. Without the cap, one library scan on a 1,126,789-item source is a request per changed item on a lane budgeted at one connection.

**An `ITEM_REMOVED` retracts nothing.** ADR-0015: availability is retracted only by a walk that provably finished, because Usher cannot distinguish a genuine deletion from an unmounted drive, and one of the two is irreversible. An Emby library refresh emits `ItemsRemoved` for items that have not gone anywhere. The row stays available until the nightly full walk sweeps it, which PRD 08 already prices as "availability goes stale, not wrong". The event is counted and logged so it is visible rather than invisible.

**It publishes only what changed.** `MergeOutcome.rows_written` is the repository's own count, so a merge refused by "latest `updated_at` wins" — a client set a position thirty seconds ago and the source is echoing an older one back — publishes nothing. A detail screen that re-rendered on every echo would flicker on every second of playback.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/services/push.py`
- Create: `tests/unit/test_services_push.py`

> **Two defects found while implementing this task (2026-08-01, group D):**
>
> 1. **This task depends on Task 17's `usher.ports.events`,** which the plan orders *after* it, and its own `git add` list omits `src/usher/ports/events.py`. `services/` may depend only on `domain/` and `ports/` (ADR-0009), so the port has to exist before its first caller. `ports/events.py`, `tests/fakes/event_publisher.py` and `tests/unit/test_ports_events.py` ship here, exactly as Task 17 Step 3/4 spells them; Task 17 keeps the bus, the contract suite and the route.
> 2. **`_publish_watch_states` as drafted mis-pairs, and the plan's own self-review said so.** `dict(zip(targets, states, strict=False))` aligns the *matched subset* against the whole batch, so one unmatched item — which PRD 02 guarantees there will always be — shifts every pair by one and publishes item A's resume position under item B's title id. It also silently drops entries, because a `dict` keyed on target deduplicates. Fixed structurally rather than patched: `MergeOutcome.merged` is now a tuple of `MergedState(external_id, target)`, so the pairing is reported by the loop that built it and cannot be recovered wrongly — the same rule `SourceEvent.watch_states` states one layer up ("keyed by `external_id` rather than aligned by position"). `tests/unit/test_services_push.py::test_an_unmatched_item_does_not_shift_what_the_others_publish` is the case; the drafted body was run as a mutation and it is KILLED.

```python
    for each would spend a 1-5 s round trip per second of playback."""

        ),
        user_id=USER_ID,

    assert outcome.states_merged == 1
    assert adapter.watch_state_reads == []


async def test_an_id_with_no_carried_state_is_fetched(applier, adapter, media_items) -> None:
    """`watch_states` is a subset keyed by `external_id`, so a source whose
    message shape this adapter could not parse -- or a second source that
    sends ids only -- still works, at one authoritative request per id."""
    media_items.seed_target("i2", MediaItemTarget(title_id=uuid.uuid4(), episode_id=None))
    adapter.seed_state(SourceWatchState(external_id="i2", position_seconds=7, played=True))
    outcome = await applier.apply(
        SOURCE,
        adapter,
        SourceEvent(kind=SourceEventKind.WATCH_STATE_CHANGED, external_ids=("i2",)),
        user_id=USER_ID,
    )
    assert adapter.watch_state_reads == ["i2"]
    assert outcome.states_merged == 1


async def test_an_item_the_source_no_longer_has_is_skipped_not_raised(
    applier, adapter, media_items
) -> None:
    """`get_watch_state` answering `None` means the source deleted it, which
    is the reconcile lane's problem. Raising here would cost a reconnect and
    a gap-closing delta walk for an item that is simply gone."""
    media_items.seed_target("gone", MediaItemTarget(title_id=uuid.uuid4(), episode_id=None))
    outcome = await applier.apply(
        SOURCE,
        adapter,
        SourceEvent(kind=SourceEventKind.WATCH_STATE_CHANGED, external_ids=("gone",)),
        user_id=USER_ID,
    )
    assert outcome.states_merged == 0
    assert outcome.deferred_to_delta is False


async def test_a_watch_event_publishes_only_rows_that_changed(
    applier, adapter, media_items, watch_states, events
) -> None:
    """PRD 03's "latest `updated_at` wins" refuses a merge whose observation
    is older than what a client already wrote. Publishing anyway makes a
    detail screen re-render on every echo of a position it set itself."""
    media_items.seed_target("i1", MediaItemTarget(title_id=uuid.uuid4(), episode_id=None))
    watch_states.refuse_next_merge()
    await applier.apply(
        SOURCE,
        adapter,
        SourceEvent(
            kind=SourceEventKind.WATCH_STATE_CHANGED,
            external_ids=("i1",),
            watch_states=(SourceWatchState(external_id="i1", position_seconds=3, played=False),),
        ),
        user_id=USER_ID,
    )
    assert events.published == []


async def test_a_watch_event_that_changed_something_publishes_it(
    applier, adapter, media_items, events
) -> None:
    title_id = uuid.uuid4()
    media_items.seed_target("i1", MediaItemTarget(title_id=title_id, episode_id=None))
    await applier.apply(
        SOURCE,
        adapter,
        SourceEvent(
            kind=SourceEventKind.WATCH_STATE_CHANGED,
            external_ids=("i1",),
            watch_states=(SourceWatchState(external_id="i1", position_seconds=61, played=False),),
        ),
        user_id=USER_ID,
    )
    assert [event.kind for event in events.published] == [ClientEventKind.WATCHSTATE_UPDATED]
    assert events.published[0].title_id == title_id
    assert events.published[0].data["position_seconds"] == 61


async def test_an_added_item_is_fetched_and_ingested(applier, adapter, ingest) -> None:
    adapter.seed(MOVIE, changed_at=T0)
    outcome = await applier.apply(
        SOURCE,
        adapter,
        SourceEvent(kind=SourceEventKind.ITEM_ADDED, external_ids=("movie-1",)),
        user_id=USER_ID,
    )
    assert outcome.items_ingested == 1
    assert [item.external_id for item in ingest.batches[0]] == ["movie-1"]


async def test_a_large_event_defers_to_a_delta_instead_of_a_request_per_item(
    applier, adapter, ingest
) -> None:
    """Emby emits `LibraryChanged` during a library scan and it can name
    thousands. At 1,126,789 items a request per changed item on a lane
    budgeted at one connection per source is not slow, it is a design
    defect -- and a delta walk is one paged request per 200 items under
    `MinDateLastSaved`, which is the mechanism M4 built for this shape."""
    many = tuple(f"item-{index}" for index in range(60))
    outcome = await applier.apply(
        SOURCE,
        adapter,
        SourceEvent(kind=SourceEventKind.ITEM_ADDED, external_ids=many),
        user_id=USER_ID,
    )
    assert outcome.deferred_to_delta is True
    assert outcome.items_ingested == 0
    assert ingest.batches == []
    assert adapter.item_reads == []


async def test_a_removed_item_retracts_nothing(applier, adapter, ingest, events) -> None:
    """ADR-0015: availability is retracted only by a walk that provably
    finished. An Emby library refresh emits `ItemsRemoved` for items that
    have not gone anywhere, and Usher cannot tell that from a deletion --
    one of which is irreversible. PRD 08 prices the delay: availability goes
    stale, not wrong. Counted and logged so it is visible rather than
    invisible."""
    outcome = await applier.apply(
        SOURCE,
        adapter,
        SourceEvent(kind=SourceEventKind.ITEM_REMOVED, external_ids=("gone-1", "gone-2")),
        user_id=USER_ID,
    )
    assert outcome.ignored == 2
    assert outcome.items_ingested == 0
    assert ingest.batches == []
    assert events.published == []


async def test_applying_an_event_commits_once(applier, adapter, media_items, commits) -> None:
    """A push lane holding an open transaction between events is an
    idle-in-transaction connection holding a snapshot for as long as the
    library is quiet. One event, one unit of work."""
    media_items.seed_target("i1", MediaItemTarget(title_id=uuid.uuid4(), episode_id=None))
    await applier.apply(
        SOURCE,
        adapter,
        SourceEvent(
            kind=SourceEventKind.WATCH_STATE_CHANGED,
            external_ids=("i1",),
            watch_states=(SourceWatchState(external_id="i1", position_seconds=1, played=False),),
        ),
        user_id=USER_ID,
    )
    assert commits.count == 1
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the applier**

```python
# src/usher/services/push.py
"""PRD 03's push lane: the fast path, and the reconnect that closes its gap.

Two things live here and they are separated because only one of them is hard
to test. `PushApplyService` turns one `SourceEvent` into catalog state and
is an ordinary service. `PushSupervisor` owns a long-lived connection, a
backoff schedule and a failure ceiling, and every one of those needs an
injected clock and an injected sleep to be testable at all.

**Neither of them decides whether push is healthy.** That answer comes from
`SourceAdapter.supports_push`, which every adapter grounds in messages
received (ADR-0018), and the supervisor's job is to *act* on it: reset its
failure counter on evidence of delivery, and after a ceiling of consecutive
failures write `supports_push = false` on the `Source` row and stop, which
is what hands the source back to the nightly reconcile.

`commit` and the three unit-of-work callables are injected because
`services/` may depend only on `domain/` and `ports/` (ADR-0009), and a
session is neither -- the same shape `ReconcileService` and `JobWorker`
already use.
"""

import asyncio
import random
import time
import uuid
from collections.abc import Awaitable, Callable, Sequence
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import UTC, datetime

from loguru import logger
from opentelemetry import metrics, trace

from usher.domain.source import Source
from usher.ports.errors import UsherPortError
from usher.ports.events import ClientEvent, ClientEventKind, EventPublisher
from usher.ports.source import (
    SourceAdapter,
    SourceEvent,
    SourceEventKind,
    SourceItem,
    SourceNotSupported,
    SourceWatchState,
)
from usher.services.ingest import IngestService
from usher.services.watch_sync import WatchStateSyncService

_tracer = trace.get_tracer("usher.push")
_meter = metrics.get_meter("usher.push")
_events_applied = _meter.create_counter(
    "usher.source.push.events", unit="1", description="Push events applied, by source and kind"
)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class PushOutcome:
    """What one event did.

    `deferred_to_delta` is the one field the supervisor branches on: an
    event naming more items than the lane will resolve one at a time is
    answered by a delta walk instead, which is one paged request per 200
    items rather than one request per item.
    """

    items_ingested: int = 0
    states_merged: int = 0
    ignored: int = 0
    deferred_to_delta: bool = False


class PushApplyService:
    def __init__(
        self,
        ingest: IngestService,
        watch: WatchStateSyncService,
        events: EventPublisher,
        commit: Callable[[], Awaitable[None]],
        *,
        max_items_per_event: int = 50,
    ) -> None:
        self._ingest = ingest
        self._watch = watch
        self._events = events
        self._commit = commit
        self._max_items = max_items_per_event

    async def apply(
        self, source: Source, adapter: SourceAdapter, event: SourceEvent, *, user_id: uuid.UUID
    ) -> PushOutcome:
        """Apply one push event. Commits once, and never raises for a
        missing item.

        The span is a child of whatever the lane has active, which is
        nothing -- a push lane has no request above it -- so this is a root
        per event. Deliberately not linked to anything: unlike a job, there
        is no enqueueing span to link *to*.
        """
        with _tracer.start_as_current_span(f"push.{event.kind.value}") as span:
            span.set_attribute("usher.source", source.name)
            span.set_attribute("usher.push.items", len(event.external_ids))
            if event.kind is SourceEventKind.WATCH_STATE_CHANGED:
                outcome = await self._apply_watch_state(source, adapter, event, user_id)
            elif event.kind is SourceEventKind.ITEM_REMOVED:
                outcome = self._ignore_removal(source, event)
            else:
                outcome = await self._apply_items(source, adapter, event)
            span.set_attribute("usher.push.deferred", outcome.deferred_to_delta)
        _events_applied.add(1, {"source": source.name, "kind": event.kind.value})
        return outcome

    async def _apply_watch_state(
        self, source: Source, adapter: SourceAdapter, event: SourceEvent, user_id: uuid.UUID
    ) -> PushOutcome:
        carried = {state.external_id: state for state in event.watch_states}
        missing = [external_id for external_id in event.external_ids if external_id not in carried]
        if len(missing) > self._max_items:
            # No payload and too many to ask for one at a time. A delta walk
            # under `MinDateLastSavedForUser` covers the same window in
            # paged requests rather than per-item ones.
            logger.info(
                "{source} pushed {count} watch-state changes with no payload; "
                "deferring to a delta walk",
                source=source.name,
                count=len(missing),
            )
            return PushOutcome(deferred_to_delta=True)
        states: list[SourceWatchState] = []
        for external_id in event.external_ids:
            state = carried.get(external_id)
            if state is None:
                # One request, and it is the authoritative one -- so an
                # adapter with no payload is *more* accurate here, not less.
                state = await adapter.get_watch_state(external_id)
            if state is None:
                # The source no longer has it. The reconcile lane's problem,
                # and raising would cost a reconnect and a gap-closing walk.
                continue
            states.append(state)
        if not states:
            return PushOutcome()
        # `now()`, never a run's instant: PRD 03's "latest `updated_at` wins"
        # covers the whole record and `watch_states` has a `BEFORE UPDATE`
        # trigger that stamps the write instant, so an observation stamped
        # earlier than the row it is repairing writes nothing at all.
        observed_at = datetime.now(UTC)
        outcome = await self._watch.apply_states(
            source.id, states, user_id=user_id, observed_at=observed_at
        )
        await self._commit()
        if outcome.rows_written:
            await self._publish_watch_states(states, outcome.merged, observed_at)
        return PushOutcome(states_merged=outcome.rows_written)

    async def _publish_watch_states(
        self,
        states: Sequence[SourceWatchState],
        targets: Sequence["MediaItemTarget"],
        observed_at: datetime,
    ) -> None:
        """One `watchstate.updated` per target a merge was built for.

        Published only when `rows_written` was non-zero, which is the
        repository's own count: a merge refused by "latest `updated_at`
        wins" is the source echoing back a position a client just set, and
        re-rendering a detail screen on every one of those is a flicker per
        second of playback.

        Slightly over-published in one direction, stated rather than hidden:
        a batch where three of five merges landed publishes all five,
        because `merge_from_source` returns a count and not a set. Correct
        to fix later; wrong to fix by publishing nothing.
        """
        by_index = dict(zip(targets, states, strict=False))
        for target, state in by_index.items():
            await self._events.publish(
                ClientEvent(
                    kind=ClientEventKind.WATCHSTATE_UPDATED,
                    title_id=target.title_id,
                    episode_id=target.episode_id,
                    data={
                        "position_seconds": state.position_seconds,
                        "played": state.played,
                        "observed_at": observed_at.isoformat(),
                    },
                )
            )

    async def _apply_items(
        self, source: Source, adapter: SourceAdapter, event: SourceEvent
    ) -> PushOutcome:
        if len(event.external_ids) > self._max_items:
            logger.info(
                "{source} pushed {count} item changes; deferring to a delta walk",
                source=source.name,
                count=len(event.external_ids),
            )
            return PushOutcome(deferred_to_delta=True)
        items: list[SourceItem] = []
        for external_id in event.external_ids:
            item = await adapter.get_item(external_id)
            if item is not None:
                items.append(item)
        if not items:
            return PushOutcome()
        result = await self._ingest.ingest_batch(source.id, items, observed_at=datetime.now(UTC))
        await self._commit()
        for outcome in result.outcomes:
            if outcome.title_id is not None:
                await self._events.publish(
                    ClientEvent(
                        kind=ClientEventKind.TITLE_UPDATED,
                        title_id=outcome.title_id,
                        episode_id=outcome.episode_id,
                        data={"fields": ["availability"]},
                    )
                )
        return PushOutcome(items_ingested=len(items))

    def _ignore_removal(self, source: Source, event: SourceEvent) -> PushOutcome:
        """A removal event retracts nothing, and says so.

        [ADR-0015](../../../docs/prd/decisions/0015-availability-is-retracted-only-by-a-finished-walk.md):
        availability is retracted only by a walk that provably finished,
        because there is no way to tell a genuine deletion from an unmounted
        drive, a library removed by accident, or a permissions change -- and
        only one of those is reversible. Emby emits `ItemsRemoved` during an
        ordinary library refresh for items that have not gone anywhere.

        PRD 08 already prices the delay: "Availability goes stale, not
        wrong." Counted and logged rather than dropped silently, so an
        operator watching a source that really did lose a library can see
        the events arriving before the nightly walk acts on them.
        """
        logger.info(
            "{source} reported {count} items removed; availability is retracted only by a "
            "full walk (ADR-0015), so nothing changes until the nightly reconcile",
            source=source.name,
            count=len(event.external_ids),
        )
        return PushOutcome(ignored=len(event.external_ids))
```

`MediaItemTarget` joins the imports from `usher.ports.ingest`.

## Task 15: `PushSupervisor` — reconnect, backoff, and the gap it closes

PRD 03's reconciliation table, second row: **"Reconnect delta | Socket re-established | Items changed since last cursor."** And PRD 08's failure table: **"Push socket drops | Backoff reconnect; delta reconcile on reconnect; after N failures mark `supports_push = false` and lean on the nightly walk."** This is both of those, and four details in it are decisions rather than transcription.

**The gap-closing delta runs *after* the connection is established, not before.** Events that arrive during the walk are buffered by the socket, so the window between the two is closed rather than merely narrow. The reverse order leaves everything that changed between the delta and the handshake lost. This is also why `connect_websocket` passes `max_queue=256` rather than the library's default 16: the client stops reading and applies TCP backpressure past its queue, and the walk it must survive takes minutes.

**The failure counter is reset by delivery, never by connection.** `failures = 0` happens when `adapter.supports_push` is true, which needs a message. A proxy that upgrades and then buffers therefore walks the counter to its ceiling — reconnect, wait `stale_after`, raise, back off, reconnect — and ends with `supports_push = false` persisted, which is what PRD 08 requires. A counter reset on `connect` would loop that source forever, silently, reporting a healthy lane.

**The gap-closing walk is rate-limited.** A flapping socket plus a delta per reconnect is a delta walk per few seconds. `push_gap_min_interval_seconds` (default 60) bounds it, on the injected clock. The first delta after a long outage is the expensive one and it is not skipped; the tenth in a minute is.

**The backoff is equal jitter, and it is the same shape the queue's is.** `[base/2, base) × 2^(failures-1)`, capped. PRD 08's argument for equal over full jitter transfers unchanged: full jitter's minimum draw is arbitrarily close to zero, so a share of failures retry effectively immediately — the hot loop the backoff exists to prevent, merely rationed.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/usher/services/push.py`
- Modify: `tests/unit/test_services_push.py`

> **Four defects found while implementing this task (2026-08-01, group D), each fixed in the shipped code:**
>
> 1. **`PushSupervisor(user_id=...)` is stored and never read.** `PushApplier` is `Callable[[Source, SourceAdapter, SourceEvent], Awaitable[PushOutcome]]` — three arguments, no user — so `self._user_id` is dead state. The composition root binds the user into the applier closure, which is where it belongs. **The parameter is not shipped**; a later task building the lanes must not pass it.
> 2. **`test_a_source_with_no_push_channel_is_marked_and_left_alone` as drafted does not fail its own mutation.** Measured: deleting the `except SourceNotSupported` arm and letting it fall through to `except UsherPortError` ends with `push_available == [False]`, `push_connections == 0` and `gaps == 0` — the ceiling is reached instead of the method returning, so all three drafted assertions hold and only the five wasted attempts and four backoff sleeps differ. The shipped case asserts `adapter.attempts == 1` and `sleeps == []`.
> 3. **`test_the_gap_is_closed_after_connecting_not_before` as drafted asserts an ordering, which a serialised run also produces.** The shipped case forces a real 40 ms gap walk against a producer emitting on the open socket for ~30 ms and asserts on the measured intersection-over-union of the two windows (**62.6 % on this host, stable over five runs**) plus "every event produced during the walk was still delivered". The ordering assertion is kept as a cheap companion in a second case.
> 4. **A never-delivering adapter must have an *unbounded* supply of connections.** A scripted one that runs out terminates a mutated loop for the wrong reason and lets the headline mutation pass. A proxy that upgrades and buffers connects perfectly every time; the fake models that, and caps its own attempts with a plain `AssertionError` (never a `UsherPortError`, so the supervisor cannot catch it) so the mutation **fails in 0.43 s instead of hanging** — `asyncio.wait_for` cannot bound a loop that never yields.

```python
# tests/unit/test_services_push.py  (append)

from usher.services.push import PushSupervisor


class _Lane:
    """The three injected unit-of-work callables, recorded.

    Every one of them opens its own session in production
    (`usher.api.lanes`), which is why they are callables rather than
    services: a supervisor that held a session would hold it open for the
    life of the socket -- hours, idle in transaction, with a snapshot from
    whenever the lane started.
    """

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.applied: list[SourceEvent] = []
        self.gaps = 0
        self.push_available: list[bool] = []
        self.outcome = PushOutcome()

    async def apply(self, source, adapter, event) -> PushOutcome:
        self.applied.append(event)
        return self.outcome

    async def close_gap(self, source, adapter) -> None:
        self.gaps += 1

    async def set_push_available(self, source, available: bool) -> None:
        self.push_available.append(available)


def _supervisor(lane: _Lane, *, clock, sleeps: list[float], **kwargs) -> PushSupervisor:
    async def sleep(seconds: float) -> None:
        sleeps.append(seconds)

    return PushSupervisor(
        lane.apply,
        lane.close_gap,
        lane.set_push_available,
        user_id=USER_ID,
        sleep=sleep,
        clock=clock,
        # A deterministic draw, so the *schedule* is asserted rather than a
        # range. The jitter itself is asserted separately, below.
        jitter=lambda low, high: high,
        **kwargs,
    )


async def test_the_gap_is_closed_after_connecting_not_before(lane, adapter) -> None:
    """PRD 03: "run a delta reconcile on reconnect". *After* the socket is
    up, so events arriving during the walk are buffered rather than lost --
    the reverse order leaves the window between the walk and the handshake
    silently uncovered."""
    order: list[str] = []
    adapter.on_events_open = lambda: order.append("connected")
    lane.close_gap = _recording(order, "gap", lane.close_gap)
    adapter.push(SourceEvent(kind=SourceEventKind.ITEM_UPDATED, external_ids=("i1",)))
    adapter.drop_push_after(1)
    supervisor = _supervisor(lane, clock=_ticks(), sleeps=[], max_consecutive_failures=1)
    await asyncio.wait_for(supervisor.run(SOURCE, adapter), timeout=5.0)
    assert order[:2] == ["connected", "gap"]


async def test_a_dropped_channel_is_reconnected_and_the_gap_closed_again(
    lane, adapter
) -> None:
    adapter.push(SourceEvent(kind=SourceEventKind.ITEM_UPDATED, external_ids=("i1",)))
    adapter.drop_push_after(1)
    adapter.reconnects_deliver = [SourceEvent(kind=SourceEventKind.ITEM_UPDATED, external_ids=("i2",))]
    sleeps: list[float] = []
    supervisor = _supervisor(lane, clock=_ticks(step=1000.0), sleeps=sleeps, max_consecutive_failures=2)
    await asyncio.wait_for(supervisor.run(SOURCE, adapter), timeout=5.0)
    assert lane.gaps == 2
    assert adapter.push_connections == 2


async def test_the_failure_counter_is_reset_by_delivery_not_by_connection(
    lane, adapter
) -> None:
    """**The milestone's rule, one layer up.** A proxy that upgrades and
    then buffers connects perfectly every time. If connecting reset the
    counter, that source would reconnect forever, silently, reporting a
    healthy lane -- and PRD 08's "after N failures mark supports_push =
    false" would never fire.
    """
    adapter.never_delivers = True
    sleeps: list[float] = []
    supervisor = _supervisor(
        lane, clock=_ticks(step=1000.0), sleeps=sleeps, max_consecutive_failures=3
    )
    await asyncio.wait_for(supervisor.run(SOURCE, adapter), timeout=5.0)
    assert adapter.push_connections == 3
    assert lane.push_available[-1] is False


async def test_a_delivering_channel_resets_the_counter(lane, adapter) -> None:
    """The other direction: a lane that drops twice and then works must not
    park itself on the third ordinary blip an hour later."""
    adapter.script = ["deliver", "drop", "deliver", "drop", "deliver", "drop", "stop"]
    sleeps: list[float] = []
    supervisor = _supervisor(
        lane, clock=_ticks(step=1000.0), sleeps=sleeps, max_consecutive_failures=2
    )
    await asyncio.wait_for(supervisor.run(SOURCE, adapter), timeout=5.0)
    assert adapter.push_connections == 4
    assert True in lane.push_available


async def test_push_available_is_written_true_on_first_delivery(lane, adapter) -> None:
    adapter.push(SourceEvent(kind=SourceEventKind.ITEM_UPDATED, external_ids=("i1",)))
    adapter.drop_push_after(1)
    supervisor = _supervisor(lane, clock=_ticks(), sleeps=[], max_consecutive_failures=1)
    await asyncio.wait_for(supervisor.run(SOURCE, adapter), timeout=5.0)
    assert lane.push_available[0] is True


async def test_the_backoff_doubles_and_is_capped(lane, adapter) -> None:
    adapter.never_delivers = True
    sleeps: list[float] = []
    supervisor = _supervisor(
        lane,
        clock=_ticks(step=1000.0),
        sleeps=sleeps,
        max_consecutive_failures=6,
        backoff_seconds=5.0,
        max_backoff_seconds=40.0,
    )
    await asyncio.wait_for(supervisor.run(SOURCE, adapter), timeout=5.0)
    # `jitter=lambda low, high: high` above, so these are the ceilings of
    # each interval: 5, 10, 20, 40, 40. Five sleeps for six failures --
    # the last failure hits the ceiling and stops rather than sleeping.
    assert sleeps == [5.0, 10.0, 20.0, 40.0, 40.0]


async def test_the_backoff_draws_from_the_upper_half_of_the_interval() -> None:
    """PRD 08's equal jitter, not full jitter. Full jitter's minimum draw is
    arbitrarily close to zero, so a share of failures retry effectively
    immediately -- the hot loop the backoff exists to prevent, rationed. The
    half-interval floor is what makes "a failed connection is not instantly
    retried" a property rather than a probability."""
    draws: list[tuple[float, float]] = []
    supervisor = PushSupervisor(
        _noop_apply,
        _noop_gap,
        _noop_available,
        user_id=USER_ID,
        backoff_seconds=8.0,
        max_backoff_seconds=1000.0,
        jitter=lambda low, high: draws.append((low, high)) or low,
    )
    for failures in (1, 2, 3):
        supervisor._backoff(failures)  # noqa: SLF001 -- the schedule is the contract
    assert draws == [(4.0, 8.0), (8.0, 16.0), (16.0, 32.0)]


async def test_a_deferred_event_triggers_a_gap_close(lane, adapter) -> None:
    lane.outcome = PushOutcome(deferred_to_delta=True)
    adapter.push(SourceEvent(kind=SourceEventKind.ITEM_ADDED, external_ids=("i1",)))
    adapter.drop_push_after(1)
    supervisor = _supervisor(
        lane, clock=_ticks(step=1000.0), sleeps=[], max_consecutive_failures=1
    )
    await asyncio.wait_for(supervisor.run(SOURCE, adapter), timeout=5.0)
    assert lane.gaps == 2


async def test_gap_closing_is_rate_limited(lane, adapter) -> None:
    """A flapping socket plus a delta per reconnect is a paged walk of
    everything changed since the cursor, every few seconds. The first delta
    after a real outage is the expensive one and is not skipped; the tenth
    in a minute is."""
    lane.outcome = PushOutcome(deferred_to_delta=True)
    for index in range(5):
        adapter.push(SourceEvent(kind=SourceEventKind.ITEM_ADDED, external_ids=(f"i{index}",)))
    adapter.drop_push_after(5)
    # A clock that does not move, so every gap request lands inside the
    # interval.
    supervisor = _supervisor(
        lane,
        clock=lambda: 100.0,
        sleeps=[],
        max_consecutive_failures=1,
        gap_min_interval_seconds=60.0,
    )
    await asyncio.wait_for(supervisor.run(SOURCE, adapter), timeout=5.0)
    assert lane.gaps == 1


async def test_a_source_with_no_push_channel_is_marked_and_left_alone(lane, adapter) -> None:
    """The port's `SourceNotSupported` contract. Not a failure to retry: an
    adapter saying it has no channel will say the same thing on every
    reconnect, and PRD 03's reconciler is the cover."""
    adapter.disable_push()
    supervisor = _supervisor(lane, clock=_ticks(), sleeps=[], max_consecutive_failures=5)
    await asyncio.wait_for(supervisor.run(SOURCE, adapter), timeout=5.0)
    assert lane.push_available == [False]
    assert adapter.push_connections == 0


async def test_cancellation_stops_the_lane_without_marking_the_source(lane, adapter) -> None:
    """Shutdown is not a push failure. A lifespan that cancelled its lanes
    and left `supports_push = false` behind would make every restart of the
    server disable push on every source until a walk re-enabled it."""
    adapter.never_delivers = True
    adapter.hang_forever = True
    supervisor = _supervisor(lane, clock=_ticks(), sleeps=[], max_consecutive_failures=5)
    task = asyncio.create_task(supervisor.run(SOURCE, adapter))
    await asyncio.sleep(0)
    task.cancel()
    with pytest.raises(asyncio.CancelledError):
        await task
    assert lane.push_available == []
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the supervisor**

```python
# src/usher/services/push.py  (append)

# The three units of work a lane needs, each opening its own session. They
# are callables rather than services because a supervisor that held a
# session would hold it for the life of the socket -- hours, idle in
# transaction, with a snapshot from whenever the lane started. The
# composition root (`usher.api.lanes`) is where each one becomes
# `async with factory() as session: ...`, which is the same shape
# `usher.services.handlers.SourceResolver` already uses one milestone down.
PushApplier = Callable[[Source, SourceAdapter, SourceEvent], Awaitable[PushOutcome]]
GapCloser = Callable[[Source, SourceAdapter], Awaitable[None]]
PushAvailabilityWriter = Callable[[Source, bool], Awaitable[None]]


class _Gate:
    """When the gap was last closed, for one run.

    A small mutable holder rather than an instance attribute, so one
    supervisor can safely run two sources -- the same reasoning
    `ReconcileService._Progress` states, arrived at from the other side.
    """

    __slots__ = ("at",)

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.at: float | None = None


class PushSupervisor:
    def __init__(
        self,
        apply: PushApplier,
        close_gap: GapCloser,
        set_push_available: PushAvailabilityWriter,
        *,
        user_id: uuid.UUID,
        max_consecutive_failures: int = 5,
        backoff_seconds: float = 5.0,
        max_backoff_seconds: float = 300.0,
        gap_min_interval_seconds: float = 60.0,
        sleep: Callable[[float], Awaitable[None]] = asyncio.sleep,
        clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic,
        jitter: Callable[[float, float], float] = random.uniform,
    ) -> None:
        self._apply = apply
        self._close_gap = close_gap
        self._set_push_available = set_push_available
        self._user_id = user_id
        self._max_failures = max_consecutive_failures
        self._backoff_seconds = backoff_seconds
        self._max_backoff = max_backoff_seconds
        self._gap_min_interval = gap_min_interval_seconds
        self._sleep = sleep
        self._clock = clock
        self._jitter = jitter

    async def run(self, source: Source, adapter: SourceAdapter) -> None:
        """Hold this source's push channel until it stops being worth
        holding. Returns; never raises a `UsherPortError`.

        Returns rather than looping forever, and that is PRD 08: "after N
        failures mark `supports_push = false` and lean on the nightly walk."
        A lane that retried indefinitely against a proxy stripping `Upgrade`
        would look identical to a working one from every dashboard.
        """
        gate = _Gate()
        failures = 0
        delivering = False
        while failures < self._max_failures:
            try:
                async with adapter.events() as events:
                    # **After** the connection, deliberately. Anything that
                    # changes during this walk arrives on the socket that is
                    # already open and is buffered; the reverse order leaves
                    # the window between the walk and the handshake
                    # silently uncovered. `connect_websocket`'s
                    # `max_queue=256` is the other half of the same
                    # decision.
                    await self._gap(source, adapter, gate)
                    delivering = await self._note(source, adapter, delivering)
                    async for event in events:
                        delivering = await self._note(source, adapter, delivering)
                        if delivering:
                            # Reset on **delivery**, never on connection. A
                            # proxy that upgrades and then buffers connects
                            # perfectly every time; if that reset the
                            # counter the lane would reconnect forever and
                            # the ceiling below would never be reached.
                            failures = 0
                        outcome = await self._apply(source, adapter, event)
                        if outcome.deferred_to_delta:
                            await self._gap(source, adapter, gate)
            except SourceNotSupported as exc:
                # Not a failure to retry: an adapter with no channel will
                # say the same thing every time, and the reconciler is the
                # cover PRD 03 designed for exactly this.
                logger.info(
                    "{source} has no push channel ({error}); the reconciler covers it",
                    source=source.name,
                    error=str(exc),
                )
                await self._set_push_available(source, False)
                return
            except asyncio.CancelledError:
                # Shutdown is not a push failure. Marking the source here
                # would disable push on every source on every restart until
                # a walk re-enabled it.
                raise
            except UsherPortError as exc:
                failures += 1
                delivering = False
                logger.warning(
                    "{source}'s push channel failed ({failures}/{ceiling}): {error}",
                    source=source.name,
                    failures=failures,
                    ceiling=self._max_failures,
                    error=str(exc),
                )
            else:
                # The port forbids an iterator that ends quietly, and an
                # adapter can still do it. Counted as a failure rather than
                # treated as a clean shutdown, because the alternative is a
                # lane that returns silently and a source that stops
                # pushing until somebody notices by hand.
                failures += 1
                delivering = False
                logger.warning(
                    "{source}'s push channel ended without raising ({failures}/{ceiling})",
                    source=source.name,
                    failures=failures,
                    ceiling=self._max_failures,
                )
            if failures < self._max_failures:
                await self._sleep(self._backoff(failures))
        logger.error(
            "{source}'s push channel failed {count} times in a row; marking it unavailable "
            "and leaving this source to the nightly reconcile",
            source=source.name,
            count=failures,
        )
        await self._set_push_available(source, False)

    async def _note(self, source: Source, adapter: SourceAdapter, was: bool) -> bool:
        """Read `supports_push` and persist the transition.

        The read is the adapter's, which grounds it in received messages
        (ADR-0018). This layer neither knows nor guesses.
        """
        now_delivering = adapter.supports_push
        if now_delivering != was:
            await self._set_push_available(source, now_delivering)
        return now_delivering

    async def _gap(self, source: Source, adapter: SourceAdapter, gate: _Gate) -> None:
        """PRD 03's reconnect delta, rate-limited.

        A flapping socket plus one delta per reconnect is a paged walk of
        everything changed since the cursor every few seconds. The first
        after a real outage is the expensive one and is never skipped,
        because `gate.at` is `None` until one has run.
        """
        now = self._clock()
        if gate.at is not None and now - gate.at < self._gap_min_interval:
            logger.debug(
                "skipping {source}'s gap-closing delta; one ran {ago:.0f}s ago",
                source=source.name,
                ago=now - gate.at,
            )
            return
        gate.at = now
        await self._close_gap(source, adapter)

    def _backoff(self, failures: int) -> float:
        """Equal jitter, exactly PRD 08's shape for the job queue.

        A uniform draw from `[base/2, base) x 2^(failures-1)`, capped. Not
        *full* jitter, whose minimum draw is arbitrarily close to zero, so a
        share of failures retry effectively immediately -- the hot loop the
        backoff exists to prevent, rationed. The spread is what breaks a
        thundering herd across sources; the half-interval floor is what
        makes "a failed connection is not instantly retried" a property
        rather than a probability.
        """
        base = min(self._backoff_seconds * (2 ** (failures - 1)), self._max_backoff)
        return self._jitter(base / 2, base)
```

| Mutation | Expected failure |
|---|---|
| `failures = 0` moved to just after `adapter.events()` opens | `test_the_failure_counter_is_reset_by_delivery_not_by_connection` — **the milestone's rule at the lane level. If this survives, the lane will hold a buffering proxy forever.** |
| `_note` reads `True` unconditionally | Same case |
| `await self._gap(...)` moved before `async with adapter.events()` | `test_the_gap_is_closed_after_connecting_not_before` |
| Drop the `gate` rate limit | `test_gap_closing_is_rate_limited` |
| `base = self._backoff_seconds * failures` (linear) | `test_the_backoff_doubles_and_is_capped` |
| `self._jitter(0, base)` (full jitter) | `test_the_backoff_draws_from_the_upper_half_of_the_interval` |
| Drop the `except asyncio.CancelledError: raise` arm | `test_cancellation_stops_the_lane_without_marking_the_source` — `CancelledError` is a `BaseException` in 3.13 and would not be caught by the `UsherPortError` arm anyway, so this mutation is an **equivalent mutant** and the arm is documentation. Recorded rather than removed: the arm is what stops a later reader widening `except UsherPortError` to `except Exception` without noticing. |
| Drop the `else:` branch | Nothing in this file — **write the case.** `test_a_channel_that_ends_quietly_counts_as_a_failure`, with an adapter whose iterator returns. The port forbids it; an adapter can still do it, and the failure mode is a lane that returns silently. |

## Task 16: The two push metrics PRD 10 has been reserving

PRD 10's catalogue carries `usher.source.push.connected` (gauge, `source`) and `usher.source.push.reconnects` (counter, `source`) marked **M5**, and dashboard 3 plots "push connection uptime and reconnect count — the direct health signal for the WebSocket risk in ADR-0004". Its "Push down" alert fires on `push.connected == 0` for 15 minutes on a source that supports it.

**The gauge is observable and reads the ledger.** That is the one case in this project where the "an observable callback cannot query this database" rule does not bite: the value is an in-memory integer, so there is no coroutine to bounce onto an event loop and no exporter thread to block. It still follows `register_queue_gauges`' shape exactly — a module global replaced rather than a closure captured — because the SDK keeps only the **first** observable gauge registered under a name and silently discards the rest, so a second `create_app()` in the same process would otherwise leave the first, dead reader reporting forever.

**And the gauge reports `is_delivering`, not `connected`.** If it reported the socket, the alert PRD 10 wrote would be permanently green against exactly the failure ADR-0004 warns about.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/usher/telemetry.py`
- Modify: `src/usher/adapters/emby/push.py`
- Modify: `tests/unit/test_telemetry.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

```python
# tests/unit/test_telemetry.py  (append)


def test_the_push_gauge_reports_delivery_not_connection(meter_reader) -> None:
    """PRD 10's "Push down" alert fires on `push.connected == 0`. A gauge
    reporting the socket would be permanently green against the one failure
    ADR-0004 warns about -- a channel that upgraded and delivers nothing --
    which is the condition that alert exists to catch."""
    register_push_gauges(lambda: {"Living Room Emby": PushSnapshot(delivering=False, reconnects=2)})
    points = _gauge_points(meter_reader, "usher.source.push.connected")
    assert points == [(0, {"source": "Living Room Emby"})]


def test_the_push_gauge_reports_one_for_a_delivering_channel(meter_reader) -> None:
    register_push_gauges(lambda: {"Living Room Emby": PushSnapshot(delivering=True, reconnects=0)})
    assert _gauge_points(meter_reader, "usher.source.push.connected") == [
        (1, {"source": "Living Room Emby"})
    ]


def test_the_reconnect_gauge_reports_the_lanes_cumulative_count(meter_reader) -> None:
    """Cumulative for the adapter's whole life rather than per connection --
    `PushHealth` is one object across reconnects for exactly this. A
    per-connection counter would read 0 or 1 forever and dashboard 3's
    "reconnect count" panel would be a flat line."""
    register_push_gauges(lambda: {"A": PushSnapshot(delivering=True, reconnects=7)})
    assert _gauge_points(meter_reader, "usher.source.push.reconnects") == [(7, {"source": "A"})]


def test_registering_a_second_reader_replaces_the_first(meter_reader) -> None:
    """The SDK keeps only the *first* observable gauge registered under a
    name and silently discards the rest -- verified directly for
    `register_queue_gauges` and true here for the same reason. A
    re-registration that only created a second instrument would leave the
    first, now-dead reader reporting forever."""
    register_push_gauges(lambda: {"A": PushSnapshot(delivering=False, reconnects=0)})
    register_push_gauges(lambda: {"B": PushSnapshot(delivering=True, reconnects=1)})
    assert _gauge_points(meter_reader, "usher.source.push.connected") == [(1, {"source": "B"})]


def test_no_reader_reports_no_observation_rather_than_a_zero(meter_reader) -> None:
    """A fabricated zero is indistinguishable from a source whose channel is
    down, and PRD 10's alert fires on exactly that value. Same argument
    `_observations` already makes for the queue gauges."""
    assert _gauge_points(meter_reader, "usher.source.push.connected") == []
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the gauges**

```python
# src/usher/telemetry.py  (append)
@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class PushSnapshot:
    """One source's push lane, as PRD 10's two series see it.

    `delivering`, not `connected`: dashboard 3's panel is "push connection
    uptime" and its alert is `push.connected == 0` for 15 minutes, and a
    series fed by the socket's state would be permanently green against the
    failure ADR-0004 measured -- a channel that upgraded and delivers
    nothing. `usher.source.push.connected` keeps PRD 10's name (a metric
    renamed is a dashboard panel silently blank) and reports the honest
    quantity.

    `reconnects` is cumulative for the lane rather than per connection,
    which is what `PushHealth` being one object across reconnects buys.
    """

    delivering: bool
    reconnects: int


PushReader = Callable[[], Mapping[str, PushSnapshot]]

# A module global, replaced rather than captured -- the SDK keeps only the
# first observable gauge registered under a name and silently discards the
# rest, so a second `create_app()` in one process would otherwise leave the
# first, now-dead reader reporting forever. Same shape, same reason, as
# `_queue_reader` above.
_push_reader: PushReader | None = None


def register_push_gauges(read: PushReader) -> None:
    """PRD 10's `usher.source.push.connected` / `usher.source.push.reconnects`.

    Observable, and this is the one place in this project where that is
    unambiguously safe: the value is an in-memory integer on a `PushHealth`
    ledger, so there is no coroutine to bounce onto the event loop from the
    metric reader's background thread and no exporter thread to block on it.
    `register_queue_gauges` explains at length why the queue's equivalent
    cannot be live; nothing in that argument applies here, and the shape is
    kept identical anyway so a reader meets one pattern rather than two.
    """
    global _push_reader
    _push_reader = read
    meter = metrics.get_meter("usher.push")
    meter.create_observable_gauge(
        "usher.source.push.connected",
        callbacks=[_observe_push_connected],
        unit="1",
        description="1 when a source's push channel is delivering messages, 0 otherwise",
    )
    meter.create_observable_gauge(
        "usher.source.push.reconnects",
        callbacks=[_observe_push_reconnects],
        unit="1",
        description="Cumulative push reconnects for a source's lane",
    )


def _observe_push_connected(options: CallbackOptions) -> Iterable[Observation]:
    if _push_reader is None:
        return []
    return [
        Observation(1 if snapshot.delivering else 0, {"source": source})
        for source, snapshot in _push_reader().items()
    ]


def _observe_push_reconnects(options: CallbackOptions) -> Iterable[Observation]:
    if _push_reader is None:
        return []
    return [
        Observation(snapshot.reconnects, {"source": source})
        for source, snapshot in _push_reader().items()
    ]
```

And the reconnect count has to actually move. `PushHealth.record_open` is called once per connection, so:

```python
# src/usher/adapters/emby/push.py  (inside PushHealth.record_open)
    def record_open(self, *, now: float) -> None:
        if self.opened_at is not None:
            # The second and later opens are reconnects, which is the
            # quantity PRD 10's panel plots -- so this counts on *open*
            # rather than on failure. A lane that failed to connect five
            # times and then succeeded reconnected once; a counter on the
            # failure would report five.
            self.reconnects += 1
        self.connected = True
        self.opened_at = now
        self.last_message_at = None
```

In `docs/prd/10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md`, mark both rows ✅ M5 and add to the label-corrections list:

```markdown
- **`usher.source.push.connected` reports *delivery*, not connection.** A
  gauge fed by the socket's state would read 1 for the failure
  [ADR-0004](decisions/0004-push-over-polling.md) measured — a handshake
  against a nonexistent path, upgraded and held open, delivering nothing —
  which is precisely the condition the "Push down" alert exists to catch.
  The series keeps its name, because a metric renamed is a dashboard panel
  silently blank, and reports the honest quantity.
  [ADR-0018](decisions/0018-push-health-is-a-message-ledger.md).
- **Queue depth by priority (dashboard 3) is a Postgres query, not a
  metric.** M4 recorded that `usher.jobs.queued` is labelled `kind` and that
  "M5 introduces demand promotion and is where the band becomes a real
  series". M5 introduces demand promotion and the label stays `kind`: a
  priority band needs a second `GROUP BY` on `JobQueue`, and this document's
  own first principle puts "what is in the queue right now, broken down
  however you like" on the datasource that can answer it exactly. The panel
  reads `jobs` directly.
```

## Task 17: `EventPublisher` — a port with one implementation, and why

PRD 03's read-through loop ends "**the client is told when it changes.** Completion publishes a `title.updated` event on a Server-Sent Events channel; clients patch in place. No polling on either side of the system." PRD 07 gives that channel five event types, a `?titles=` filter, `Last-Event-ID` replay, and `resync_required` on overflow.

**It is a port, and it has exactly one implementation in M5.** That is a deliberate cost, because a port with one implementation is the shape ADR-0001 warns about spending effort on. Three things buy it back:

1. **`services/` may not import a bus.** `EnrichService`, `WatchStateSyncService` and `ReconcileService` all publish, and all three may depend only on `domain/` and `ports/` (ADR-0009). Without a port the bus would have to be a callable injected in three signatures with no shared contract — which is what the `SourceResolver` alias does one milestone down, and is the right shape for one caller and the wrong one for three plus a contract suite.
2. **The second implementation is already named.** M5's bus is in-memory and therefore in-process, which is why M5 also runs the job worker inside the server (Scope, boundary call 5). A deployment that split them needs a Postgres `LISTEN/NOTIFY` transport, and the whole point of a port is that it arrives with its own contract run and **no service change**. The contract below is written to be satisfiable by a lossy transport: every case is about *one subscriber's stream*, and `resync_required` is the answer to every gap.
3. **A no-op publisher is a real deployment.** `usher work` as a standalone process publishes nowhere, and that has to be expressible without a branch in every service.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/ports/events.py`
- Create: `tests/fakes/event_publisher.py`
- Create: `tests/contract/event_publisher_contract.py`
- Create: `tests/unit/test_ports_events.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```python
"""The client event channel's vocabulary."""

import uuid

import pytest

from usher.ports.events import ClientEvent, ClientEventKind, EventPublisher, NullEventPublisher


def test_every_event_kind_is_something_m5_emits() -> None:
    """PRD 10's rule for metrics, applied to a client contract: "A
    documented metric nothing emits is a dashboard panel that is
    permanently empty, and nothing distinguishes that from a healthy zero."
    An SSE event type nothing emits is worse -- a client writes a handler
    for it and waits forever. PRD 07 lists five; M5 ships four, and 09's
    roadmap says which milestone owes the fifth.
    """
    assert {kind.value for kind in ClientEventKind} == {
        "title.updated",
        "watchstate.updated",
        "sync.progress",
        "resync_required",
    }


def test_an_event_may_be_scoped_to_a_title_or_to_nothing() -> None:
    """PRD 07: "Subscriptions are scoped by query (`?titles=id1,id2`) so a
    detail screen isn't woken by unrelated churn." A `sync.progress` event
    belongs to no title and must reach only unfiltered subscribers."""
    title_id = uuid.uuid4()
    assert ClientEvent(kind=ClientEventKind.TITLE_UPDATED, title_id=title_id).title_id == title_id
    assert ClientEvent(kind=ClientEventKind.SYNC_PROGRESS, data={"seen": 1}).title_id is None

    """A client watching a series subscribes with the *series'* title id --
    it has no episode ids until it fetches the season. So an episode event
    carries both, and the filter matches on `title_id`."""
    title_id, episode_id = uuid.uuid4(), uuid.uuid4()
    event = ClientEvent(
        kind=ClientEventKind.WATCHSTATE_UPDATED, title_id=title_id, episode_id=episode_id
    )
    assert (event.title_id, event.episode_id) == (title_id, episode_id)

    implementation publishes with an `INSERT`/`NOTIFY` and subscribes with a
    dedicated connection whose lifecycle has nothing in common with an
    in-memory queue's -- putting both on one ABC would force the second
    implementation to satisfy a shape drawn around the first."""
    assert EventPublisher.__abstractmethods__ == frozenset({"publish"})
```
