```python
# src/usher/ports/events.py
"""The client event channel (PRD 07's SSE surface, PRD 03's read-through
loop), as a port.

**One method, and subscription is not on it.** A Postgres `LISTEN/NOTIFY`
implementation -- the named second one, for a deployment that splits the
worker from the server -- publishes with an `INSERT` plus a `NOTIFY` and
subscribes on a dedicated connection whose lifecycle has nothing in common
with an in-memory queue's. Putting both on one ABC would draw the shape
around the first implementation and make the second satisfy it.
`usher.services.events.InMemoryEventBus` therefore implements this *and*
offers `subscribe`, which is its own concern rather than the port's.

**`publish` is `async` and the shipped implementation never suspends in
it.** That is not a contradiction, it is the contract: the port is async
because a transport can be, and the guarantee every implementation owes is
that a subscriber which has stopped reading cannot slow, block, or fail the
service that published. `EnrichService` finishing a title must not depend on
a browser tab.
"""

import uuid
from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
from collections.abc import Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from enum import StrEnum
from typing import Any


class ClientEventKind(StrEnum):
    """PRD 07's SSE table, restricted to what M5 emits.

    `row.invalidated` is absent because nothing composes a row until M7, and
    `bootstrap.progress` because bootstrap runs in the CLI process and M5's
    bus is in-process (see the M5 plan's Scope, boundary call 5). PRD 10's
    argument for keeping its metric catalogue honest applies harder here: an
    empty dashboard panel is a puzzle, and an SSE event type nothing emits
    is a client handler that waits forever.
    """

    TITLE_UPDATED = "title.updated"
    WATCHSTATE_UPDATED = "watchstate.updated"
    SYNC_PROGRESS = "sync.progress"
    # Not a domain event: the channel telling a client its own stream has a
    # hole in it. PRD 07: "On buffer overflow the server emits
    # `resync_required` rather than silently skipping events -- a client
    # that missed changes is told to refetch instead of being left quietly
    # stale."
    RESYNC_REQUIRED = "resync_required"


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class ClientEvent:
    """One thing worth telling a client.

    `title_id` is the **filter key**, and an episode event carries its
    series' title alongside its own episode id for exactly that reason: a
    client watching a series subscribes with the series' title id, because
    that is the only id it has before it fetches a season. A filter keyed on
    `episode_id` would wake nobody.

    `data` is the SSE payload and is deliberately untyped at this layer --
    `api/dto/events.py` owns the wire shape, and the port owning it too
    would make every payload change a port change. Everything in it must be
    JSON-serialisable, and nothing in it may be a credential: PRD 08's rule
    reaches a response body exactly as it reaches a log line.
    """

    kind: ClientEventKind
    title_id: uuid.UUID | None = None
    episode_id: uuid.UUID | None = None
    data: Mapping[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)


class EventPublisher(ABC):
    @abstractmethod
    async def publish(self, event: ClientEvent) -> None:
        """Offer an event to whoever is listening.

        **Never raises, and never blocks on a subscriber.** Both halves are
        contract rather than courtesy: this is called from
        `EnrichService.enrich`, from the push lane, and from a reconcile's
        per-batch flush, and none of those may fail or stall because a
        client stopped reading. An implementation that cannot deliver drops
        or diverts, and tells that subscriber (`RESYNC_REQUIRED`) rather
        than telling the publisher.

        Delivery is best-effort and unordered *across* subscribers. Within
        one subscriber's stream, order is preserved and a gap is announced.
        That is the whole guarantee, and it is drawn to be satisfiable by a
        lossy transport rather than by an in-process queue.
        """


class NullEventPublisher(EventPublisher):
    """Publishes nowhere.

    A real deployment rather than a test double: `usher work` as a
    standalone process has no SSE clients to tell, and M5's bus is
    in-process. Without this, every service would need a `publisher is not
    None` branch, which is three places to forget it.
    """

    async def publish(self, event: ClientEvent) -> None:
        return None
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the fake and the contract skeleton**

```python
# tests/fakes/event_publisher.py
"""A recording `EventPublisher`.

**Where this is more forgiving than the real bus, and it is nearly
everything.** It records and never fans out, so no queue, no bound, no
overflow, no ordering across subscribers, no replay and no cancellation is
exercised by it at all -- it is a spy for services, not a bus. It also never
blocks, which means a service tested only against this would pass even if
the real bus stalled its caller. `InMemoryEventBus` runs the identical
`EventPublisherContract`, and the non-blocking case there is asserted on
measured intervals rather than on a completion.
"""

from usher.ports.events import ClientEvent, EventPublisher


class FakeEventPublisher(EventPublisher):
    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.published: list[ClientEvent] = []

    async def publish(self, event: ClientEvent) -> None:
        self.published.append(event)
```

```python
# tests/contract/event_publisher_contract.py
"""What every `EventPublisher` must guarantee to the services that call it.

Subclass and provide a `publisher` fixture:

    class TestFakeEventPublisher(EventPublisherContract):
        @pytest.fixture
        def publisher(self) -> EventPublisher:
            return FakeEventPublisher()

The subscriber-facing cases live in `EventBusContract` below, which
`InMemoryEventBus` runs and `FakeEventPublisher` does not -- a spy has no
subscribers, and pretending otherwise would ratify a bus that never
delivered.
"""

import asyncio

import pytest

from usher.ports.events import ClientEvent, ClientEventKind, EventPublisher


class EventPublisherContract:
    async def test_publish_accepts_an_event_with_no_subscribers(
        self, publisher: EventPublisher
    ) -> None:
        """The normal state of a household's server. `EnrichService`
        finishing a title at 04:00 must not care that nobody is watching."""
        await publisher.publish(ClientEvent(kind=ClientEventKind.TITLE_UPDATED))

    async def test_publish_never_raises_for_a_subscriber_that_cannot_keep_up(
        self, publisher: EventPublisher
    ) -> None:
        """A browser tab that stopped reading must not fail an enrichment.
        Nothing here can *arrange* a slow subscriber through the port -- the
        port has no subscribe -- so this asserts the weaker, universally
        checkable half: a burst larger than any plausible buffer completes.
        `EventBusContract` asserts the real thing."""
        for index in range(1_000):
            await publisher.publish(
                ClientEvent(kind=ClientEventKind.SYNC_PROGRESS, data={"seen": index})
            )

    async def test_publish_is_not_a_suspension_point_a_caller_can_be_starved_on(
        self, publisher: EventPublisher
    ) -> None:
        """Measured, not asserted from a completion. A serialised run
        completes too. This gathers a publisher against a task that only
        sets a flag and asserts the publish finished within one event-loop
        turn of starting -- which is what "never blocks" means operationally
        and what a bus with an unbounded `await queue.put()` would fail.
        """
        started = asyncio.get_running_loop().time()
        await publisher.publish(ClientEvent(kind=ClientEventKind.TITLE_UPDATED))
        assert asyncio.get_running_loop().time() - started < 0.05
```

```python
from tests.fakes.event_publisher import FakeEventPublisher
from usher.ports.events import EventPublisher, NullEventPublisher

import pytest


class TestFakeEventPublisher(EventPublisherContract):
    @pytest.fixture
    def publisher(self) -> EventPublisher:
        return FakeEventPublisher()


class TestNullEventPublisher(EventPublisherContract):
    @pytest.fixture
    def publisher(self) -> EventPublisher:
        return NullEventPublisher()
```

## Task 18: `InMemoryEventBus` — a slow subscriber never blocks a publisher

The single property this whole component exists to have, and the single property a serialised test cannot see.

**The shape.** One bounded `asyncio.Queue` per subscriber. `publish` walks the subscribers and calls a **synchronous** `offer` on each, which is `put_nowait` and a branch. Nothing in the publish path awaits a subscriber. That is why `EnrichService` finishing a title cannot be slowed by a browser tab, and it is why an unbounded `await queue.put(...)` — the obvious spelling — is the bug this task's central case is written to catch.

**Measuring it honestly.** "The publish completed and the slow subscriber got its event" is satisfied by a fully serialised run. The assertion here is on *intervals*: a subscriber is parked mid-`__anext__` for a recorded window, and the publisher's own window must sit inside it. That is `JobQueueContract.overlapping()`'s shape, reused because it is the only one that distinguishes concurrency from a count.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/services/events.py`
- Modify: `tests/contract/event_publisher_contract.py`
- Create: `tests/unit/test_services_events.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

```python
# tests/unit/test_services_events.py
"""The in-memory client event bus."""

import asyncio
import uuid

import pytest

from usher.ports.events import ClientEvent, ClientEventKind
from usher.services.events import InMemoryEventBus


def _event(index: int, title_id: uuid.UUID | None = None) -> ClientEvent:
    return ClientEvent(
        kind=ClientEventKind.SYNC_PROGRESS, title_id=title_id, data={"seen": index}
    )


async def test_a_subscriber_receives_what_is_published_after_it_subscribed() -> None:
    bus = InMemoryEventBus()
    async with bus.subscribe() as stream:
        await bus.publish(_event(1))
        sent = await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(stream)), timeout=1.0)
    assert sent.event.data == {"seen": 1}
    assert sent.id == 1


async def test_two_subscribers_both_receive_it() -> None:
    bus = InMemoryEventBus()
    async with bus.subscribe() as first, bus.subscribe() as second:
        await bus.publish(_event(1))
        one = await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(first)), timeout=1.0)
        two = await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(second)), timeout=1.0)
    assert one.event.data == two.event.data == {"seen": 1}


async def test_a_subscriber_scoped_to_titles_is_not_woken_by_unrelated_churn() -> None:
    """PRD 07: "Subscriptions are scoped by query (`?titles=id1,id2`) so a
    detail screen isn't woken by unrelated churn." A nightly walk publishes
    a `sync.progress` per batch -- 1,127 of them against the one measured
    library -- and a detail screen that re-rendered on each is the reason
    this filter exists rather than being a nicety."""
    wanted, other = uuid.uuid4(), uuid.uuid4()
    bus = InMemoryEventBus()
    async with bus.subscribe(titles=frozenset({wanted})) as stream:
        await bus.publish(_event(1, title_id=other))
        await bus.publish(_event(2))  # unscoped: a sync.progress
        await bus.publish(_event(3, title_id=wanted))
        sent = await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(stream)), timeout=1.0)
    assert sent.event.data == {"seen": 3}


async def test_an_unfiltered_subscriber_receives_everything() -> None:
    """An admin UI wants `sync.progress`, which belongs to no title. The
    filter is opt-in, and its absence means "everything" rather than
    "nothing"."""
    bus = InMemoryEventBus()
    async with bus.subscribe() as stream:
        await bus.publish(_event(1, title_id=uuid.uuid4()))
        await bus.publish(_event(2))
        first = await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(stream)), timeout=1.0)
        second = await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(stream)), timeout=1.0)
    assert [first.event.data["seen"], second.event.data["seen"]] == [1, 2]


async def test_an_episode_event_reaches_a_subscriber_scoped_to_its_series() -> None:
    """A client watching a series has the series' title id and no episode
    ids until it fetches a season, so the filter matches on `title_id` and
    an episode event carries both."""
    series = uuid.uuid4()
    bus = InMemoryEventBus()
    async with bus.subscribe(titles=frozenset({series})) as stream:
        await bus.publish(
            ClientEvent(
                kind=ClientEventKind.WATCHSTATE_UPDATED,
                title_id=series,
                episode_id=uuid.uuid4(),
                data={"played": True},
            )
        )
        sent = await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(stream)), timeout=1.0)
    assert sent.event.episode_id is not None


async def test_publishing_does_not_block_on_a_subscriber_that_is_not_reading() -> None:
    """**The property this component exists for, measured rather than
    inferred.** "The publish completed" is also what a fully serialised run
    produces. This records the window during which a subscriber is parked
    inside `__anext__` and asserts the publisher's own window sits inside
    it -- the same shape `JobQueueContract.overlapping()` uses, and the only
    one that tells concurrency from a count.

    The bug it rules out is one line: `await subscriber.queue.put(event)`
    instead of `put_nowait`. With it, an enrichment completing at 04:00
    hangs until a browser tab that closed hours ago is garbage collected.
    """
    bus = InMemoryEventBus(queue_size=2)
    parked_from: float | None = None
    parked_until: float | None = None
    released = asyncio.Event()

    async def slow_reader() -> None:
        nonlocal parked_from, parked_until
        async with bus.subscribe() as stream:
            iterator = aiter(stream)
            await anext(iterator)  # drain the first, then stop reading
            parked_from = asyncio.get_running_loop().time()
            await released.wait()
            parked_until = asyncio.get_running_loop().time()

    reader = asyncio.create_task(slow_reader())
    await bus.publish(_event(0))
    # Let the reader take its one event and park.
    for _ in range(10):
        await asyncio.sleep(0)
        if parked_from is not None:
            break
    assert parked_from is not None, "the reader never parked; the test measured nothing"

    publish_from = asyncio.get_running_loop().time()
    for index in range(1, 20):
        await bus.publish(_event(index))
    publish_until = asyncio.get_running_loop().time()

    released.set()
    await asyncio.wait_for(reader, timeout=1.0)
    assert parked_until is not None
    assert parked_from <= publish_from and publish_until <= parked_until, (
        f"publish ran {publish_from}-{publish_until}, outside the reader's parked window "
        f"{parked_from}-{parked_until}: the two never overlapped, so this case proves nothing"
    )


async def test_a_cancelled_subscriber_is_removed() -> None:
    """An SSE client disconnecting is the common case, not the exception --
    a browser tab close, a phone locking, a proxy timing out. A bus that
    kept the queue would grow one per connection for the life of the
    process."""
    bus = InMemoryEventBus()
    async with bus.subscribe():
        assert bus.subscribers == 1
    assert bus.subscribers == 0


async def test_a_subscriber_removed_by_an_exception_is_still_removed() -> None:
    bus = InMemoryEventBus()
    with pytest.raises(ZeroDivisionError):
        async with bus.subscribe():
            raise ZeroDivisionError
    assert bus.subscribers == 0
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the bus**

```python
# src/usher/services/events.py
"""The in-process client event bus (PRD 07's SSE channel).

**One rule, and everything here is shaped by it: a subscriber that stopped
reading may not slow, block, or fail the service that published.**
`EnrichService.enrich` publishes `title.updated` at the end of a title's
enrichment, `PushApplyService` publishes on every merged watch state, and a
reconcile publishes once per batch -- 1,127 times against the one measured
library. None of those may await a browser tab.

So `publish` never awaits a subscriber. It walks them and calls a
synchronous `offer`, which is a `put_nowait` and a branch. The `async def`
is the port's, because a `LISTEN/NOTIFY` transport would genuinely suspend;
this implementation does not, and that is the property, not an accident.

**A full queue is answered, not dropped.** PRD 07: "On buffer overflow the
server emits `resync_required` rather than silently skipping events -- a
client that missed changes is told to refetch instead of being left quietly
stale." A subscriber that overflows has its queue emptied and one
`resync_required` put in its place, which is both the smallest possible
state and the honest one.

**Ids carry an epoch.** The replay ring is in-memory, so ids restart at 1
every time the process does -- and a client reconnecting with
`Last-Event-ID: 40` would be replayed events 41+ *of a different sequence*.
The id a client sees is `<epoch>-<n>` where the epoch is minted per bus, so
a mismatch is detectable and answers `resync_required`.
"""

import asyncio
import secrets
import uuid
from collections import deque
from collections.abc import AsyncIterator, Iterator
from contextlib import asynccontextmanager, contextmanager
from dataclasses import dataclass

from loguru import logger

from usher.ports.events import ClientEvent, ClientEventKind, EventPublisher

DEFAULT_BUFFER_SIZE = 256
DEFAULT_QUEUE_SIZE = 64


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class SentEvent:
    """An event with the id a client sees.

    `id` is monotonic within one bus and `epoch` distinguishes one bus's
    sequence from another's -- the wire form is `f"{epoch}-{id}"`, assembled
    in `api/dto/events.py` rather than here, because the separator is a wire
    decision.
    """

    id: int
    epoch: str
    event: ClientEvent


class _Subscriber:
    __slots__ = ("queue", "titles", "overflowed")

    def __init__(self, titles: frozenset[uuid.UUID] | None, queue_size: int) -> None:
        self.queue: asyncio.Queue[SentEvent] = asyncio.Queue(maxsize=queue_size)
        self.titles = titles
        self.overflowed = False

    def wants(self, event: ClientEvent) -> bool:
        """An unfiltered subscriber wants everything; a filtered one wants
        events for its titles and nothing else.

        Matching on `title_id` and never on `episode_id`: a client watching
        a series subscribes with the series' title, because that is the only
        id it holds before it fetches a season.
        """
        if self.titles is None:
            return True
        return event.title_id is not None and event.title_id in self.titles

    def offer(self, sent: SentEvent, *, epoch: str, next_id: int) -> None:
        """Non-blocking. **This is the whole design.**

        `put_nowait` and a branch, never `await put`. The awaiting spelling
        is one character shorter and makes an enrichment completing at 04:00
        hang until a browser tab that closed hours ago is garbage collected.
        """
        if self.overflowed:
            # Already told to resync; further events would be replaced by
            # the refetch anyway, and queueing them would re-fill the queue
            # the moment the client reads the resync.
            return
        try:
            self.queue.put_nowait(sent)
        except asyncio.QueueFull:
            self.overflowed = True
            while not self.queue.empty():
                self.queue.get_nowait()
            self.queue.put_nowait(
                SentEvent(
                    id=next_id,
                    epoch=epoch,
                    event=ClientEvent(
                        kind=ClientEventKind.RESYNC_REQUIRED,
                        data={"reason": "buffer_overflow"},
                    ),
                )
            )
            logger.warning("an SSE subscriber overflowed its buffer and was told to resync")


class InMemoryEventBus(EventPublisher):
    def __init__(
        self, *, buffer_size: int = DEFAULT_BUFFER_SIZE, queue_size: int = DEFAULT_QUEUE_SIZE
    ) -> None:
        self._buffer: deque[SentEvent] = deque(maxlen=buffer_size)
        self._subscribers: set[_Subscriber] = set()
        self._queue_size = queue_size
        self._next_id = 0
        # 8 hex characters: enough that two processes on one host do not
        # collide in practice, short enough that the SSE `id:` line stays
        # readable in a `curl` session. Not a secret -- a client sends it
        # back verbatim -- so `token_hex` is used for uniformity rather than
        # for unguessability.
        self._epoch = secrets.token_hex(4)

    @property
    def subscribers(self) -> int:
        """For PRD 10's `usher.sse.connections`. An in-memory integer, which
        is the one case where an observable OTel callback really can read
        live state -- see `usher.telemetry.register_queue_gauges` for why
        the queue's equivalent cannot."""
        return len(self._subscribers)

    @property
    def epoch(self) -> str:
        return self._epoch

    async def publish(self, event: ClientEvent) -> None:
        self._next_id += 1
        sent = SentEvent(id=self._next_id, epoch=self._epoch, event=event)
        self._buffer.append(sent)
        # `list(...)` because `offer` cannot mutate the set but a subscriber
        # unsubscribing concurrently can -- and iterating a set being
        # mutated raises. Nothing here awaits, so this is the only
        # concurrency this method has.
        for subscriber in list(self._subscribers):
            if subscriber.wants(event):
                subscriber.offer(sent, epoch=self._epoch, next_id=self._next_id)

    @asynccontextmanager
    async def subscribe(
        self, *, titles: frozenset[uuid.UUID] | None = None, last_event_id: str | None = None
    ) -> AsyncIterator[AsyncIterator[SentEvent]]:
        """One client's stream, unsubscribed however the block ends.

        A context manager rather than a bare iterator, because the removal
        has to happen on cancellation -- and an SSE client disconnecting is
        the common case, not the exception. A bus that leaked one queue per
        connection would grow for the life of the process.
        """
        subscriber = _Subscriber(titles, self._queue_size)
        self._subscribers.add(subscriber)
        try:
            yield self._stream(subscriber, last_event_id)
        finally:
            self._subscribers.discard(subscriber)

    async def _stream(
        self, subscriber: _Subscriber, last_event_id: str | None
    ) -> AsyncIterator[SentEvent]:
        for sent in self._replay(subscriber, last_event_id):
            yield sent
        while True:
            yield await subscriber.queue.get()
```

`_replay` is Task 19's; write it as `def _replay(self, subscriber, last_event_id) -> Iterator[SentEvent]: return iter(())` here so this task's cases pass, and fill it there. Named and stubbed rather than absent, because the `_stream` shape is what Task 19 hangs off.

## Task 19: Overflow and a stale `Last-Event-ID` both answer `resync_required`

PRD 07 gives two guarantees about a client's stream: **"Reconnect sends `Last-Event-ID`; the server replays from a bounded buffer"**, and **"On buffer overflow the server emits `resync_required` rather than silently skipping events — a client that missed changes is told to refetch instead of being left quietly stale."**

Three ways a stream can have a hole, and all three answer the same way:

| Hole | How it happens | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| The subscriber's queue filled | A client stopped reading while a walk published 1,127 `sync.progress` events | `resync_required`, queue emptied |
| `Last-Event-ID` is older than the ring | The client was offline longer than `sse_buffer_size` events | `resync_required` |
| `Last-Event-ID` is from a different epoch | The server restarted; ids restarted at 1 | `resync_required` |

The third is the one that is easy to miss and impossible to detect without the epoch. A bus whose ids restart at 1 replaying "everything after 40" to a client that saw 40 events of a *previous* sequence delivers a plausible-looking stream that is silently wrong.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/usher/services/events.py`
- Modify: `tests/unit/test_services_events.py`
- Modify: `tests/contract/event_publisher_contract.py`

```python
# tests/unit/test_services_events.py  (append)


async def test_a_subscriber_that_overflows_is_told_to_resync() -> None:
    """PRD 07's exact requirement. Dropping events silently leaves a client
    confidently stale, which is worse than telling it to refetch: it has no
    way to find out."""
    bus = InMemoryEventBus(queue_size=3)
    async with bus.subscribe() as stream:
        for index in range(10):
            await bus.publish(_event(index))
        sent = await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(stream)), timeout=1.0)
    assert sent.event.kind is ClientEventKind.RESYNC_REQUIRED
    assert sent.event.data == {"reason": "buffer_overflow"}


async def test_an_overflowed_subscriber_gets_exactly_one_resync() -> None:
    """A queue that re-filled behind the resync would hand the client a
    second one the moment it read the first, forever."""
    bus = InMemoryEventBus(queue_size=2)
    async with bus.subscribe() as stream:
        for index in range(50):
            await bus.publish(_event(index))
        first = await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(stream)), timeout=1.0)
        with pytest.raises(TimeoutError):
            await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(stream)), timeout=0.05)
    assert first.event.kind is ClientEventKind.RESYNC_REQUIRED


async def test_replay_resumes_after_the_last_event_the_client_saw() -> None:
    """The reconnect PRD 07 designed for. Without it a client that dropped
    its connection for two seconds during a walk loses whatever landed in
    them, with nothing to say so."""
    bus = InMemoryEventBus()
    await bus.publish(_event(1))
    await bus.publish(_event(2))
    await bus.publish(_event(3))
    async with bus.subscribe(last_event_id=f"{bus.epoch}-1") as stream:
        iterator = aiter(stream)
        second = await asyncio.wait_for(anext(iterator), timeout=1.0)
        third = await asyncio.wait_for(anext(iterator), timeout=1.0)
    assert [second.event.data["seen"], third.event.data["seen"]] == [2, 3]


async def test_replay_honours_the_subscribers_title_filter() -> None:
    wanted = uuid.uuid4()
    bus = InMemoryEventBus()
    await bus.publish(_event(1, title_id=uuid.uuid4()))
    await bus.publish(_event(2, title_id=wanted))
    async with bus.subscribe(titles=frozenset({wanted}), last_event_id=f"{bus.epoch}-0") as stream:
        sent = await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(stream)), timeout=1.0)
    assert sent.event.data == {"seen": 2}


async def test_a_last_event_id_older_than_the_buffer_is_told_to_resync() -> None:
    """Replaying whatever is still in the ring and calling it a resume is
    the failure: the client silently misses the events that fell off the
    front and has no way to learn it."""
    bus = InMemoryEventBus(buffer_size=3)
    for index in range(10):
        await bus.publish(_event(index))
    async with bus.subscribe(last_event_id=f"{bus.epoch}-1") as stream:
        sent = await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(stream)), timeout=1.0)
    assert sent.event.kind is ClientEventKind.RESYNC_REQUIRED
    assert sent.event.data == {"reason": "buffer_expired"}


async def test_a_last_event_id_from_a_previous_process_is_told_to_resync() -> None:
    """**The one that is impossible without the epoch.** The ring is
    in-memory, so ids restart at 1 with the process. A client reconnecting
    with `Last-Event-ID: 40` after a restart would be replayed events 41+ of
    a completely different sequence -- a plausible-looking stream that is
    silently wrong."""
    bus = InMemoryEventBus()
    await bus.publish(_event(1))
    async with bus.subscribe(last_event_id="deadbeef-40") as stream:
        sent = await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(stream)), timeout=1.0)
    assert sent.event.kind is ClientEventKind.RESYNC_REQUIRED
    assert sent.event.data == {"reason": "unknown_epoch"}


async def test_an_unparseable_last_event_id_is_told_to_resync() -> None:
    """A client sends back whatever it last saw, and a proxy or a bad client
    can mangle it. Raising would answer a reconnect with a 500."""
    bus = InMemoryEventBus()
    async with bus.subscribe(last_event_id="not-an-id") as stream:
        sent = await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(stream)), timeout=1.0)
    assert sent.event.kind is ClientEventKind.RESYNC_REQUIRED


async def test_a_last_event_id_at_the_head_replays_nothing_and_waits() -> None:
    bus = InMemoryEventBus()
    await bus.publish(_event(1))
    async with bus.subscribe(last_event_id=f"{bus.epoch}-1") as stream:
        with pytest.raises(TimeoutError):
            await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(stream)), timeout=0.05)
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the replay**

```python
# src/usher/services/events.py  (replacing the stub)
    def _replay(
        self, subscriber: _Subscriber, last_event_id: str | None
    ) -> Iterator[SentEvent]:
        """Everything after `last_event_id`, or one `resync_required`.

        Three holes, one answer, and the third is invisible without the
        epoch: a ring that restarted with the process would replay
        "everything after 40" to a client that saw forty events of a
        *different* sequence, which is a plausible stream that is silently
        wrong.

        `resync_required` is minted with the current `_next_id` rather than
        with a new one: it is a statement about the stream rather than an
        event in it, and advancing the sequence for it would make a client
        that reconnects immediately afterwards ask for an id nothing ever
        published.
        """
        if last_event_id is None:
            return
        parsed = _parse_event_id(last_event_id)
        if parsed is None:
            yield self._resync("malformed_last_event_id")
            return
        epoch, seen = parsed
        if epoch != self._epoch:
            yield self._resync("unknown_epoch")
            return
        if self._buffer and self._buffer[0].id > seen + 1:
            # The oldest thing still held is *after* the next one the client
            # needs, so the gap is unrecoverable. Replaying what is left and
            # calling it a resume is the failure this branch exists for.
            yield self._resync("buffer_expired")
            return
        for sent in list(self._buffer):
            if sent.id > seen and subscriber.wants(sent.event):
                yield sent

    def _resync(self, reason: str) -> SentEvent:
        return SentEvent(
            id=self._next_id,
            epoch=self._epoch,
            event=ClientEvent(kind=ClientEventKind.RESYNC_REQUIRED, data={"reason": reason}),
        )


def _parse_event_id(raw: str) -> tuple[str, int] | None:
    """`"<epoch>-<n>"` into its parts, or `None`.

    A client sends back whatever it last saw and a proxy can mangle it, so
    this returns rather than raising: answering a reconnect with a 500 is
    the one response worse than answering it with `resync_required`.
    """
    epoch, separator, number = raw.rpartition("-")
    if not separator or not epoch:
        return None
    try:
        return epoch, int(number)
    except ValueError:
        return None
```

and the contract gains the subscriber-facing cases, in a second class so `FakeEventPublisher` does not have to satisfy them:

```python
# tests/contract/event_publisher_contract.py  (append)
class EventBusContract:
    """What an `EventPublisher` that *also* offers subscription must
    guarantee to one client's stream.

    Separate from `EventPublisherContract` because `FakeEventPublisher` has
    no subscribers, and a suite it "passed" by having nothing to check would
    ratify a bus that never delivered. Subclass and provide a `bus` fixture.

    Every case here is about a **single** subscriber's stream, deliberately:
    that is the guarantee a Postgres `LISTEN/NOTIFY` transport could also
    make, and a contract drawn around an in-process queue's ordering across
    subscribers would be a contract only this implementation can satisfy.
    """

    async def test_a_subscriber_that_overflows_is_told_to_resync(self, bus) -> None: ...
    async def test_replay_resumes_after_the_last_event_the_client_saw(self, bus) -> None: ...
    async def test_a_last_event_id_older_than_the_buffer_is_told_to_resync(self, bus) -> None: ...
    async def test_a_last_event_id_from_a_previous_process_is_told_to_resync(self, bus) -> None: ...
```

## Task 20: `GET /events`

PRD 07's streaming surface, and the first of M5's two routes. Four things about it are decisions rather than transcription.

**SSE, not WebSocket, and PRD 07 already argued it:** "the channel is server→client only, it survives proxies that mangle upgrades, and it reconnects natively in browsers". The third is the one that pays: a browser's `EventSource` retries and resends `Last-Event-ID` with no client code at all, which is why Task 19 exists.

**A heartbeat comment every `sse_heartbeat_seconds`.** The same reason the WebSocket lane needs one: nginx closes an idle connection at 60 s and Cloudflare at ~100 s, and an SSE stream on an idle library sends nothing for hours. `: keepalive\n\n` is a comment line the spec requires clients to ignore, so it costs a client nothing and keeps a proxy from closing the connection.

**No `response_model`.** A `StreamingResponse` is bytes, and FastAPI's serializer never sees it. The wire shape is owned by `api/dto/events.py` and asserted there, which is the same split `api/dto/` already makes — and `SseEventKind` is on the wire, versioned independently of `ClientEventKind`, so an internal rename is not a client break.

**A malformed `?titles=` is a 422 in M3's shape.** `{"detail": "..."}`, not an RFC 9457 problem document — see "Does a streaming surface force the error envelope?" above.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/api/dto/events.py`
- Create: `src/usher/api/routers/events.py`
- Modify: `src/usher/api/deps.py`, `src/usher/api/app.py`, `src/usher/telemetry.py`
- Create: `tests/unit/test_api_dto_events.py`, `tests/unit/test_api_events.py`

```python
# tests/unit/test_api_dto_events.py
"""The SSE wire format."""

import json
import uuid

from usher.api.dto.events import encode_sse
from usher.ports.events import ClientEvent, ClientEventKind
from usher.services.events import SentEvent


def _sent(event: ClientEvent, *, id: int = 7, epoch: str = "abcd1234") -> SentEvent:
    return SentEvent(id=id, epoch=epoch, event=event)


def test_a_frame_carries_an_id_an_event_and_a_json_data_line() -> None:
    """The three fields an `EventSource` reads. `id:` is what comes back as
    `Last-Event-ID`, so the epoch has to be in it -- Task 19's whole
    unknown-epoch branch is unreachable otherwise."""
    frame = encode_sse(
        _sent(ClientEvent(kind=ClientEventKind.TITLE_UPDATED, data={"fields": ["overview"]}))
    )
    lines = frame.split("\n")
    assert lines[0] == "id: abcd1234-7"
    assert lines[1] == "event: title.updated"
    assert json.loads(lines[2].removeprefix("data: ")) == {"fields": ["overview"]}
    assert frame.endswith("\n\n"), "an SSE frame is terminated by a blank line"


def test_a_title_id_is_on_the_data_line_not_only_in_the_filter() -> None:
    """A client subscribed to three titles has to know which one changed.
    The `?titles=` filter decides *whether* a frame is sent, not what it
    says."""
    title_id = uuid.uuid4()
    frame = encode_sse(_sent(ClientEvent(kind=ClientEventKind.TITLE_UPDATED, title_id=title_id)))
    payload = json.loads(frame.split("\n")[2].removeprefix("data: "))
    assert payload["title_id"] == str(title_id)
    assert "episode_id" not in payload


def test_an_episode_event_carries_both_ids() -> None:
    title_id, episode_id = uuid.uuid4(), uuid.uuid4()
    frame = encode_sse(
        _sent(
            ClientEvent(
                kind=ClientEventKind.WATCHSTATE_UPDATED,
                title_id=title_id,
                episode_id=episode_id,
                data={"played": True},
            )
        )
    )
    payload = json.loads(frame.split("\n")[2].removeprefix("data: "))
    assert payload == {"played": True, "title_id": str(title_id), "episode_id": str(episode_id)}


def test_a_data_payload_never_spans_two_lines() -> None:
    """A newline inside `data:` is a second `data:` line to an SSE parser,
    and an unescaped one silently truncates the frame. `json.dumps` escapes
    them; this is the case that stops somebody replacing it with an f-string.
    """
    frame = encode_sse(
        _sent(ClientEvent(kind=ClientEventKind.SYNC_PROGRESS, data={"error": "line one\nline two"}))
    )
    assert len([line for line in frame.split("\n") if line.startswith("data: ")]) == 1


def test_the_wire_vocabulary_is_its_own_enum() -> None:
    """`api/dto/` types are distinct from domain models (PRD 07: "Internal
    refactors don't break clients; wire changes are deliberate"). This is
    the assertion that makes renaming `ClientEventKind.TITLE_UPDATED` a
    compile-time decision rather than a silent wire break."""
    from usher.api.dto.events import SseEventKind

    assert {kind.value for kind in SseEventKind} == {
        "title.updated",
        "watchstate.updated",
        "sync.progress",
        "resync_required",
    }
```

```python
# tests/unit/test_api_events.py
"""`GET /events`."""

import asyncio
import uuid

import httpx
import pytest
from asgi_lifespan import LifespanManager

from usher.ports.events import ClientEvent, ClientEventKind


async def test_the_response_is_an_event_stream(client) -> None:
    async with client.stream("GET", "/events") as response:
        assert response.status_code == 200
        assert response.headers["content-type"].startswith("text/event-stream")
        # Two headers a proxy reads, and both matter in this deployment:
        # nginx buffers a response body by default, which holds every event
        # until the buffer fills, and an intermediary that cached this once
        # would serve a dead stream forever.
        assert response.headers["cache-control"] == "no-cache"
        assert response.headers["x-accel-buffering"] == "no"


async def test_a_published_event_reaches_a_connected_client(client, bus) -> None:
    async with client.stream("GET", "/events") as response:
        lines = aiter(response.aiter_lines())
        await bus.publish(ClientEvent(kind=ClientEventKind.TITLE_UPDATED, data={"fields": ["x"]}))
        frame = await asyncio.wait_for(_read_frame(lines), timeout=2.0)
    assert "event: title.updated" in frame


async def test_a_titles_filter_scopes_the_stream(client, bus) -> None:
    wanted, other = uuid.uuid4(), uuid.uuid4()
    async with client.stream("GET", f"/events?titles={wanted}") as response:
        lines = aiter(response.aiter_lines())
        await bus.publish(ClientEvent(kind=ClientEventKind.TITLE_UPDATED, title_id=other))
        await bus.publish(ClientEvent(kind=ClientEventKind.TITLE_UPDATED, title_id=wanted))
        frame = await asyncio.wait_for(_read_frame(lines), timeout=2.0)
    assert str(wanted) in frame
    assert str(other) not in frame


async def test_two_titles_are_comma_separated(client, bus) -> None:
    first, second = uuid.uuid4(), uuid.uuid4()
    async with client.stream("GET", f"/events?titles={first},{second}") as response:
        lines = aiter(response.aiter_lines())
        await bus.publish(ClientEvent(kind=ClientEventKind.TITLE_UPDATED, title_id=second))
        frame = await asyncio.wait_for(_read_frame(lines), timeout=2.0)
    assert str(second) in frame


async def test_a_malformed_titles_filter_is_a_422_that_does_not_echo_it(client) -> None:
    """M3's shipped error shape, not PRD 07's RFC 9457 envelope -- see the
    plan's "Does a streaming surface force the error envelope?". And the
    detail names the *rule* rather than the submitted value: `usher.api.
    errors` strips `input` from every validation error app-wide because a
    422 must never echo what it rejected."""
    response = await client.get("/events?titles=not-a-uuid")
    assert response.status_code == 422
    assert "not-a-uuid" not in response.text


async def test_a_heartbeat_keeps_an_idle_stream_open(client) -> None:
    """nginx closes an idle connection at 60 s and Cloudflare at ~100 s. An
    SSE stream on a library nobody touched sends nothing for hours, so the
    server generates the traffic -- the same requirement PRD 03 states for
    the WebSocket lane, in the other direction. A `:` comment line is one an
    SSE client is required to ignore."""
    async with client.stream("GET", "/events") as response:
        line = await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(response.aiter_lines())), timeout=2.0)
    assert line.startswith(":")


async def test_a_last_event_id_header_is_honoured(client, bus) -> None:
    await bus.publish(ClientEvent(kind=ClientEventKind.TITLE_UPDATED, data={"n": 1}))
    await bus.publish(ClientEvent(kind=ClientEventKind.TITLE_UPDATED, data={"n": 2}))
    headers = {"Last-Event-ID": f"{bus.epoch}-1"}
    async with client.stream("GET", "/events", headers=headers) as response:
        frame = await asyncio.wait_for(_read_frame(aiter(response.aiter_lines())), timeout=2.0)
    assert '"n": 2' in frame or '"n":2' in frame


async def test_a_disconnect_unsubscribes(client, bus) -> None:
    """An SSE client disconnecting is the common case. A route that leaked a
    subscriber per connection would grow one queue per browser tab for the
    life of the process."""
    assert bus.subscribers == 0
    async with client.stream("GET", "/events"):
        await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
        assert bus.subscribers == 1
    for _ in range(20):
        await asyncio.sleep(0.01)
        if bus.subscribers == 0:
            break
    assert bus.subscribers == 0


async def _read_frame(lines) -> str:
    """Read until a blank line, skipping heartbeat comments."""
    collected: list[str] = []
    while True:
        line = await anext(lines)
        if line.startswith(":"):
            continue
        if line == "":
            if collected:
                return "\n".join(collected)
            continue
        collected.append(line)
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the wire format and the route**

```python
# src/usher/api/dto/events.py
"""The SSE wire format (PRD 07).

`api/dto/` types are distinct from `domain/` and `ports/` models, and here
that split has one immediate job: `SseEventKind` is the vocabulary a client
matches on, so renaming `ClientEventKind.TITLE_UPDATED` internally is a
mypy error at the mapping below rather than a silent wire break.

There is no `response_model`. A `StreamingResponse` is bytes and FastAPI's
serializer never sees it, so the shape is asserted here rather than
described in `/openapi.json` — the one place in this API where that is true,
and it is a property of SSE rather than of this route.
"""

import json
import uuid
from enum import StrEnum
from typing import Any

from usher.ports.events import ClientEventKind
from usher.services.events import SentEvent


class SseEventKind(StrEnum):
    """PRD 07's event names, on the wire."""

    TITLE_UPDATED = "title.updated"
    WATCHSTATE_UPDATED = "watchstate.updated"
    SYNC_PROGRESS = "sync.progress"
    RESYNC_REQUIRED = "resync_required"


_WIRE: dict[ClientEventKind, SseEventKind] = {
    ClientEventKind.TITLE_UPDATED: SseEventKind.TITLE_UPDATED,
    ClientEventKind.WATCHSTATE_UPDATED: SseEventKind.WATCHSTATE_UPDATED,
    ClientEventKind.SYNC_PROGRESS: SseEventKind.SYNC_PROGRESS,
    ClientEventKind.RESYNC_REQUIRED: SseEventKind.RESYNC_REQUIRED,
}


def encode_sse(sent: SentEvent) -> str:
    """One `SentEvent` as an SSE frame.

    `id:` carries the epoch, which is what makes Task 19's unknown-epoch
    branch reachable at all: a browser sends this value back verbatim as
    `Last-Event-ID`, and without the epoch a restarted server cannot tell a
    resume from a different sequence's id.

    `data:` is one line, always. A newline inside a payload is a second
    `data:` line to an SSE parser and silently truncates the frame, so
    `json.dumps` -- which escapes them -- is load-bearing rather than
    stylistic.
    """
    payload: dict[str, Any] = dict(sent.event.data)
    if sent.event.title_id is not None:
        payload["title_id"] = str(sent.event.title_id)
    if sent.event.episode_id is not None:
        payload["episode_id"] = str(sent.event.episode_id)
    return (
        f"id: {sent.epoch}-{sent.id}\n"
        f"event: {_WIRE[sent.event.kind].value}\n"
        f"data: {json.dumps(payload, separators=(',', ':'))}\n\n"
    )


def parse_titles(raw: str | None) -> frozenset[uuid.UUID] | None:
    """`?titles=id1,id2` into a filter, or `None` for "everything".

    Raises `ValueError` for anything that is not a uuid; the route turns
    that into a 422 whose detail names the *rule* and never the submitted
    value -- `usher.api.errors` strips `input` from every validation error
    app-wide for the same reason, and PRD 08's "a rejected request never
    echoes the body it rejected" does not stop being true for a query
    string.
    """
    if raw is None or raw == "":
        return None
    return frozenset(uuid.UUID(part.strip()) for part in raw.split(","))
```

```python
# src/usher/api/routers/events.py
"""`GET /events` — PRD 07's SSE channel.

SSE rather than a WebSocket, and PRD 07 states the argument: the channel is
server→client only, it survives proxies that mangle upgrades, and it
reconnects natively in browsers. The third is what pays for Task 19: an
`EventSource` retries and resends `Last-Event-ID` with no client code at
all.
"""

import asyncio
from collections.abc import AsyncIterator

from fastapi import APIRouter, HTTPException, Query, Request, status
from fastapi.responses import StreamingResponse

from usher.api.deps import EventBusDep, SettingsDep
from usher.api.dto.events import encode_sse, parse_titles

router = APIRouter(tags=["events"])

_HEARTBEAT = ": keepalive\n\n"


@router.get("/events")
async def events(
    request: Request,
    bus: EventBusDep,
    settings: SettingsDep,
    titles: str | None = Query(default=None, description="Comma-separated title ids to scope to"),
) -> StreamingResponse:
    try:
        wanted = parse_titles(titles)
    except ValueError as exc:
        # The rule, never the value. PRD 08: a rejected request never echoes
        # what it rejected, and a query string is a submitted body's
        # neighbour rather than its exception.
        raise HTTPException(
            status_code=status.HTTP_422_UNPROCESSABLE_ENTITY,
            detail="titles must be a comma-separated list of uuids",
        ) from exc
    last_event_id = request.headers.get("last-event-id")

    async def stream() -> AsyncIterator[str]:
        # The heartbeat goes first, before anything is published: it flushes
        # the response head through every proxy in the path, so a client
        # knows it is connected rather than waiting on a library that may
        # not change for hours.
        yield _HEARTBEAT
        async with bus.subscribe(titles=wanted, last_event_id=last_event_id) as sent_events:
            iterator = aiter(sent_events)
            while True:
                try:
                    sent = await asyncio.wait_for(
                        anext(iterator), timeout=settings.sse_heartbeat_seconds
                    )
                except TimeoutError:
                    # nginx closes an idle connection at 60 s and Cloudflare
                    # at ~100 s, and this stream sends nothing on a quiet
                    # library. A `:` comment is a line the SSE spec requires
                    # a client to ignore, so it costs a client nothing.
                    yield _HEARTBEAT
                    continue
                except StopAsyncIteration:
                    return
                yield encode_sse(sent)

    return StreamingResponse(
        stream(),
        media_type="text/event-stream",
        headers={
            # nginx buffers a proxied response body by default, which holds
            # every event until the buffer fills -- the exact opposite of
            # what this route is for. `X-Accel-Buffering: no` is nginx's own
            # opt-out and is ignored by everything else.
            "Cache-Control": "no-cache",
            "X-Accel-Buffering": "no",
            "Connection": "keep-alive",
        },
    )
```

```python
# src/usher/api/deps.py  (append)
def get_event_bus(request: Request) -> InMemoryEventBus:
    """The process-wide bus, built by `create_app`'s lifespan.

    On `app.state` rather than request-scoped for the reason `EnrichService`
    is absent from this module: a per-request bus would give every SSE
    connection its own, and a publisher would fan out to nobody. Same
    defensive `getattr`/`cast` shape as `get_session_factory`, and for the
    same reason -- `app.state` is typed `Any`.
    """
    bus = getattr(request.app.state, "events", None)
    if bus is None:
        raise RuntimeError(
            "app.state.events is not set -- create_app's lifespan has not run."
        )
    return cast(InMemoryEventBus, bus)


EventBusDep = Annotated[InMemoryEventBus, Depends(get_event_bus)]


def get_event_publisher(bus: EventBusDep) -> EventPublisher:
    """The same object, as the port. Routes and services that only publish
    take this, so nothing outside `get_event_bus` depends on the bus
    offering `subscribe`."""
    return bus
```

and `usher.telemetry` gains the third gauge:

```python
# src/usher/telemetry.py  (append)
_sse_reader: Callable[[], int] | None = None


def register_sse_gauge(read: Callable[[], int]) -> None:
    """PRD 10's `usher.sse.connections`.

    A live read of an in-memory integer, which is the one shape an
    observable OTel callback may safely do here -- `register_queue_gauges`
    explains why the queue's equivalent cannot. Module global rather than a
    captured closure, because the SDK keeps only the first observable gauge
    registered under a name.
    """
    global _sse_reader
    _sse_reader = read
    metrics.get_meter("usher.api").create_observable_gauge(
        "usher.sse.connections",
        callbacks=[_observe_sse_connections],
        unit="1",
        description="Open SSE client connections",
    )


def _observe_sse_connections(options: CallbackOptions) -> Iterable[Observation]:
    return [] if _sse_reader is None else [Observation(_sse_reader())]
```

In `docs/prd/07-client-api.md`, mark the SSE table's rows with what M5 ships:

```markdown
| Event | Payload | Client action | |
|---|---|---|---|
| `title.updated` | Title id + changed fields | Patch in place | ✅ M5 |
| `watchstate.updated` | Title/episode id, position, played | Update progress | ✅ M5 |
| `row.invalidated` | Row slug | Refetch that row | M7 |
| `sync.progress` | Source, counts, phase | Admin UI only | ✅ M5 |
| `bootstrap.progress` | Phase, percent | Admin UI only | — |

> **Settled in M5.** `resync_required` is a fifth event and the channel's
> own: it answers a subscriber whose buffer overflowed, a `Last-Event-ID`
> older than the replay ring, and one minted by a previous process (the
> ring is in-memory, so ids restart with it — the id a client sees is
> `<epoch>-<n>` for exactly that reason).
>
> `bootstrap.progress` is **not emitted**, and the reason is structural
> rather than an omission: `usher bootstrap` is a separate process and M5's
> bus is in-process, so there is no channel from one to the other. Emitting
> a type nothing publishes would be a client handler that waits forever —
> the same argument [10](10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md) makes for a metric
> nothing records. A cross-process transport is the named second
> implementation of the `EventPublisher` port
> ([ADR-0019](decisions/0019-the-client-event-channel-is-a-port.md)) and is
> what would make it emittable.
```

## Task 21: Three publishers, and one that would have been noise

The bus exists; something has to fill it. Three services publish, and each is one call in a place that already exists.

| Publisher | Event | Where |
|---|---|---|
| `EnrichService.enrich` | `title.updated` | On success only. A failed enrichment records `Title.enrichment_error` and leaves the tier where it was (ADR-0008); telling a client "this changed" when the change was a recorded failure would make it refetch an identical stub. |
| `PushApplyService` | `watchstate.updated`, `title.updated` | Task 14, already written |
| `ReconcileService._flush` | `sync.progress` | Once per batch |

**And one that is deliberately not a publisher.** `WatchStateSyncService._flush` — the nightly watch-state walk — publishes nothing. A walk of the one measured library merges up to 1,126,789 states; a `watchstate.updated` per merged row is a fan-out per row per night to every connected client, and every one of them is the source echoing back state that has not changed since the last walk. The push lane publishes because a push event *is* a change. The walk is a backstop, and a backstop that told every client about every row it re-confirmed would be the polling this design exists to remove, arriving from the other side.

**`sync.progress` is scoped to nobody**, so it reaches only unfiltered subscribers — which PRD 07 marks "Admin UI only". A detail screen with `?titles=…` never sees one, which is exactly the scoping that table implies.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/usher/services/enrich.py`, `src/usher/services/reconcile.py`
- Modify: `src/usher/api/deps.py`, `src/usher/cli.py`
- Modify: `tests/unit/test_services_enrich.py`, `tests/unit/test_services_reconcile.py`

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the publishers**

`EnrichService.__init__` takes `events: EventPublisher = NullEventPublisher()`… **no.** A mutable-looking default that is actually a shared instance is a trap even when the object is stateless, and every other collaborator on these services is required. Take it as a required keyword argument and let the two composition roots supply `NullEventPublisher()` where they mean it:

```python
# src/usher/services/enrich.py
    def __init__(
        self,
        titles: TitleRepository,
        episodes: EpisodeRepository,
        payloads: RawPayloadStore,
        provider: MetadataProvider,
        commit: Callable[[], Awaitable[None]],
        events: EventPublisher,
        *,
        cache_max_age_days: int = 30,
    ) -> None:
```

and at the end of the success path, after the commit:

```python
        # PRD 03's read-through loop, closed. On success only: a failure
        # records `enrichment_error` and leaves the tier exactly where it
        # was (ADR-0008), so "this changed" would make a client refetch an
        # identical stub -- once per attempt of a backoff schedule.
        await self._events.publish(
            ClientEvent(
                kind=ClientEventKind.TITLE_UPDATED,
                title_id=title.id,
                # The fields a client can patch without refetching. PRD 07:
                # "Title id + changed fields | Patch in place."
                data={"fields": sorted(changed) + ["enrichment_state"]},
            )
        )
```

where `changed` is the set `_merge` already computes for `field_provenance`. If it is a local rather than a return value, hoist it — the alternative is publishing `["*"]`, which turns "patch in place" back into "refetch".

```python
# src/usher/services/reconcile.py  (in _flush, after the commit)
        # Scoped to no title, so only an unfiltered subscriber sees it --
        # PRD 07 marks this "Admin UI only" and the scoping is what
        # implements that. A nightly walk of the one measured library
        # flushes 1,127 of these.
        await self._events.publish(
            ClientEvent(
                kind=ClientEventKind.SYNC_PROGRESS,
                data={
                    "source": self._source_name,
                    "kind": run.kind.value,
                    "items_seen": run.items_seen,
                    "items_matched": run.items_matched,
                    "items_unmatched": run.items_unmatched,
                },
            )
        )
```

`_flush` does not currently hold the source's name — it has `run.source_id`. Pass the `Source` down from `reconcile` into `_walk`/`_flush` rather than adding a repository read: it is already in scope at the top of `reconcile`, and a name in an event payload that a client renders should be the name an operator configured.

Both composition roots gain the argument. `usher.api.deps.get_reconcile_service` takes `Depends(get_event_publisher)`; `usher.cli._build_pipeline` passes `NullEventPublisher()` and says why:

```python
    # `NullEventPublisher`, deliberately. `usher work` and `usher sync` are
    # separate processes and M5's bus is in-process (ADR-0019), so there is
    # no SSE client on the other side of one of these. A client that
    # refetches still gets the enriched title -- degradation rather than
    # breakage, PRD 08's own principle -- and the fix is the named second
    # implementation of the port rather than a branch here.
```

## Task 22: `MediaItemRepository.list_for_title`

PRD 07's title response carries `availability` — which sources hold this title, and what each copy is. `media_items` has held that since M1 and nothing has ever read it by `title_id`; `ix_media_items_title_id` has existed since M4's migration and has no query behind it.

Two properties that are not obvious:

**It returns every source's copy, and unavailable ones too.** A read keyed on `(source_id, title_id)` makes a two-source household's detail screen show one badge. And PRD 02 is "soft-delete availability, hard-delete nothing" — a client rendering "not on any source" for a film on a temporarily unmounted drive is showing a different fact than the one stored. `available` is on the wire and the client decides.

**Ordered, and by something stable.** An unordered read makes a detail screen shuffle its badges between refreshes for no reason a user can see. `available DESC, last_seen_at DESC, id` — the copy that is there, then the freshest, then a total order.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/usher/ports/repository.py`, `src/usher/db/repositories/media_item.py`
- Modify: `tests/fakes/media_item_repository.py`, `tests/contract/media_item_repository_contract.py`

```python
    async def test_list_for_title_returns_every_copy_across_sources(self, repo) -> None:
        """PRD 07's `availability` array. A read keyed on
        `(source_id, title_id)` makes a two-source household's detail screen
        show one badge; a read that filtered on `available` makes a film on
        a temporarily unmounted drive read as "not on any source", which is
        a different fact than the one stored (PRD 02: soft-delete
        availability, hard-delete nothing).
        """
        title_id = new_id()
        other_source = await self.given_source(repo)
        await repo.upsert_many(
            [
                _upsert(SOURCE_ID, "a", title_id=title_id, last_seen_at=T1),
                _upsert(other_source, "b", title_id=title_id, last_seen_at=T0),
                _upsert(SOURCE_ID, "c", title_id=new_id(), last_seen_at=T1),
            ]
        )
        await repo.mark_unseen_unavailable(
            other_source, seen_since=T1, max_retract_fraction=1.0
        )
        rows = await repo.list_for_title(title_id)
        assert [(row.external_id, row.available) for row in rows] == [("a", True), ("b", False)]

    async def test_list_for_title_is_ordered_available_then_freshest(self, repo) -> None:
        """An unordered read makes a detail screen shuffle its badges
        between refreshes for no reason a user can see -- and `RETURNING`
        and a bare `SELECT` both promise nothing about row order, which M4
        measured against real Postgres at three queue depths."""
        title_id = new_id()
        await repo.upsert_many(
            [
                _upsert(SOURCE_ID, "old", title_id=title_id, last_seen_at=T0),
                _upsert(SOURCE_ID, "new", title_id=title_id, last_seen_at=T1),
            ]
        )
        assert [row.external_id for row in await repo.list_for_title(title_id)] == ["new", "old"]

    async def test_list_for_title_answers_empty_for_a_title_on_no_source(self, repo) -> None:
        """The catalog holds 1,271,138 titles and the one measured source
        holds 1,126,789 items, so most titles are on no source at all. That
        is a normal answer, not a missing row."""
        assert await repo.list_for_title(new_id()) == []
```

`given_source` is an existing hook on that contract (it exists because a real repository has a foreign key to `sources` and a fake does not).

```python
# src/usher/ports/repository.py  (on MediaItemRepository)
    @abstractmethod
    async def list_for_title(self, title_id: uuid.UUID) -> list[MediaItem]:
        """Every copy of one title, across every source.

        PRD 07's `availability` array. **Unavailable copies are returned**,
        with `available = false`, rather than filtered: PRD 02 is
        "soft-delete availability, hard-delete nothing", and a client
        rendering "not on any source" for a film on a temporarily unmounted
        drive is showing a different fact than the one stored. The client
        decides what a retracted copy means.

        Ordered `available` first, then most recently seen, then by id --
        stable, so a detail screen does not shuffle its badges between
        refreshes. Nothing about a `SELECT` promises an order otherwise.

        Empty is the common answer: the catalog holds 1,271,138 titles and
        the one measured source holds 1,126,789 items, most of which are
        episodes, so the great majority of titles are on no source.
        """
```

```python
# src/usher/db/repositories/media_item.py
    async def list_for_title(self, title_id: uuid.UUID) -> list[MediaItem]:
        # `ix_media_items_title_id` exists since M4's migration and has had
        # no query behind it until now. One indexed read; nothing here pages,
        # because a title has copies in the single digits -- `list_unmatched`
        # is the method on this port that needed an `OFFSET` and it is
        # measured at 388.9 ms at offset 1,126,574 for exactly that reason.
        rows = await self._session.scalars(
            select(MediaItemRow)
            .where(MediaItemRow.title_id == title_id)
            .order_by(
                MediaItemRow.available.desc(),
                MediaItemRow.last_seen_at.desc(),
                MediaItemRow.id,
            )
        )
        return [_to_domain(row) for row in rows]
```

```python
# tests/fakes/media_item_repository.py
    async def list_for_title(self, title_id: uuid.UUID) -> list[MediaItem]:
        matching = [item for item in self._items.values() if item.title_id == title_id]
        return sorted(
            matching, key=lambda item: (not item.available, -item.last_seen_at.timestamp(), item.id)
        )
```

## Task 23: `TitleReadService` — a degraded source narrows the answer, never fails it

The read-through half, and it is where PRD 08's governing rule becomes code: **"a degraded subsystem narrows functionality; it never fails a request local state can answer."**

**This service does not hold a `SourceAdapter`, and that is the design rather than an omission.** Every fact it returns is in `titles`, `media_items` and `watch_states`. There is therefore no code path from an unreachable Emby to a failed `GET /titles/{id}` — not because the failure is caught, but because the call that could fail is not made. `usher.ports.errors` already draws the line the other way round (`PortUnavailable` is "distinct from *the requested thing does not exist*", and `get_item` returns `None` for absence and never raises it); this is the same line seen from the read side.

**Demand promotion is here, not in the route.** PRD 03: "Requesting an unenriched title promotes its job to the front of the queue rather than blocking the response. The API returns the stub immediately with `enrichment_state: "stub"`." Three properties, each a case:

- **It enqueues at `JobPriority.DEMAND`**, which is the first caller of the promotion clause M4 wrote and measured (`ON CONFLICT … SET priority = GREATEST(…) WHERE jobs.status <> 'parked' AND jobs.priority < excluded.priority`).
- **It does not enqueue for an already-enriched title.** A queue that grew a row per title view is the queue M4's "enqueueing an `enrich` job for each makes the queue permanently the size of the library" argument is about, arriving from the client side.
- **It does not un-park.** PRD 08: "Re-enqueueing does not un-park. Poison a human has not looked at is not fixed by asking for it again, and a parked job's priority is not promoted behind their back either." The enqueue statement enforces this and this service does not work around it — a parked title renders its `enrichment_error` to the client instead, which is a field PRD 07 already requires.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/services/titles.py`
- Create: `tests/unit/test_services_titles.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

```python
# tests/unit/test_services_titles.py
"""Read-through: the local answer, and the promotion it triggers."""

import uuid

import pytest

from usher.domain.enums import EnrichmentState, JobKind, TitleKind
from usher.domain.jobs import JobPriority
from usher.services.titles import TitleReadService


async def test_a_title_is_answered_from_local_state(service, titles, media_items) -> None:
    title = await _seed(titles, EnrichmentState.ENRICHED)
    media_items.seed_item(title.id, source_id=SOURCE.id, external_id="e1", available=True)
    detail = await service.detail(title.id, user_id=USER_ID)
    assert detail is not None
    assert detail.title.id == title.id
    assert [copy.external_id for copy in detail.availability] == ["e1"]


async def test_availability_names_the_source_an_operator_configured(
    service, titles, media_items, sources
) -> None:
    """A client renders "on Living Room Emby", not a uuid. The name comes
    from the `Source` row, and one batched read serves every copy -- a
    household has sources in the single digits and a per-copy lookup here
    would be a query per badge."""
    title = await _seed(titles, EnrichmentState.ENRICHED)
    media_items.seed_item(title.id, source_id=SOURCE.id, external_id="e1", available=True)
    detail = await service.detail(title.id, user_id=USER_ID)
    assert detail is not None
    assert detail.availability[0].source_name == "Living Room Emby"


async def test_a_missing_title_is_none_not_an_error(service) -> None:
    """`None` for absence, matching every read on every port in this
    project. The route turns it into a 404; a raise would make the common
    case travel through an exception path."""
    assert await service.detail(uuid.uuid4(), user_id=USER_ID) is None


async def test_watch_state_is_this_users(service, titles, watch_states) -> None:
    title = await _seed(titles, EnrichmentState.ENRICHED)
    watch_states.seed(user_id=USER_ID, title_id=title.id, position_seconds=1840, played=False)
    watch_states.seed(user_id=OTHER_USER_ID, title_id=title.id, position_seconds=9999, played=True)
    detail = await service.detail(title.id, user_id=USER_ID)
    assert detail is not None
    assert detail.watch_state is not None
    assert detail.watch_state.position_seconds == 1840


async def test_a_stub_is_returned_immediately_and_promoted(service, titles, queue) -> None:
    """PRD 03: "Requesting an unenriched title promotes its job to the front
    of the queue rather than blocking the response. The API returns the stub
    immediately with `enrichment_state: "stub"`." The *first* caller of the
    promotion clause M4 wrote and left uncalled."""
    title = await _seed(titles, EnrichmentState.STUB)
    detail = await service.detail(title.id, user_id=USER_ID)
    assert detail is not None
    assert detail.title.enrichment_state is EnrichmentState.STUB
    assert detail.promoted is True
    jobs = queue.all_jobs()
    assert [(job.kind, job.key, job.priority) for job in jobs] == [
        (JobKind.ENRICH, str(title.id), JobPriority.DEMAND)
    ]


async def test_a_skeleton_is_promoted_too(service, titles, queue) -> None:
    """`skeleton` is the tier a bulk-imported title sits at and it is the
    one most in need of a client-triggered fill. A guard written as
    `state is EnrichmentState.STUB` promotes only the middle rung -- and
    `EnrichmentState` is a `StrEnum` whose members compare
    lexicographically, so the tempting `state < ENRICHED` never promotes
    anything at all (ADR-0008; `ENRICHED` sorts below both other rungs)."""
    title = await _seed(titles, EnrichmentState.SKELETON)
    await service.detail(title.id, user_id=USER_ID)
    assert [job.priority for job in queue.all_jobs()] == [JobPriority.DEMAND]


async def test_an_enriched_title_is_not_enqueued(service, titles, queue) -> None:
    """A queue that grew a row per title view is M4's "enqueueing an enrich
    job for each makes the queue permanently the size of the library",
    arriving from the client side."""
    title = await _seed(titles, EnrichmentState.ENRICHED)
    detail = await service.detail(title.id, user_id=USER_ID)
    assert detail is not None
    assert detail.promoted is False
    assert queue.all_jobs() == []


async def test_a_parked_job_is_not_promoted_behind_a_humans_back(
    service, titles, queue
) -> None:
    """PRD 08: "Re-enqueueing does not un-park. Poison a human has not
    looked at is not fixed by asking for it again, and a parked job's
    priority is not promoted behind their back either." The client is told
    what happened instead -- `enrichment_error` is on PRD 07's wire contract
    for exactly this."""
    title = await _seed(titles, EnrichmentState.STUB, error="TMDb answered 404 for tmdb_id=…")
    await queue.park(JobKind.ENRICH, str(title.id), error="TMDb answered 404")
    detail = await service.detail(title.id, user_id=USER_ID)
    assert detail is not None
    assert detail.title.enrichment_error is not None
    assert [job.status for job in queue.parked()] == [JobStatus.PARKED]


async def test_the_promotion_records_the_requests_trace(service, titles, queue) -> None:
    """PRD 10's "why did the title I just opened take 45 seconds" is one
    query, and it is `traceparent` on the job row followed backwards from
    the worker's `Link`."""
    tracer = trace.get_tracer("test")
    title = await _seed(titles, EnrichmentState.STUB)
    with tracer.start_as_current_span("server"):
        await service.detail(title.id, user_id=USER_ID)
    assert queue.all_jobs()[0].traceparent is not None


async def test_reading_a_title_never_touches_a_source(service, titles, media_items) -> None:
    """PRD 08's governing rule, as a structural property rather than a
    caught exception: this service holds no `SourceAdapter`, so there is no
    path from an unreachable Emby to a failed read. The assertion is on the
    constructor's own dependencies, because "it did not raise" is also what
    a service that caught everything would produce."""
    import inspect

    from usher.ports.source import SourceAdapter, SourceAdapterFactory

    annotations = inspect.signature(TitleReadService.__init__).parameters
    assert not any(
        parameter.annotation in (SourceAdapter, SourceAdapterFactory)
        for parameter in annotations.values()
    )
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the service**

```python
# src/usher/services/titles.py
"""Read-through: what a client gets when it opens a title (PRD 03, PRD 07).

**Nothing here calls a source, and that is the whole design.** PRD 08: "a
degraded subsystem narrows functionality; it never fails a request local
state can answer." Every fact this returns is in `titles`, `media_items` and
`watch_states`, so an unreachable Emby cannot fail a title read -- not
because the failure is caught, but because the call that could fail is never
made. `usher.ports.errors` draws the same line from the other side:
`PortUnavailable` is "distinct from *the requested thing does not exist*".

**And it promotes.** PRD 03: "Requesting an unenriched title promotes its
job to the front of the queue rather than blocking the response. The API
returns the stub immediately." This is the first caller of the enqueue
clause M4 wrote and measured and deliberately left uncalled.
"""

import uuid
from dataclasses import dataclass

from usher.domain.enums import ENRICHMENT_RANK, EnrichmentState, HdrFormat, JobKind
from usher.domain.jobs import JobPriority
from usher.domain.title import Title
from usher.domain.watch import WatchState
from usher.ports.jobs import JobQueue, JobRequest
from usher.ports.repository import (
    MediaItemRepository,
    SourceRepository,
    TitleRepository,
    WatchStateRepository,
)
from usher.telemetry import current_traceparent


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class TitleAvailability:
    """One copy of a title on one source, as a client renders it.

    `source_name` rather than only `source_id`: a client renders "on Living
    Room Emby", and PRD 07's own example response carries a name. Nothing
    source-*specific* escapes here -- a name an operator typed is not an
    Emby concept.

    `available` is on the wire rather than being filtered out, because PRD
    02 is soft-delete availability and "this is on a source that is
    currently not reporting it" is a different thing to render than "this is
    on no source".
    """

    source_id: uuid.UUID
    source_name: str
    external_id: str
    available: bool
    container: str | None
    video_codec: str | None
    hdr_format: HdrFormat | None
    resolution: str | None
    runtime_seconds: int | None


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class TitleDetail:
    title: Title
    availability: tuple[TitleAvailability, ...]
    watch_state: WatchState | None
    # Whether this read moved an enrichment job to the front of the queue.
    # Returned rather than kept internal because it is what makes the
    # read-through path observable end to end -- PRD 10's "promotion latency
    # against the 5 s read-through target" needs something to start from.
    promoted: bool


class TitleReadService:
    def __init__(
        self,
        titles: TitleRepository,
        media_items: MediaItemRepository,
        sources: SourceRepository,
        watch_states: WatchStateRepository,
        queue: JobQueue,
    ) -> None:
        self._titles = titles
        self._media_items = media_items
        self._sources = sources
        self._watch_states = watch_states
        self._queue = queue

    async def detail(self, title_id: uuid.UUID, *, user_id: uuid.UUID) -> TitleDetail | None:
        """One title, everything local about it, and a promotion if it needs
        one. `None` when no such title exists.

        Four reads, none of them per-copy: the title, its media items, the
        source list (a household has sources in the single digits, and one
        batched read serves every badge), and this user's watch state.
        """
        title = await self._titles.get(title_id)
        if title is None:
            return None
        copies = await self._media_items.list_for_title(title_id)
        names = {source.id: source.name for source in await self._sources.list_all()}
        watch_state = await self._watch_states.get_for_title(user_id, title_id)
        return TitleDetail(
            title=title,
            availability=tuple(
                TitleAvailability(
                    source_id=copy.source_id,
                    # A source deleted between the two reads is the only way
                    # this misses, and "Unknown source" is a better answer
                    # than a `KeyError` on a screen an operator opens to
                    # find out what happened.
                    source_name=names.get(copy.source_id, "Unknown source"),
                    external_id=copy.external_id,
                    available=copy.available,
                    container=copy.container,
                    video_codec=copy.video_codec,
                    hdr_format=copy.hdr_format,
                    resolution=(
                        f"{copy.width}x{copy.height}"
                        if copy.width is not None and copy.height is not None
                        else None
                    ),
                    runtime_seconds=copy.runtime_seconds,
                )
                for copy in copies
            ),
            watch_state=watch_state,
            promoted=await self._promote(title),
        )

    async def _promote(self, title: Title) -> bool:
        """Move this title's enrichment to the front of the queue.

        **The comparison goes through `ENRICHMENT_RANK`**, never through the
        members. `EnrichmentState` is a `StrEnum` and compares
        lexicographically, so `title.enrichment_state < EnrichmentState.
        ENRICHED` is `False` for a `SKELETON` -- the guard would silently
        never promote anything, which is the exact shape ADR-0008's rank map
        exists to make unspellable.

        **A parked job stays parked.** PRD 08: "Re-enqueueing does not
        un-park... and a parked job's priority is not promoted behind their
        back either." The enqueue statement enforces it
        (`WHERE jobs.status <> 'parked'`) and nothing here works around it;
        the client is told through `Title.enrichment_error`, which PRD 07's
        wire contract carries for exactly this.

        Returns whether an enqueue was attempted, not whether a row changed
        -- a second request for the same title writes nothing, which is the
        honest number and not a failure to promote.
        """
        if ENRICHMENT_RANK[title.enrichment_state] >= ENRICHMENT_RANK[EnrichmentState.ENRICHED]:
            return False
        await self._queue.enqueue(
            [
                JobRequest(
                    kind=JobKind.ENRICH,
                    key=str(title.id),
                    priority=JobPriority.DEMAND,
                    # PRD 10's "why did the title I just opened take 45
                    # seconds": the worker's span links back to this
                    # request's, minutes later.
                    traceparent=current_traceparent(),
                )
            ]
        )
        return True
```

It is an equivalent mutant *now* and stops being one the moment a fourth tier exists, and the rank map is what makes the intent legible. Recorded, kept. |
| `if title.enrichment_state < EnrichmentState.ENRICHED` (direct comparison) | `test_a_stub_is_returned_immediately_and_promoted` **and** `test_a_skeleton_is_promoted_too` — ADR-0008's trap: `ENRICHED` sorts lexicographically below both other rungs, so this guard promotes nothing at all, silently |
| `priority=JobPriority.NEW` | `test_a_stub_is_returned_immediately_and_promoted` |
| Drop the `traceparent` | `test_the_promotion_records_the_requests_trace` |
