| `names[copy.source_id]` (no `.get`) | Nothing — **write the case.** A source deleted between the two reads gives a `KeyError` and a 500 on the screen an operator opens to find out what happened. |
| Take a `SourceAdapterFactory` and call `verify()` per copy | `test_reading_a_title_never_touches_a_source` |

## Task 24: `GET /titles/{id}`

M5's second and last route. PRD 07's response example, restricted to what M4 fills: metadata, `enrichment_state`, `enrichment_error`, `availability`, `watch_state`.

**Four fields PRD 07 shows are deliberately absent**, and each names the milestone that fills it rather than shipping an empty list a client cannot distinguish from a genuine absence: `images` (M9's proxy), `credits` (M7 derives `Person`/`Credit` from `raw_payloads`), `similar` (M6's neighbours, and its own route), and the season/episode hierarchy (M9's `GET /series/{id}/seasons`). PRD 09's boundary call 2 already assigns all four.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/api/dto/title.py`, `src/usher/api/routers/titles.py`
- Modify: `src/usher/api/deps.py`, `src/usher/api/app.py`
- Create: `tests/unit/test_api_titles.py`

```python
# tests/unit/test_api_titles.py
"""`GET /titles/{id}`."""

import uuid

import pytest


async def test_a_title_renders_its_metadata_and_availability(client, seeded) -> None:
    response = await client.get(f"/titles/{seeded.title_id}")
    assert response.status_code == 200
    body = response.json()
    assert body["id"] == str(seeded.title_id)
    assert body["name"] == "Example Movie"
    assert body["enrichment_state"] == "enriched"
    assert body["availability"] == [
        {
            "source_id": str(seeded.source_id),
            "source": "Living Room Emby",
            "available": True,
            "container": "mkv",
            "video_codec": "hevc",
            "hdr_format": "DV",
            "resolution": "3840x2160",
            "runtime_seconds": 9360,
        }
    ]


async def test_an_unknown_title_is_a_404_in_the_shape_m3_ships(client) -> None:
    """Not RFC 9457 -- see the plan's "Does a streaming surface force the
    error envelope?". Identical in kind to `GET /admin/sources/{id}/status`'s
    404, which M3 shipped in this shape deliberately."""
    response = await client.get(f"/titles/{uuid.uuid4()}")
    assert response.status_code == 404
    assert response.json() == {"detail": "title not found"}


async def test_a_malformed_id_is_a_422_that_does_not_echo_it(client) -> None:
    response = await client.get("/titles/not-a-uuid")
    assert response.status_code == 422
    assert "not-a-uuid" not in response.text


async def test_watch_state_is_rendered_when_there_is_one(client, seeded) -> None:
    response = await client.get(f"/titles/{seeded.title_id}")
    assert response.json()["watch_state"] == {
        "position_seconds": 1840,
        "played": False,
        "play_count": 0,
        "last_played_at": None,
    }


async def test_watch_state_is_null_when_there_is_none(client, unwatched) -> None:
    """`null`, not a zeroed object. PRD 07's whole "render deliberately
    rather than inferring intent from nulls" argument runs the other way
    here: a fabricated all-zero state is indistinguishable from a real one
    that says "started and abandoned at second zero"."""
    assert (await client.get(f"/titles/{unwatched.title_id}")).json()["watch_state"] is None


async def test_a_stub_renders_its_state_and_its_error(client, parked_stub) -> None:
    """PRD 07: `enrichment_state` on every title-bearing response so clients
    render skeleton shimmer deliberately, and `enrichment_error` as a
    *separate, independent* field (ADR-0008) so a failed attempt is visible
    without inventing a `failed` tier."""
    body = (await client.get(f"/titles/{parked_stub.title_id}")).json()
    assert body["enrichment_state"] == "stub"
    assert body["enrichment_error"] == "TMDb answered 404"


async def test_opening_a_stub_promotes_its_enrichment(client, stub, queue) -> None:
    await client.get(f"/titles/{stub.title_id}")
    assert [(job.key, job.priority) for job in queue.all_jobs()] == [
        (str(stub.title_id), 100)
    ]


async def test_the_response_carries_no_source_specific_concept(client, seeded) -> None:
    """PRD 07's first line: "Nothing in this surface mentions a media
    server; sources appear only as availability badges and playback
    targets." An `external_id` on the wire is Emby's own item id, which is
    both a leak of a source concept and a value a client has no use for --
    every route that needs one takes a `Title.id`.
    """
    body = (await client.get(f"/titles/{seeded.title_id}")).json()
    assert "external_id" not in str(body)
    assert "emby" not in str(body).lower()


async def test_the_response_carries_no_credential(client, seeded) -> None:
    """The rule that has one documented exception in v1, and this route is
    not it (ADR-0012's exception is a `direct` playback target's URL, which
    is M9's `POST /titles/{id}/play`)."""
    body = (await client.get(f"/titles/{seeded.title_id}")).json()
    assert "api_key" not in str(body)
    assert "credentials_ref" not in str(body)
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the DTOs and the route**

```python
# src/usher/api/dto/title.py
"""`GET /titles/{id}` (PRD 07).

**Four fields PRD 07's example carries are absent**, and each is deferred to
the milestone that fills it rather than shipped empty: `images` (M9's
proxy), `credits` (M7 derives `Person`/`Credit` from `raw_payloads` with no
second network call), `similar` (M6's neighbours, and its own route), and
the season/episode hierarchy (M9's `GET /series/{id}/seasons`). PRD 09's
boundary call 2 assigns all four. An empty list would be worse than an
absent field: a client cannot tell "not derived yet" from "this film has no
cast".

**No `external_id` on the wire.** PRD 07's first line is "Nothing in this
surface mentions a media server", and a source's own item id is both a
source concept escaping its adapter and a value no client has a use for --
every route a client calls takes a `Title.id`.
"""

import uuid

from pydantic import AwareDatetime, BaseModel

from usher.domain.enums import EnrichmentState, HdrFormat, TitleKind
from usher.services.titles import TitleDetail


class WatchStateResponse(BaseModel):
    position_seconds: int
    played: bool
    play_count: int
    last_played_at: AwareDatetime | None


class AvailabilityResponse(BaseModel):
    source_id: uuid.UUID
    # `source`, not `source_name`: PRD 07's own example spells it this way,
    # and this is a wire contract rather than a rename of a domain field.
    source: str
    available: bool
    container: str | None
    video_codec: str | None
    hdr_format: HdrFormat | None
    resolution: str | None
    runtime_seconds: int | None


class TitleResponse(BaseModel):
    id: uuid.UUID
    kind: TitleKind
    name: str
    year: int | None
    overview: str | None
    tagline: str | None
    runtime_minutes: int | None
    genres: tuple[str, ...]
    community_rating: float | None
    # PRD 07: "Every title-bearing response carries `enrichment_state` so
    # clients render deliberately -- skeleton shimmer on fields known to be
    # missing -- rather than inferring intent from nulls."
    enrichment_state: EnrichmentState
    # And a *separate, independent* field (ADR-0008): the wire contract does
    # not carry a `failed` tier, because a skeleton whose enrichment failed
    # is still a perfectly usable skeleton.
    enrichment_error: str | None
    availability: list[AvailabilityResponse]
    watch_state: WatchStateResponse | None

    @classmethod
    def of(cls, detail: TitleDetail) -> "TitleResponse":
        return cls(
            id=detail.title.id,
            kind=detail.title.kind,
            name=detail.title.name,
            year=detail.title.year,
            overview=detail.title.overview,
            tagline=detail.title.tagline,
            runtime_minutes=detail.title.runtime_minutes,
            genres=detail.title.genres,
            community_rating=detail.title.community_rating,
            enrichment_state=detail.title.enrichment_state,
            enrichment_error=detail.title.enrichment_error,
            availability=[
                AvailabilityResponse(
                    source_id=copy.source_id,
                    source=copy.source_name,
                    available=copy.available,
                    container=copy.container,
                    video_codec=copy.video_codec,
                    hdr_format=copy.hdr_format,
                    resolution=copy.resolution,
                    runtime_seconds=copy.runtime_seconds,
                )
                for copy in detail.availability
            ],
            watch_state=(
                None
                if detail.watch_state is None
                else WatchStateResponse(
                    position_seconds=detail.watch_state.position_seconds,
                    played=detail.watch_state.played,
                    play_count=detail.watch_state.play_count,
                    last_played_at=detail.watch_state.last_played_at,
                )
            ),
        )
```

```python
# src/usher/api/routers/titles.py
"""`GET /titles/{id}` — PRD 03's read-through, at the boundary.

**This route cannot fail because a source is down**, and that is structural
rather than defensive: `TitleReadService` holds no `SourceAdapter`, so there
is no call to catch. PRD 08: "a degraded subsystem narrows functionality; it
never fails a request local state can answer."

**It is a `GET` that writes**, once, and deliberately: opening an unenriched
title enqueues its `enrich` job at `JobPriority.DEMAND` (PRD 03's demand
promotion). Idempotent, and a second open writes zero rows -- the enqueue
statement's `WHERE jobs.priority < excluded.priority` sees nothing to
promote. `get_session` commits it as it commits any other request.
"""

import uuid

from fastapi import APIRouter, HTTPException, status

from usher.api.deps import DefaultUserIdDep, TitleReadServiceDep
from usher.api.dto.title import TitleResponse

router = APIRouter(tags=["titles"])


@router.get("/titles/{title_id}", response_model=TitleResponse)
async def get_title(
    title_id: uuid.UUID, titles: TitleReadServiceDep, user_id: DefaultUserIdDep
) -> TitleResponse:
    detail = await titles.detail(title_id, user_id=user_id)
    if detail is None:
        # FastAPI's default shape, exactly as `GET /admin/sources/{id}/status`
        # ships it. PRD 07's RFC 9457 envelope is M9's, where the first
        # route that must answer "the source is down and I cannot serve this
        # from local state" arrives -- see this milestone's plan for why a
        # streaming surface does not force it earlier.
        raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND, detail="title not found")
    return TitleResponse.of(detail)
```

```python
# src/usher/api/deps.py  (append)
def get_title_read_service(
    titles: Annotated[TitleRepository, Depends(get_title_repository)],
    media_items: MediaItemRepositoryDep,
    sources: Annotated[SourceRepository, Depends(get_source_repository)],
    watch_states: Annotated[WatchStateRepository, Depends(get_watch_state_repository)],
    queue: JobQueueDep,
) -> TitleReadService:
    return TitleReadService(titles, media_items, sources, watch_states, queue)


TitleReadServiceDep = Annotated[TitleReadService, Depends(get_title_read_service)]
```

`get_source_repository` is new (M4 constructed `PostgresSourceRepository` inside `get_source_service`); extract it so both use one provider.

And both routers are included in `create_app`:

```python
    app.include_router(health.router)
    app.include_router(sources.router)
    app.include_router(titles.router)
    app.include_router(events.router)
```

In `docs/prd/07-client-api.md`, annotate the Resources table:

```markdown
> **Built in M5: `GET /titles/{id}`, narrowed.** It carries metadata,
> `enrichment_state`, `enrichment_error`, `availability` and `watch_state` —
> every field backed by a table M4 fills. `credits`, `images` and the
> season/episode hierarchy are **absent rather than empty**: `Person`/
> `Credit` land with M7 and `Image` with M9, each re-derived from
> `raw_payloads` with no second network call ([09](09-roadmap.md)'s M4
> boundary call 2), and an empty list would be indistinguishable from a film
> with no cast. `GET /titles/{id}/similar` is M6's, with its own row here.
>
> Opening a `skeleton` or `stub` enqueues its enrichment at
> `JobPriority.DEMAND` — the first caller of the promotion clause M4 built —
> and returns immediately. A **parked** enrichment is not promoted (PRD
> [08](08-operations.md): re-enqueueing does not un-park, and a parked job's
> priority is not raised behind a human's back); the client is told through
> `enrichment_error` instead.
>
> **The route never calls a source.** Every field is local, so there is no
> path from an unreachable Emby to a failed title read — which is
> [08](08-operations.md)'s "never fails a request local state can answer" as
> a structural property rather than a caught exception.
```

## Task 25: Eleven settings, each read by something

PRD 08 puts knobs like these in a TOML config layer that still does not exist; until it does they live in `Settings` as environment settings, exactly as M2's `bulk_*`, M3's `source_*` and M4's `sync_*`/`job_*`/`tmdb_*` blocks do. **Every one below is read by a caller in this milestone** — the rule `config.py`'s own comment already states ("none is a field that validates and then influences nothing").

- Modify: `src/usher/config.py`, `.env.example`

```python
# tests/unit/test_config.py  (append)


def test_push_and_sse_settings_have_defaults_that_match_the_measured_deployment() -> None:
    settings = _settings()
    assert settings.push_enabled is True
    assert settings.worker_enabled is True
    assert settings.push_stale_after_seconds == 90.0
    assert settings.push_poll_seconds == 5.0
    assert settings.push_backoff_seconds == 5.0
    assert settings.push_max_backoff_seconds == 300.0
    assert settings.push_max_consecutive_failures == 5
    assert settings.push_max_items_per_event == 50
    assert settings.push_gap_min_interval_seconds == 60.0
    assert settings.push_source_refresh_seconds == 60.0
    assert settings.sse_buffer_size == 256
    assert settings.sse_queue_size == 64
    assert settings.sse_heartbeat_seconds == 20.0


def test_the_sse_heartbeat_is_under_every_proxy_idle_timeout() -> None:
    """nginx closes an idle connection at 60 s and Cloudflare at ~100 s
    (ADR-0004's operational facts, which apply to a long-lived HTTP response
    exactly as they apply to a WebSocket). A default at or above 60 would
    make an idle SSE stream drop on every proxied deployment."""
    assert _settings().sse_heartbeat_seconds < 60.0


def test_the_staleness_window_is_bounded_below_by_something_useful() -> None:
    """A window shorter than the source's own message interval reconnects a
    healthy channel forever. `gt=0` alone would permit `0.001`; the floor is
    a *documented* one rather than a guessed one -- Emby's `Sessions`
    interval is the subscription's own `0,1000`, i.e. one second, and this
    leaves three orders of magnitude of headroom."""
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        _settings(push_stale_after_seconds=0.5)


def test_max_items_per_event_is_bounded_above() -> None:
    """The cap exists because Emby emits `LibraryChanged` during a library
    scan and it can name thousands, against a source measured at 1,126,789
    items and 1-5 s per request. A ceiling an operator could set to 100,000
    would turn the guard off while looking configured."""
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        _settings(push_max_items_per_event=5000)
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the settings**

```python
# src/usher/config.py  (after the ingest-pipeline block)
    # The push lane and the client event channel (PRD 03, PRD 07). Same
    # reasoning as every block above: PRD 08's TOML config layer does not
    # exist yet. Deliberately named `push_*` and `sse_*` rather than
    # `websocket_*` -- a WebSocket is how *Emby* implements push, and
    # `config.py` is not an adapter.
    #
    # The two lane switches exist because PRD 01 promises "a `--worker`
    # entrypoint flag ... so lanes can be moved to a separate container
    # later by editing compose, with no code change". These are that flag,
    # as configuration rather than as an argument, so one image serves an
    # all-in-one deployment and a split one.
    push_enabled: bool = True
    worker_enabled: bool = True
    # How long a push channel may deliver *nothing at all* before it is torn
    # down and reconnected. Not a socket timeout: `websockets`'
    # ping_interval/ping_timeout already detect a dead TCP peer, and this
    # detects a live one that has stopped delivering -- the failure ADR-0004
    # measured when a handshake against a nonexistent path was upgraded and
    # held open. `ge=5.0` rather than `gt=0`: Emby's own `Sessions` interval
    # is the subscription's `0,1000` (one second), and a window shorter than
    # that reconnects a healthy channel forever.
    push_stale_after_seconds: float = Field(default=90.0, ge=5.0)
    # How long one `recv` waits before reporting "nothing yet", which is the
    # tick the staleness watchdog runs on. Small enough that a channel
    # crossing the window above is noticed within a tick of doing so.
    push_poll_seconds: float = Field(default=5.0, gt=0)
    # The base of the reconnect backoff, before jitter, and its ceiling.
    # Equal jitter, the same shape `job_backoff_seconds` drives one lane
    # over -- PRD 08's argument for it against full jitter transfers
    # unchanged.
    push_backoff_seconds: float = Field(default=5.0, gt=0)
    push_max_backoff_seconds: float = Field(default=300.0, gt=0)
    # PRD 08: "after N failures mark `supports_push = false` and lean on the
    # nightly walk". `ge=1` for the reason `job_max_attempts` has one: a
    # ceiling of zero disables push on the first blip.
    push_max_consecutive_failures: int = Field(default=5, ge=1)
    # How many items one push event may name before the lane stops resolving
    # them one at a time and asks for a delta walk instead. Emby emits
    # `LibraryChanged` during a library scan and it can name thousands;
    # against 1,126,789 items at 1-5 s per request, a request per changed
    # item is a design defect rather than a slow path. Bounded above at 500,
    # because a value an operator could set to 100,000 turns the guard off
    # while looking configured.
    push_max_items_per_event: int = Field(default=50, ge=1, le=500)
    # The floor between two gap-closing delta walks. A flapping socket plus
    # one delta per reconnect is a paged walk of everything changed since
    # the cursor, every few seconds.
    push_gap_min_interval_seconds: float = Field(default=60.0, ge=0)
    # How often the lane supervisor re-reads the source list, so a source
    # added through `POST /admin/sources` gets a lane without a restart.
    push_source_refresh_seconds: float = Field(default=60.0, gt=0)

    # How many events the SSE replay ring holds, for `Last-Event-ID`. A
    # client offline for longer than this is answered `resync_required`
    # rather than replayed a partial stream.
    sse_buffer_size: int = Field(default=256, ge=1, le=10_000)
    # Per-subscriber queue depth. On overflow the subscriber's queue is
    # emptied and replaced with one `resync_required` (PRD 07), so this is a
    # tolerance for a slow client rather than a delivery guarantee.
    sse_queue_size: int = Field(default=64, ge=1, le=10_000)
    # A comment line every this many seconds on an otherwise idle stream.
    # nginx closes an idle connection at 60 s and Cloudflare at ~100 s
    # (ADR-0004's operational facts, which are about an idle HTTP connection
    # and apply here unchanged), so `lt=60` is a compliance bound expressed
    # as a type.
    sse_heartbeat_seconds: float = Field(default=20.0, gt=0, lt=60.0)
```

and the same block, commented, in `.env.example`.

## Task 26: The server process grows two lanes

Scope boundary call 5, built. `create_app`'s lifespan starts a push lane per enabled source and one job-worker lane, and stops them on shutdown.

**Why the worker moves in-process.** PRD 03's read-through loop is `open → stub → promote → enrich → title.updated → client patches`, and M5's bus is in-memory. With the worker in a separate process the enrichment completes and nothing is told. PRD 01 already describes the intended shape — "Everything is asyncio in one process... A `--worker` entrypoint flag exists from day one so lanes can be moved to a separate container later" — and `api/deps.py`'s own comment already located the TMDb client ("It belongs on `app.state` at lifespan"). Both lanes are settings-gated, which is that flag.

**Why the lane supervisor re-reads the source list.** Sources are added at runtime through `POST /admin/sources` (PRD 08: "a deployment that needs a compose edit and a restart to connect a media server is the wrong shape for this"). A lane set fixed at startup would need a restart per source.

**Why the engine's `finally` matters more than it did.** `create_app`'s lifespan already carries a `try/finally` around `yield` with a comment saying "M5 onward adds websocket connections, job workers, and HTTP clients to this same lifespan, where a skipped cleanup call is a real leak, not a theoretical one". This is that milestone; the comment stops being a prediction.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/api/lanes.py`
- Modify: `src/usher/api/app.py`, `src/usher/api/deps.py`
- Create: `tests/unit/test_api_lanes.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

```python
# tests/unit/test_api_lanes.py
"""The lifespan's background lanes."""

import asyncio

import pytest

from usher.api.lanes import LaneSupervisor


async def test_a_lane_is_started_for_each_enabled_source(supervisor, sources) -> None:
    sources.seed(_source("A", enabled=True))
    sources.seed(_source("B", enabled=True))
    sources.seed(_source("C", enabled=False))
    await supervisor.start()
    await _settle()
    assert sorted(supervisor.running_sources()) == ["A", "B"]
    await supervisor.stop()


async def test_a_source_added_later_gets_a_lane_without_a_restart(supervisor, sources) -> None:
    """PRD 08: "Sources live in the database because they are added through
    the admin API. A deployment that needs a compose edit and a restart to
    connect a media server is the wrong shape for this." A lane set fixed at
    startup makes that false for push."""
    await supervisor.start()
    await _settle()
    sources.seed(_source("A", enabled=True))
    await supervisor.refresh()
    await _settle()
    assert supervisor.running_sources() == ["A"]
    await supervisor.stop()


async def test_a_disabled_source_has_its_lane_cancelled(supervisor, sources) -> None:
    """`enabled` is how an operator parks a server that is being rebuilt,
    and a lane that kept reconnecting to it would keep the backoff schedule
    warm against a machine nobody wants touched."""
    sources.seed(_source("A", enabled=True))
    await supervisor.start()
    await _settle()
    sources.replace(_source("A", enabled=False))
    await supervisor.refresh()
    await _settle()
    assert supervisor.running_sources() == []
    await supervisor.stop()


async def test_stopping_cancels_every_lane_and_closes_every_adapter(
    supervisor, sources, adapters
) -> None:
    sources.seed(_source("A", enabled=True))
    await supervisor.start()
    await _settle()
    await supervisor.stop()
    assert supervisor.running_sources() == []
    assert all(adapter.closed for adapter in adapters.built)


async def test_a_lane_that_crashes_does_not_take_the_others_down(
    supervisor, sources, adapters
) -> None:
    """A `PushSupervisor.run` that raised something that is not a
    `UsherPortError` -- a bug -- must cost its own source. Two lanes sharing
    one task group would take the whole set down, and the server with them
    if the group is awaited in the lifespan."""
    sources.seed(_source("A", enabled=True))
    sources.seed(_source("B", enabled=True))
    adapters.raise_for("A", ZeroDivisionError("a bug in the lane"))
    await supervisor.start()
    await _settle()
    assert supervisor.running_sources() == ["B"]
    await supervisor.stop()


async def test_a_source_with_no_credentials_is_skipped_with_a_message(
    supervisor, sources, credentials
) -> None:
    """The same reasoning `usher sync` applies one layer over: an operator
    with three sources needs the second and third to run when the first's
    credential row has gone missing."""
    sources.seed(_source("A", enabled=True))
    credentials.forget("A")
    await supervisor.start()
    await _settle()
    assert supervisor.running_sources() == []
    await supervisor.stop()


async def test_the_worker_lane_runs_when_enabled(supervisor, queue) -> None:
    """PRD 03's read-through loop only closes if the enrichment happens in
    the process the SSE client is connected to. M5's bus is in-memory, so
    this is what makes `title.updated` reach anybody."""
    await supervisor.start()
    await _settle()
    assert supervisor.worker_running() is True
    await supervisor.stop()
    assert supervisor.worker_running() is False


async def test_the_lanes_are_settings_gated(sources) -> None:
    """PRD 01: "A `--worker` entrypoint flag exists from day one so lanes
    can be moved to a separate container later by editing compose, with no
    code change." These settings are that flag."""
    supervisor = _supervisor(sources, push_enabled=False, worker_enabled=False)
    await supervisor.start()
    await _settle()
    assert supervisor.running_sources() == []
    assert supervisor.worker_running() is False
    await supervisor.stop()


async def _settle() -> None:
    """Let every freshly created task reach its first suspension point.

    Ten turns rather than one: a lane's first act is to open a session, then
    read the source list, then connect -- and a single `sleep(0)` yields
    once, which is enough for a bare mock and not enough for anything that
    actually awaits. Asserting after one turn is how a lane test passes
    against a supervisor that starts nothing.
    """
    for _ in range(10):
        await asyncio.sleep(0)
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the lane supervisor**

```python
# src/usher/api/lanes.py
"""The server process's background lanes (PRD 01's concurrency model).

Two kinds: one push lane per enabled source, and one job worker. Both are
settings-gated, which is PRD 01's "`--worker` entrypoint flag ... so lanes
can be moved to a separate container later by editing compose, with no code
change" expressed as configuration rather than as an argument -- one image
serves an all-in-one deployment and a split one.

**The worker runs here rather than only in `usher work`, and that is the
milestone's boundary call 5.** PRD 03's read-through loop is `open → stub →
promote → enrich → title.updated → client patches`, and M5's event bus is
in-memory. With the worker in another process the enrichment completes and
nothing is told; the client's next refetch gets the right answer, which is
degradation rather than breakage, but it is not the loop PRD 03 describes.
`usher work` keeps working for an operator who wants a separate worker, and
publishes to a `NullEventPublisher` -- stated in its own composition root.

**This is the composition root for the lanes**, so it is the place that
turns each of `PushSupervisor`'s three injected callables into
`async with factory() as session: ...`. A supervisor that held a session
would hold it for the life of a socket -- hours, idle in transaction, with a
snapshot from whenever the lane started.
"""

import asyncio
import uuid
from collections.abc import Awaitable, Callable

from loguru import logger
from sqlalchemy.ext.asyncio import AsyncSession, async_sessionmaker

from usher.config import Settings
from usher.domain.source import Source
from usher.ports.events import EventPublisher
from usher.ports.source import SourceAdapter, SourceAdapterFactory, SourceEvent
from usher.services.push import PushApplyService, PushOutcome, PushSupervisor
from usher.telemetry import PushSnapshot


class LaneSupervisor:
    def __init__(
        self,
        settings: Settings,
        session_factory: async_sessionmaker[AsyncSession],
        adapters: SourceAdapterFactory,
        events: EventPublisher,
        *,
        build_worker: Callable[[AsyncSession], Awaitable[int]] | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        self._settings = settings
        self._sessions = session_factory
        self._adapters = adapters
        self._events = events
        self._build_worker = build_worker
        self._lanes: dict[uuid.UUID, asyncio.Task[None]] = {}
        self._names: dict[uuid.UUID, str] = {}
        self._open_adapters: dict[uuid.UUID, SourceAdapter] = {}
        self._worker: asyncio.Task[None] | None = None
        self._refresher: asyncio.Task[None] | None = None

    # -- lifecycle -------------------------------------------------------

    async def start(self) -> None:
        if self._settings.worker_enabled:
            self._worker = asyncio.create_task(self._run_worker(), name="usher.lane.worker")
        if self._settings.push_enabled:
            await self.refresh()
            self._refresher = asyncio.create_task(self._refresh_loop(), name="usher.lane.refresh")

    async def stop(self) -> None:
        """Cancel every lane, then close every adapter.

        In that order: an adapter closed under a live lane makes the lane's
        next call raise `PortUnavailable`, which the supervisor would count
        as a failure and back off on -- during shutdown, into a task that is
        about to be cancelled anyway. Cancelling first makes shutdown quiet.
        """
        tasks = [task for task in (self._worker, self._refresher, *self._lanes.values()) if task]
        for task in tasks:
            task.cancel()
        # `return_exceptions=True`: a lane that was cancelled mid-await
        # raises `CancelledError` here, and one that had already crashed
        # raises whatever it crashed with. Neither may stop the rest of
        # shutdown.
        await asyncio.gather(*tasks, return_exceptions=True)
        self._lanes.clear()
        self._worker = None
        self._refresher = None
        for adapter in self._open_adapters.values():
            await adapter.aclose()
        self._open_adapters.clear()

    # -- observation, for the health route and PRD 10's gauges -----------

    def running_sources(self) -> list[str]:
        return sorted(
            self._names[source_id]
            for source_id, task in self._lanes.items()
            if not task.done()
        )

    def worker_running(self) -> bool:
        return self._worker is not None and not self._worker.done()

    def push_snapshots(self) -> dict[str, PushSnapshot]:
        """PRD 10's two push series, read live off each adapter's ledger.

        An in-memory integer per source, which is why an observable OTel
        callback may read this directly -- see
        `usher.telemetry.register_queue_gauges` for why the queue's
        equivalent may not.
        """
        return {
            self._names[source_id]: PushSnapshot(
                delivering=adapter.supports_push,
                reconnects=_reconnects(adapter),
            )
            for source_id, adapter in self._open_adapters.items()
        }

    def push_available(self, source_id: uuid.UUID) -> bool | None:
        """What `GET /admin/sources/{id}/status` reports, or `None` when no
        lane is running for that source -- "not probed", which is the honest
        answer rather than `False`."""
        adapter = self._open_adapters.get(source_id)
        return None if adapter is None else adapter.supports_push

    # -- the push lanes --------------------------------------------------

    async def refresh(self) -> None:
        """Start a lane for every enabled source that has none, and cancel
        the lanes of sources that have gone or been disabled.

        A source added through `POST /admin/sources` gets a lane without a
        restart, which PRD 08 requires of everything else about a source
        and would otherwise be false for push alone. A *disabled* one loses
        its lane, because `enabled` is how an operator parks a server that
        is being rebuilt and a lane would keep the backoff schedule warm
        against a machine nobody wants touched.
        """
        async with self._sessions() as session:
            sources = [
                source
                for source in await PostgresSourceRepository(session).list_all()
                if source.enabled
            ]
        wanted = {source.id: source for source in sources}
        for source_id in list(self._lanes):
            if source_id not in wanted or self._lanes[source_id].done():
                await self._stop_lane(source_id)
        for source_id, source in wanted.items():
            if source_id not in self._lanes:
                await self._start_lane(source)

    async def _refresh_loop(self) -> None:
        while True:
            await asyncio.sleep(self._settings.push_source_refresh_seconds)
            try:
                await self.refresh()
            except asyncio.CancelledError:
                raise
            except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
                # A failed refresh must not end the refresher: a database
                # blip would otherwise leave the lane set frozen until a
                # restart, which is exactly the shape PRD 08's degradation
                # table refuses everywhere else.
                logger.warning("refreshing push lanes failed: {error}", error=str(exc))

    async def _start_lane(self, source: Source) -> None:
        adapter = await self._open_adapter(source)
        if adapter is None:
            return
        self._names[source.id] = source.name
        self._open_adapters[source.id] = adapter
        supervisor = PushSupervisor(
            self._apply,
            self._close_gap,
            self._write_push_available,
            user_id=await self._default_user_id(),
            max_consecutive_failures=self._settings.push_max_consecutive_failures,
            backoff_seconds=self._settings.push_backoff_seconds,
            max_backoff_seconds=self._settings.push_max_backoff_seconds,
            gap_min_interval_seconds=self._settings.push_gap_min_interval_seconds,
        )
        self._lanes[source.id] = asyncio.create_task(
            self._guard(source, supervisor.run(source, adapter)),
            name=f"usher.lane.push.{source.name}",
        )

    async def _guard(self, source: Source, lane: Awaitable[None]) -> None:
        """One crashed lane costs its own source and nothing else.

        `PushSupervisor.run` never raises a `UsherPortError`; anything that
        escapes it is a bug, and a bug in one source's lane must not take
        the other sources' lanes -- or the HTTP server -- down with it. A
        single `TaskGroup` over every lane would do exactly that.
        """
        try:
            await lane
        except asyncio.CancelledError:
            raise
        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
            logger.exception(
                "the push lane for {source} crashed and will not restart until the "
                "next refresh: {error}",
                source=source.name,
                error=str(exc),
            )

    async def _stop_lane(self, source_id: uuid.UUID) -> None:
        task = self._lanes.pop(source_id, None)
        if task is not None:
            task.cancel()
            await asyncio.gather(task, return_exceptions=True)
        adapter = self._open_adapters.pop(source_id, None)
        if adapter is not None:
            await adapter.aclose()
```

The three unit-of-work callables, each `async with self._sessions() as session:` and each committing through the session it opened:

```python
    async def _apply(
        self, source: Source, adapter: SourceAdapter, event: SourceEvent
    ) -> PushOutcome:
        async with self._sessions() as session:
            applier = PushApplyService(
                _ingest_service(session, self._settings),
                _watch_service(session, self._settings),
                self._events,
                session.commit,
                max_items_per_event=self._settings.push_max_items_per_event,
            )
            return await applier.apply(
                source, adapter, event, user_id=await self._default_user_id()
            )

    async def _close_gap(self, source: Source, adapter: SourceAdapter) -> None:
        """PRD 03's reconnect delta: the item lane, then the watch lane.

        In that order, and for the reason `usher sync` runs them in that
        order: `WatchStateSyncService` resolves each state against a
        `MediaItem`, so a watch walk that ran first would count every state
        unmatched and merge nothing.
        """
        async with self._sessions() as session:
            pipeline = _reconcile_and_watch(session, self._settings, self._events)
            await pipeline.reconcile.reconcile(source, SyncRunKind.DELTA, adapter)
            await pipeline.watch.sync(source, adapter, user_id=await self._default_user_id())

    async def _write_push_available(self, source: Source, available: bool) -> None:
        async with self._sessions() as session:
            repository = PostgresSourceRepository(session)
            stored = await repository.get(source.id)
            if stored is None or stored.supports_push == available:
                # No write when nothing changed. `sources` has a
                # `set_updated_at` trigger, so an unconditional update would
                # move `updated_at` on every reconnect of a flapping lane.
                return
            await repository.update(stored.evolve(supports_push=available))
            await session.commit()

    async def _run_worker(self) -> None:
        """PRD 08's queue consumer, in the process the SSE clients are
        connected to.

        Polls rather than listens, at the same `_IDLE_SLEEP_SECONDS` floor
        `usher work` uses -- and the comment there applies unchanged: it is
        the polling floor of a lane that already has push as its real
        answer for *inbound* work. What it drains is Usher's own queue,
        which has no push.
        """
        while True:
            try:
                async with self._sessions() as session:
                    ran = await self._worker_pass(session)
            except asyncio.CancelledError:
                raise
            except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
                logger.warning("the worker lane's pass failed: {error}", error=str(exc))
                ran = 0
            if ran == 0:
                await asyncio.sleep(_IDLE_SLEEP_SECONDS)
```

> **`_ingest_service`, `_watch_service`, `_reconcile_and_watch` and `_worker_pass` are the wiring `usher.cli._build_pipeline` already contains**, and this is the place the plan is thinner than it looks. Do not retype them: extract `usher.cli._Pipeline`/`_build_pipeline` into a shared module — `src/usher/composition.py`, importable by both `usher.api.lanes` and `usher.cli` — and call it from both. The extraction is mechanical (the function takes a session and a `Settings` today and would take an `EventPublisher` too), it removes the second copy this task would otherwise create, and it is the reason the import-linter contract list does not need a seventh entry: `usher.composition` sits inside the same layer both roots already occupy. `usher.cli`'s own contract ("nothing may import it") is why the shared code cannot simply live there.

- [ ] **Step 4: Wire the lifespan**

```python
# src/usher/api/app.py
    @asynccontextmanager
    async def lifespan(app: FastAPI) -> AsyncIterator[None]:
        engine = build_engine(settings.database_url.get_secret_value())
        session_factory = build_session_factory(engine)
        app.state.session_factory = session_factory
        bus = InMemoryEventBus(
            buffer_size=settings.sse_buffer_size, queue_size=settings.sse_queue_size
        )
        app.state.events = bus
        register_sse_gauge(lambda: bus.subscribers)
        lanes = LaneSupervisor(
            settings,
            session_factory,
            ConfiguredSourceAdapterFactory(
                page_size=settings.source_page_size,
                timeout_seconds=settings.source_timeout_seconds,
                reauth_cooldown_seconds=settings.source_reauth_cooldown_seconds,
            ),
            bus,
        )
        app.state.lanes = lanes
        register_push_gauges(lanes.push_snapshots)
        await lanes.start()
        try:
            yield
        finally:
            # The comment this block has carried since M1 -- "M5 onward adds
            # websocket connections, job workers, and HTTP clients to this
            # same lifespan, where a skipped cleanup call is a real leak" --
            # stops being a prediction here. `stop()` first: an engine
            # disposed under a live lane makes that lane's next statement
            # raise into a task that is about to be cancelled anyway.
            await lanes.stop()
            await engine.dispose()
```

**`lanes.start()` opens no database connection**, and that is load-bearing for the same reason the engine build does not: `/health` must keep answering 200 with Postgres down while `/health/ready` reports 503 (verified live against a real container in M1). `start()` creates tasks; the first thing each task does is open a session, and a failure there is logged and retried by the refresher rather than raised into the lifespan. Assert it:

```python
# tests/unit/test_api_lanes.py  (append)
async def test_starting_the_lanes_opens_no_database_connection() -> None:
    """`create_app`'s lifespan builds an engine and opens no connection, and
    that is what makes `/health` answer 200 with Postgres down while
    `/health/ready` reports 503 -- verified live against a real container in
    M1. A lane supervisor that connected at `start()` would turn a database
    outage into a failure to boot."""
    app = create_app(_settings_with_unreachable_database())
    async with LifespanManager(app):
        pass  # must not raise
```

## Task 27: Readiness reports the lanes, and never gates on them

PRD 08 says `/health/ready` "reports Postgres, migration state, and per-source connectivity — degraded rather than binary, so a dashboard can distinguish 'down' from 'running without Emby'." Only the first two are implemented, and the third needs a correction before it is.

**Per-source connectivity must not be probed by a readiness check.** A probe makes an upstream request, `GET /health/ready` is polled by Docker every 2 s in the shipped compose file, and PRD 01 measures that upstream at 1–5 s per request. Worse, it inverts M1's own liveness/readiness argument: a readiness check that fails because Emby is down takes Usher out of a load balancer for a reason restarting Usher cannot fix, which is precisely what the split exists to avoid — and PRD 08's own failure table says a source being unreachable leaves the catalog "fully browsable".

**So: the lanes are reported in the body and never in the status code.** Push state is free (an in-memory ledger), it is genuinely useful to a dashboard, and it changes nothing about whether this process should receive traffic. `database` and `migrations` keep gating the code, exactly as they do today.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/usher/api/dto/health.py`, `src/usher/api/routers/health.py`
- Modify: `src/usher/services/sources.py`, `src/usher/api/deps.py`
- Modify: `tests/unit/test_api_health.py`, `docs/prd/08-operations.md`

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the lane report**

```python
# src/usher/api/dto/health.py
class LaneReport(BaseModel):
    """Which background lanes this process is running.

    **Reported, never gated on.** `ReadinessResponse.status` and its HTTP
    code are computed from `checks` alone, and this field is deliberately
    outside that model rather than a fourth boolean inside it: `ready`'s
    `all(checks.model_dump().values())` would then take this process out of
    a load balancer because Emby is unreachable, which restarting it cannot
    fix and which PRD 08's own failure table says leaves the catalog fully
    browsable.
    """

    push: list[str]
    worker: bool


class ReadinessResponse(BaseModel):
    status: Literal["ready", "degraded"]
    checks: ReadinessChecks
    lanes: LaneReport
```

```python
# src/usher/api/routers/health.py  (in `ready`)
    checks = ReadinessChecks(database=database_ok, migrations=migrations_ok)
    # `checks` alone, exactly as before. The lanes are reported below and
    # are not in this expression -- see `LaneReport`.
    is_ready = all(checks.model_dump().values())
    response.status_code = 200 if is_ready else 503
    return ReadinessResponse(
        status="ready" if is_ready else "degraded",
        checks=checks,
        lanes=LaneReport(push=lanes.running_sources(), worker=lanes.worker_running()),
    )
```

with `lanes: LaneSupervisorDep` added to the handler's signature and a `get_lane_supervisor` provider on `app.state.lanes`, in the same defensive `getattr`/`cast` shape as `get_session_factory`.

```python
# src/usher/services/sources.py
    def __init__(
        self,
        sources: SourceRepository,
        credentials: CredentialStore,
        adapters: SourceAdapterFactory,
        push_health: Callable[[uuid.UUID], bool | None] | None = None,
    ) -> None:
```

and in `status()`, after `verify()` returns:

```python
        try:
            status = await adapter.verify()
        finally:
            await adapter.aclose()
        if self._push_health is None:
            return status
        # The *running lane's* answer, not this throwaway adapter's.
        # `verify()` opens no socket (a status screen a dashboard polls must
        # not cost one per poll), so a fresh adapter can only ever report
        # `None` here -- and `None` from the lane means the same thing, "not
        # probed", for a source whose lane has not started.
        return replace(status, push_available=self._push_health(source_id))
```

`SourceStatus` is a frozen dataclass, so `dataclasses.replace` is the write path — the domain-model `.evolve()` rule is about `DomainModel` subclasses and does not apply to port DTOs.

| Mutation | Expected failure |
|---|---|
| `is_ready = all(checks.model_dump().values()) and lanes.running_sources()` | `test_a_source_whose_push_is_down_does_not_make_this_process_unready` |
| Put `push` inside `ReadinessChecks` | Same case — `ready`'s `all(...)` picks it up automatically, which is exactly why the field is outside that model |
| Probe each source in `ready` | `test_readiness_never_probes_a_source` |
| `SourceService.status` ignores `push_health` | `test_source_status_reports_the_running_lanes_push_health` |
| `push_health` returning `False` for an unknown source | `test_source_status_reports_null_when_no_lane_is_running` |

- [ ] **Step 6: Add `usher push`**

The operator trigger, and the on-demand form of the probe. `usher push --probe --source NAME` opens a channel, waits `push_stale_after_seconds`, and prints what arrived; bare `usher push` runs the lanes in the foreground with no HTTP server, for an operator watching a source that is misbehaving.

```python
# src/usher/cli.py  (in build_parser)
    push = sub.add_parser("push", help="run the push lane, or probe a source's push channel")
    push.add_argument("--source", default=None, help="source name; omit for every enabled source")
    push.add_argument(
        "--probe",
        action="store_true",
        help="connect, wait, and report what arrived, then exit",
    )
```

```python
async def _push(settings: Settings, *, source_name: str | None, probe: bool) -> None:
    """Run the push lane, or probe it once.

    `--probe` is the operator-facing form of ADR-0004's caveat: it reports
    the **messages and events that arrived**, never that the handshake
    succeeded, because a handshake against a nonexistent path also upgrades.
    It is the one thing in this project that opens a socket on purpose to
    answer a question, which is why `verify()` does not have to.
    """
    async with _session_for(settings) as session:
        pipeline = _build_pipeline(session, settings)
        sources = await _selected_sources(pipeline, source_name)
        if not sources:
            print("no enabled source matched" if source_name else "no enabled sources configured")
            return
        for source in sources:
            adapter = await _open_adapter(pipeline, source)
            if adapter is None:
                continue
            try:
                if probe:
                    result = await adapter.probe_push(
                        timeout_seconds=settings.push_stale_after_seconds
                    )
                    print(
                        f"{source.name}: upgraded={result.upgraded} "
                        f"delivering={result.delivering} "
                        f"events={[kind.value for kind in result.events] or 'none'}"
                        + (f" detail={result.detail}" if result.detail else "")
                    )
                else:
                    await _run_lane(session, settings, source, adapter)
            finally:
                await adapter.aclose()
```

```bash
PRD 08 says readiness reports per-source connectivity; probing one there
would make a request per 2s Docker poll against an upstream measured at 1-5s
per request, and would take this process out of a load balancer because Emby
is down -- which restarting it cannot fix and which PRD 08's own failure
table says leaves the catalog fully browsable. Reported in the body,
computed from an in-memory ledger, outside the model 'ready' is derived
from.

usher push --probe is the on-demand answer: it prints what arrived, never
that the handshake succeeded."
```

In `docs/prd/08-operations.md`, correct the observability paragraph:

```markdown
`GET /health` is liveness; `GET /health/ready` reports Postgres and
migration state — and **gates its status code on those two alone**. Source
and lane state are reported in the body (`lanes.push`, `lanes.worker`, and
`GET /admin/sources/{id}/status`'s `push_available`) and never in the code,
which is a correction to what this section said before M5 built the lanes:
a readiness probe that failed because a source was unreachable would take
the process out of a load balancer for a reason restarting it cannot fix,
which is the same argument that keeps liveness off the database. The
failure table above already prices an unreachable source as "catalog fully
browsable"; a 503 would contradict it.

Push state is free to report — it is an in-memory ledger of messages
received, not a probe — and readiness therefore makes **no upstream
request at all**. The shipped compose healthcheck polls this endpoint every
2 s against a source PRD 01 measures at 1–5 s per request.
```

## Task 28: A real socket, a real stream, and a shape that would catch a quadratic

Four integration files, each closing a gap the unit suite structurally cannot.

**1. A real `websockets` client against a real `websockets` server on `127.0.0.1`.** Every unit test of the channel runs against `FakePushConnection`, which performs no handshake at all — so a wrong scheme, a missing `Upgrade`, a subprotocol mismatch, a real close code and a real ping/pong are all invisible. A loopback server is not Emby, and the gap that leaves is stated rather than papered over; what it *does* close is everything between `EmbyPushChannel` and the wire. **This is permitted under the no-network rule**: the guard `CLAUDE.md` describes blocks anything that is not loopback, and `testcontainers` already reaches `127.0.0.1`. Re-run the guard as part of the final gate.

**2. The SSE stream through a real app and a real request**, so `StreamingResponse`'s own cancellation semantics, `get_session`'s commit boundary, and the ordering between "the enrichment committed" and "the client was told" are exercised rather than assumed.

**3. `GET /titles/{id}` against real Postgres**, because `FakeMediaItemRepository` has no foreign keys and `FakeWatchStateRepository` stores `observed_at` as `updated_at` — the two divergences M4 measured, and both are on this route's read path.

**4. A measurement shaped so a quadratic would show.** M4's lesson: "A statement-count assertion needs the right thing held fixed." Here the thing that could be quadratic is the SSE fan-out and the push lane's per-event database cost, so the measurement holds the **event count** fixed and varies the **subscriber count**, and separately holds the batch count fixed and varies the items per event.

**Files:**
- Create: `tests/integration/test_push_loopback.py`, `test_sse_end_to_end.py`, `test_titles_route.py`, `test_push_lane_end_to_end.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: The loopback socket**

```python
# tests/integration/test_push_loopback.py
"""`EmbyPushChannel` over a real `websockets` client and server.

**Why this is not a network test.** The server binds `127.0.0.1:0` and the
client connects to it; `CLAUDE.md`'s socket guard blocks anything that is
not loopback and leaves loopback alone, which is the same allowance
`testcontainers` runs under. Re-run the guard after this file exists -- the
whole point of that check is that a green suite proves nothing about it.

**What it closes that no unit test can.** `FakePushConnection` performs no
handshake: a wrong scheme, a missing `Upgrade` header, a proxy answering
404, a subprotocol mismatch, a real close code, a real ping/pong, and the
library's own logging are every one of them invisible against it. All of
those are real here.

**What it does not close.** This server is `websockets`, not Emby. It
accepts any path, answers any subscription, and sends whatever this file
tells it to -- so a wrong route, a wrong subscription frame, and a wrong
message envelope all pass. Only M5's live run closes those, and it is a
named step of this milestone.
"""

import asyncio
import json
import logging

import pytest
from websockets.asyncio.server import ServerConnection, serve

from usher.adapters.emby.push import EmbyPushChannel, PushHealth, connect_websocket
from usher.config import Settings
from usher.ports.errors import PortUnavailable
from usher.ports.source import SourceEventKind
from usher.telemetry import configure_logging

pytestmark = pytest.mark.integration


class _Server:
    """A real WebSocket server that records what a client sent it."""

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.paths: list[str] = []
        self.received: list[str] = []
        self.to_send: list[str] = []
        self.close_after_subscribe = False
        self._connected = asyncio.Event()

    async def handle(self, connection: ServerConnection) -> None:
        self.paths.append(connection.request.path)
        self._connected.set()
        subscription = await connection.recv()
        self.received.append(str(subscription))
        if self.close_after_subscribe:
            await connection.close(code=1011, reason="upstream error")
            return
        for frame in self.to_send:
            await connection.send(frame)
        await asyncio.sleep(3600)


@pytest.fixture
async def server():
    handler = _Server()
    async with serve(handler.handle, "127.0.0.1", 0) as running:
        host, port = running.sockets[0].getsockname()[:2]
        handler.base_url = f"http://{host}:{port}"
        yield handler


def _channel(server, **kwargs) -> EmbyPushChannel:
    return EmbyPushChannel(
        _StubSession(),
        base_url=server.base_url,
        device_id="device-1",
        health=PushHealth(stale_after=kwargs.pop("stale_after", 90.0)),
        # `proxy=None` on the real connector: `websockets` 16 reads
        # HTTPS_PROXY/WS_PROXY from the environment by default, and a
        # developer machine with one set would send a loopback connection
        # through it.
        connect=lambda url: connect_websocket(url, proxy=None),
        poll_seconds=0.05,
        **kwargs,
    )


async def test_a_real_handshake_carries_the_token_and_the_device_id(server) -> None:
    server.to_send = [json.dumps({"MessageType": "Sessions", "Data": []})]
    channel = _channel(server)
    async with channel.open() as events:
        await asyncio.wait_for(_first_or_none(events), timeout=5.0)
    assert server.paths == ["/embywebsocket?api_key=session-token-1&deviceId=device-1"]


async def test_the_subscription_frame_arrives_verbatim(server) -> None:
    server.to_send = [json.dumps({"MessageType": "Sessions", "Data": []})]
    async with _channel(server).open() as events:
        await asyncio.wait_for(_first_or_none(events), timeout=5.0)
    assert json.loads(server.received[0]) == {"MessageType": "SessionsStart", "Data": "0,1000"}


async def test_a_real_message_becomes_a_real_event(server) -> None:
    server.to_send = [
        json.dumps({"MessageType": "LibraryChanged", "Data": {"ItemsAdded": ["90000100"]}})
    ]
    channel = _channel(server)
    async with channel.open() as events:
        event = await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(events)), timeout=5.0)
    assert event.kind is SourceEventKind.ITEM_ADDED
    assert channel.health.messages_received == 1


async def test_a_real_server_close_raises_port_unavailable(server) -> None:
    """A real close frame with a real code, translated. The unit suite
    arranges this with `FakePushConnection.drop`, which raises the exception
    the wrapper is *supposed* to produce -- so it can only ever prove the
    channel propagates one, never that the wrapper produces it."""
    server.close_after_subscribe = True
    async with _channel(server).open() as events:
        with pytest.raises(PortUnavailable):
            await asyncio.wait_for(anext(aiter(events)), timeout=5.0)


async def test_a_real_ping_gets_a_real_pong(server) -> None:
    """PRD 03's heartbeat requirement, satisfied by the library's default
    and asserted rather than trusted: "Emby sends no keepalive of its own.
    nginx closes idle connections at 60 s and Cloudflare at ~100 s, so the
    client must generate traffic." A future `websockets` default of
    `ping_interval=None` would silently remove it."""
    server.to_send = []
    channel = _channel(server)
    async with channel.open():
        await asyncio.sleep(0.1)
        connection = next(iter(_server_connections(server)))
        pong = await connection.ping()
        await asyncio.wait_for(pong, timeout=5.0)


async def test_a_failed_connection_names_no_url(server) -> None:
    """`websockets.exceptions.InvalidURI.__str__` contains the URI, and this
    URI contains the token. Arranged against a port nothing is listening on,
    which is a real `OSError` rather than a fake's `PortUnavailable`."""
    channel = _channel(server)
    channel._base_url = "http://127.0.0.1:1"  # noqa: SLF001
    with pytest.raises(PortUnavailable) as caught:
        async with channel.open():
            pass
    assert "session-token-1" not in str(caught.value)


async def test_the_library_cannot_log_the_token_at_debug(server, capsys) -> None:
    """The leak Task 8 found, closed and proved against the real library
    rather than against a hand-written log call. `websockets/client.py:294`
    debug-logs the request line, which for this channel carries `api_key=`,
    and `configure_logging` forces every logger to propagate into a handler
    on root at level 0."""
    configure_logging(
        Settings(
            database_url="postgresql+asyncpg://u:p@localhost/db",
            secret_key="0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef",
            log_level="DEBUG",
            log_json=False,
        )
    )
    logging.getLogger().setLevel(logging.DEBUG)
    server.to_send = [json.dumps({"MessageType": "Sessions", "Data": []})]
    async with _channel(server).open() as events:
        await asyncio.wait_for(_first_or_none(events), timeout=5.0)
    captured = capsys.readouterr()
    assert "session-token-1" not in captured.out
    assert "session-token-1" not in captured.err
```

`connect_websocket` gains a `proxy: str | None | Literal[True] = True` parameter passed straight through, so the loopback fixture can turn it off. Default unchanged — a household behind a proxy is a real deployment and `websockets`' env-var default serves it.

- [ ] **Step 2: SSE end to end**

```python
# tests/integration/test_sse_end_to_end.py
"""PRD 03's read-through loop, closed, through a real app.

`open → stub → promote → enrich → title.updated → the client refetches and
gets the enriched row`. Every hop is real: a real request, a real Postgres,
a real `StreamingResponse`, and `get_session`'s own commit boundary.
"""

pytestmark = pytest.mark.integration


async def test_opening_a_stub_promotes_it_and_the_client_is_told_when_it_lands(
    client, app, session, seeded_stub
) -> None:
    """The loop PRD 03 diagrams, end to end.

    The **ordering** is what only this test can see: `EnrichService`
    publishes after its commit, so a client that refetches the instant it
    receives `title.updated` reads the enriched row. Publishing before the
    commit passes every unit test -- a fake repository has no transaction --
    and races here.
    """
    async with client.stream("GET", f"/events?titles={seeded_stub.title_id}") as stream:
        lines = aiter(stream.aiter_lines())
        opened = await client.get(f"/titles/{seeded_stub.title_id}")
        assert opened.json()["enrichment_state"] == "stub"

        claimed = await _claim_one_job(session)
        assert claimed.priority == 100, "the open did not promote to DEMAND"

        await _run_enrichment(app, claimed)
        frame = await asyncio.wait_for(_read_frame(lines), timeout=10.0)
        assert "event: title.updated" in frame

        refetched = await client.get(f"/titles/{seeded_stub.title_id}")
        assert refetched.json()["enrichment_state"] == "enriched", (
            "the client was told before the enrichment committed"
        )


async def test_a_second_open_writes_no_row(client, session, seeded_stub) -> None:
    """`enqueue` reports 0 for work that has not changed -- M4's `WHERE
    jobs.priority < excluded.priority`, first called from a client here. A
    detail screen a user opens twice must not cost a row version."""
    await client.get(f"/titles/{seeded_stub.title_id}")
    before = await _job_xmin(session, seeded_stub.title_id)
    await client.get(f"/titles/{seeded_stub.title_id}")
    assert await _job_xmin(session, seeded_stub.title_id) == before


async def test_a_disconnected_client_is_unsubscribed(client, app) -> None:
    bus = app.state.events
    async with client.stream("GET", "/events"):
        await asyncio.sleep(0.1)
        assert bus.subscribers == 1
    for _ in range(50):
        await asyncio.sleep(0.02)
        if bus.subscribers == 0:
            break
    assert bus.subscribers == 0


async def test_a_slow_client_is_told_to_resync_and_the_publisher_is_unaffected(
    client, app
) -> None:
    """The one property the bus exists for, through a real response. A
    client that opens the stream and never reads it fills its queue; the
    publisher must finish anyway, and the client must be told rather than
    left quietly stale."""
    bus = app.state.events
    async with client.stream("GET", "/events") as stream:
        await asyncio.sleep(0.1)
        for index in range(bus._queue_size * 4):  # noqa: SLF001
            await asyncio.wait_for(
                bus.publish(ClientEvent(kind=ClientEventKind.SYNC_PROGRESS, data={"n": index})),
                timeout=0.5,
            )
        frames = [await asyncio.wait_for(_read_frame(aiter(stream.aiter_lines())), timeout=5.0)]
    assert any("resync_required" in frame for frame in frames)
```

> **`test_sse_end_to_end.py` writes durably.** `get_session` is the request's commit boundary, so the stub title and the enqueued job survive the test — and M4's own experience is that leaving `titles` and `jobs` behind took down four tests in three other files, each of which passed in isolation. This module's fixture must `DELETE FROM jobs` and `DELETE FROM titles WHERE id = …` in its teardown. `media_items` and `sync_runs` cascade from the source and do not.

```python
# tests/integration/test_push_lane_end_to_end.py
"""Push → ingest → merge → SSE, against real Postgres, and its cost."""

pytestmark = pytest.mark.integration


async def test_a_pushed_watch_state_lands_and_is_published(session, bus) -> None:
    """The milestone in one case: an event carrying a position merges
    through `apply_states` and reaches an SSE subscriber."""


async def test_a_pushed_state_cannot_zero_real_play_history(session) -> None:
    """ADR-0014 on the push path, against the layer where it is permanent.
    Seed a row holding `play_count = 13` through a raw `INSERT` (the trigger
    is `BEFORE UPDATE`, so an insert is the only way to own the column),
    apply a push event carrying `play_count=None`, and read back **13**.

    Invisible against the fakes: `FakeWatchStateRepository`'s Python
    `if value is not None` is `COALESCE`-shaped by accident, and the real
    merge's natural one-statement spelling zeroes it -- measured, and the
    reason `merge_from_source` is two statements."""


async def test_a_pushed_played_item_enqueues_exactly_one_history_backfill(session) -> None:
    """`(kind, key)` is unique, so a film paused and resumed six times is
    one `watch_history` job rather than six. At a household's viewing rate
    the difference is a queue that stays small against one that grows with
    playback."""


async def test_the_push_lanes_cost_per_event_does_not_grow_with_the_library(session) -> None:
    """**The measurement shaped so a quadratic would show.**

    M4's lesson: "A statement-count assertion needs the right thing held
    fixed." Here the candidate quadratic is a per-item database round trip
    inside an event, so this holds the **event count** fixed at 20 and
    varies the items per event (1, then 10), and asserts the statements per
    *event* are flat rather than the statements per item.

    Captured off `before_cursor_execute`, never transcribed. Ten items in
    one event must cost the same statements as one, because `apply_states`
    resolves the batch in one `resolve_targets` and merges it in one
    `merge_from_source`.
    """


async def test_the_sse_fan_out_does_not_grow_with_the_subscriber_count(bus) -> None:
    """The other candidate. One publish is O(subscribers) by construction --
    that is what a fan-out is -- and what must *not* grow is the work per
    subscriber: `offer` is a `put_nowait` and a branch. Holds the event
    count fixed at 100 and varies subscribers (1, then 50), asserting the
    wall time per publish grows sub-linearly and that no publish awaits.

    A wall-clock assertion is weak evidence on a loaded host, so this
    asserts the *shape* -- 50 subscribers within 10x of 1 -- rather than a
    number, and the real guarantee stays
    `test_publishing_does_not_block_on_a_subscriber_that_is_not_reading`,
    which is measured on intervals.
    """
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Re-prove the network guard, both halves**

A `sitecustomize.py` that is not on `PYTHONPATH` produces identical output to one that is and blocks nothing. Both checks, or the run proves nothing:

```bash
cd /home/anirudhlath/code/usher
cat > /tmp/netguard/sitecustomize.py <<'PY'
import socket
_real_connect = socket.socket.connect
_real_connect_ex = socket.socket.connect_ex
_real_getaddrinfo = socket.getaddrinfo


def _loopback(address):
    return isinstance(address, tuple) and str(address[0]) in {"127.0.0.1", "::1", "localhost"}


def connect(self, address):
    if self.family.name != "AF_UNIX" and not _loopback(address):
        raise RuntimeError(f"NETWORK BLOCKED: {address}")
    return _real_connect(self, address)


def connect_ex(self, address):
    if self.family.name != "AF_UNIX" and not _loopback(address):
        raise RuntimeError(f"NETWORK BLOCKED: {address}")
    return _real_connect_ex(self, address)


def getaddrinfo(host, *args, **kwargs):
    if host not in {"127.0.0.1", "::1", "localhost", None}:
        raise RuntimeError(f"NETWORK BLOCKED: {host}")
    return _real_getaddrinfo(host, *args, **kwargs)


socket.socket.connect = connect
socket.socket.connect_ex = connect_ex
socket.getaddrinfo = getaddrinfo
print("[netguard] installed")
PY
PYTHONPATH=/tmp/netguard uv run python -c "
import socket
try:
    socket.getaddrinfo('api.themoviedb.org', 443)
except RuntimeError as exc:
    print('guard is live:', exc)
else:
    raise SystemExit('THE GUARD IS NOT INSTALLED -- this run would prove nothing')
"
PYTHONPATH=/tmp/netguard uv run pytest -q 2>&1 | tail -5
```

Expected: `[netguard] installed`, then `guard is live: NETWORK BLOCKED: api.themoviedb.org`, then the full suite passing with zero blocks. **The loopback WebSocket server and `testcontainers` both go through `127.0.0.1` and are unaffected**, which is the point of the loopback allowance rather than a hole in it.

## Task 29: Live verification, the mutation sweep, and the documentation

M3's live run found the watch-state write-back route was simply wrong, and 40 contract assertions had been passing against it because `FakeEmbyServer` implemented the adapter's own guess. M5 has the identical property in a worse place: **no run in this repository has ever seen a real `/embywebsocket` message.** `tests/fixtures/emby/push_*.json` is transcribed from Emby's DTOs and from ADR-0004's decompilation, `FakeEmbyServer` renders exactly that shape, and `to_source_events` parses exactly that shape. If the envelope is wrong, everything is green and the push lane delivers nothing.

- [ ] **Step 1: Run the whole mutation sweep in one pass**

Every mutation named in Tasks 1–28, run in sequence against the **full** suite rather than its own task's selection — a per-task sweep cannot see collateral in another file, which is the point of a final sweep.

```bash
cd /home/anirudhlath/code/usher
find src tests -name __pycache__ -type d -exec rm -rf {} +
export PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1
git status --porcelain   # must be empty before starting
```

For each: `cp` the file aside, apply the mutation, `uv run pytest -q`, confirm the named case (and only sensible collateral) fails, restore from the `cp`, confirm green. **In place, never in a `/tmp` copy** — `cp -r` copies `.venv/bin/*` with absolute shebangs into the original venv, so the mutation is never imported and the suite runs green while proving nothing. **`cp` backups rather than `git checkout --`**, which clobbers uncommitted work in the same file. **A run that collected zero tests is `DID-NOT-RUN`, never `KILLED`** — the harness must assert that a run actually ran.

Six mutations in this plan are named as expected to **survive**, each with its reason (`propagate = False` alongside the level; `list(self._subscribers)`; `partition` vs `rpartition`; the `CancelledError` arm; the `is ENRICHED` guard; the `stale_after` boundary). Confirm each survives for the stated reason rather than a different one, and record any that survives for a *different* reason as a missing test.

Record any mutation the suite does not catch and does not have a stated reason. Each is a missing test, and writing it is part of this step.

- [ ] **Step 2: Live-verify against the real Emby server**

Driven from a throwaway script **outside the working tree**, reading the operator's own secrets file, redacting every credential, token, user id and host from anything it prints — the rules M3's and M4's runs followed and `CLAUDE.md` records. **Bounded in the iterator, never by `max_pages`** (exhausting `max_pages` raises `PortDataMalformed`, so a walk bounded that way records `FAILED` and never reaches the sweep). **No "find the item where X" over a walk** — that is a walk of 1,126,789 items, and it is the mistake M4's run made and paid several hundred requests for.

Seven things only a live run can settle, in the order that makes each cheap:

1. **What Emby actually sends, and how often.** Connect, subscribe with `SessionsStart`, and record **every** message's `MessageType` and its top-level shape for a bounded window (10 minutes). This is the measurement the whole milestone's fixtures rest on. Diff the recorded shapes against `tests/fixtures/emby/push_*.json` — field names, nesting, types, nothing else — and correct the fixtures where they differ, shape-only, exactly as `scripts/capture_tmdb_fixture.py` does for TMDb. **Record the `Sessions` interval**, because `push_stale_after_seconds`' default of 90 s is derived from an assumption about it.
2. **Does `UserDataChanged` carry a trustworthy `PlayCount` and `LastPlayedDate`?** Toggle one item played out of band and compare the pushed entry against `GET /Users/{u}/Items/{item}` for the same item in the same second. A positive answer is a follow-up worth taking — it removes one `watch_history` job per played item — and is deliberately **not** assumed by M5.
3. **Does the socket stay up for hours?** Hold it for at least four hours, unattended, logging every message with a timestamp and every reconnect. This is the question `push_stale_after_seconds`, `ping_interval` and the whole supervisor exist for, and no fake can answer it. Report: total messages, message types, the longest silence observed, and the number of reconnects.
4. **What happens on network loss, and does reconnection re-deliver or drop?** Take the interface down (or block the port) for 60 s with a known change made *during* the outage, then restore. Confirm: the channel raises rather than hanging, the supervisor reconnects, the gap-closing delta walk runs, and **the change made during the outage is in the catalog afterwards**. That last clause is the one that matters — it is the whole justification for running the delta on reconnect, and it is the only way to learn whether Emby re-delivers (it is not expected to; the delta is the cover).
5. **Does `/embywebsocket` accept `X-Emby-Token` as a header instead of `api_key` in the query?** One connection with `additional_headers={"X-Emby-Token": token}` and no `api_key`. A positive answer removes the token from the URL entirely — from `request.path`, from the library's logging, from any proxy access log — and would be a genuine narrowing of ADR-0012's live risk rather than a mitigation of it. Recorded either way; **not** implemented in this run.
6. **Does `AuthenticateByName`'s own response carry `Policy`?** One authentication with a real password, inspecting the response for `User.Policy.IsAdministrator`. A positive answer saves Task 3's extra `GET /Users/{userId}` on every `verify()`.
7. **The health rule, against the real server.** Open a channel and assert `supports_push` is `False` before the first message arrives and `True` after. Then — the one that is the milestone — connect to a **nonexistent path** (`/embywebsocket-nope`) and confirm ADR-0004's finding still holds on this build: the handshake upgrades, `Sessions` arrives, and therefore `supports_push` reports `True` on a path that means nothing. **That is not a bug in this design; it is the evidence for it**, and it is worth re-measuring because the whole milestone is built on it being true.

**No write to a real account is required by any of the above except item 2**, and that one must record the item's complete `UserData` first and restore it exactly afterwards, confirmed by reading it back — the rule M3's and M4's runs both followed. Choose an item whose `UserData` is already all-zero so the restore is exact; on any other item `PlayCount` is not restorable by any route this project knows.

Record what was and was not run, explicitly, the way `CLAUDE.md`'s "Not verified, and the docs say so rather than implying coverage" paragraphs do.
