Two new ADRs, written out in the commit that adds them:

```markdown
# ADR-0018 — Push health is a message ledger, never an open socket

**Status:** Accepted. Implemented in M5.

## Context

[ADR-0004](0004-push-over-polling.md) verified Emby push end to end and
recorded one quirk: **a handshake against a nonexistent path also upgrades
and also receives `Sessions`.** So a successful upgrade is not evidence of
anything about the path, the subscription, or a proxy in between.

The same state arrives from three other directions. A reverse proxy that
forwards `Upgrade` and then buffers. A NAT table entry dropped while both
endpoints still believe the connection is open. A server that accepted the
subscription and lost the listener. In every one the connection object is
healthy and nothing is arriving.

`SourceAdapter.supports_push` is what PRD 03's reconciler reads to decide
whether a source needs covering. An adapter that answered it from the socket
would tell the reconciler to stand down for a source that has silently
stopped pushing.

## Decision

**No answer about push health is derived from the state of a socket.**

`PushHealth` counts received messages. `supports_push` is
`connected AND messages_received > 0 AND now - last_message_at <=
stale_after`, and there is no code path from "a connection object exists" to
`True`. A channel that has delivered nothing for `stale_after` raises
`PortUnavailable` out of its own iterator, so the lane stops holding it.
`PushSupervisor` resets its consecutive-failure counter on *delivery*, never
on connection, so an upgraded-but-silent socket walks that counter to its
ceiling and ends with `supports_push = false` persisted.

`verify()` opens no socket at all: it reports `null` ("not probed") for an
adapter with no channel and the running lane's ledger otherwise.
`SourceAdapter.probe_push` is a **concrete** method on the port whose body
is calls to `events()` and `supports_push`, so every adapter inherits the
rule rather than re-deriving it.

## Consequences

**Gained:** the failure ADR-0004 warns about is detected, acted on, and
visible — `usher.source.push.connected` reports delivery, so PRD 10's "Push
down" alert fires on it instead of being permanently green.

**Given up:** an idle library's channel stays healthy only because Emby
sends periodic `Sessions`. If a future build stopped, `stale_after` would
fire on a healthy socket and the lane would reconnect every 90 s — visible
in `usher.source.push.reconnects`, cheap (the gap-closing delta returns 0
items), and tunable, but wrong. The `Sessions` interval is measured in M5's
live run for exactly this reason.

**Also:** this is layered with `websockets`' own `ping_interval`/
`ping_timeout` rather than replacing them. Those detect a dead TCP peer.
This detects a live one that has stopped delivering, and neither substitutes
for the other.

**Rejected:** probing on every `GET /admin/sources/{id}/status`. A dashboard
polling that route would open a socket per poll against a server PRD 01
measures at 1–5 s per request, and would still be answering a question about
a socket that is not the one doing the work.

## Evidence

ADR-0004's own control handshake, 2026-07-29: a connection to a nonexistent
path upgraded and received `Sessions`. Re-measured in M5's live run against
the same build — see `CLAUDE.md`.
```

```markdown
# ADR-0019 — The client event channel is a port, with one implementation

**Status:** Accepted. Implemented in M5.

## Context

PRD 03 ends its read-through loop with "the client is told when it changes",
and PRD 07 specifies `GET /events` with a `?titles=` filter, `Last-Event-ID`
replay and `resync_required` on overflow. Three services publish
(`EnrichService`, `PushApplyService`, `ReconcileService`) and all three may
depend only on `domain/` and `ports/` (ADR-0009).

M5's bus is in-memory, which means it is in-process — and `usher work` is a
separate process, so an enrichment completing there reaches no SSE client.

## Decision

`EventPublisher` is a port with one method (`publish`) and one
implementation in M5 (`InMemoryEventBus`), plus a `NullEventPublisher` for
composition roots with no clients.

**Subscription is not on the port.** A Postgres `LISTEN/NOTIFY`
implementation publishes with an `INSERT` plus a `NOTIFY` and subscribes on
a dedicated connection whose lifecycle has nothing in common with an
in-memory queue's; one ABC carrying both would draw the shape around the
first implementation.

**The server process runs the job worker**, gated by `worker_enabled`, so
PRD 03's loop closes in the shipped deployment.

## Consequences

**Gained:** a service publishes through a contract rather than through three
independently-injected callables, and the contract is drawn to be
satisfiable by a lossy transport — every case is about one subscriber's
stream, and `resync_required` answers every gap.

**Given up:** a port with one implementation, which ADR-0001's own reasoning
says to be careful about spending effort on. Bought back by three callers, a
named second implementation, and a `NullEventPublisher` that is a real
deployment rather than a test double.

**Accepted:** `usher work` as a standalone process publishes nowhere. A
client that refetches still gets the enriched title — degradation rather
than breakage, PRD 08's own principle — and the fix is the second
implementation rather than a branch in three services.

**Also:** `bootstrap.progress`, which PRD 07 lists, is not emitted for
exactly this reason, and PRD 07 now says so rather than leaving a client
waiting on a handler that never fires.
```

`CLAUDE.md` gains, under "Verified facts worth not re-deriving":

- **What the live run recorded about real `/embywebsocket` messages**, in the same shape M3's and M4's entries use: what was confirmed, what was corrected, and what remains unverified.
- **The measured `Sessions` interval**, and whether `push_stale_after_seconds`' default survives it.
- **Whether the socket stayed up for four hours**, with the message count, the longest silence, and the reconnect count.
- **What reconnection re-delivered or dropped**, and whether the gap-closing delta recovered the change made during the outage.
- **The `websockets` DEBUG-logging leak** and the level-based fix, since it is a trap any future long-lived-client work in this repository will meet again.
- **The nonexistent-path handshake, re-measured**, since the whole milestone rests on it.
- The status line: **M5 complete**, with the new commands, routes and test count.

- [ ] **Step 4: Final gate**

```bash
cd /home/anirudhlath/code/usher
uv run pytest -q 2>&1 | tail -3
uv run pytest tests/unit -q 2>&1 | tail -3
uv run mypy src tests
uv run ruff check --no-cache . && uv run ruff format --check .
uv run lint-imports
python3 - <<'EOF'
import pathlib
import re

# `docs/prd/` plus the two documents at the repo root, and **not**
# `docs/plans/`. That exclusion is a correction rather than a convenience:
# a plan embeds PRD and ADR text whose links are relative to where the
# snippet will *live* (`docs/prd/`), not to where it is quoted, so every
# such link is "broken" from the plan's own directory and always has been.
# `.claude/rules/prd-maintenance.md` gives this check over `docs/`, and
# M4's final gate recorded `Expected: ... OK` against it — verified while
# writing this plan that it has never printed `OK`: M2, M3 and M4 each
# report between one and eight of these, every one an embedded snippet.
# Scoping it to the files whose links have to resolve is what makes a green
# result mean something.
roots = list(pathlib.Path("docs/prd").rglob("*.md"))
roots += [pathlib.Path("CLAUDE.md"), pathlib.Path("README.md")]
bad = []
for md in roots:
    for link in re.findall(r'\]\(([^)#][^)]*\.md)\)', md.read_text()):
        if not (md.parent / link).resolve().exists():
            bad.append(f"{md}: {link}")
print("\n".join(bad) if bad else "OK")
EOF
```

Expected: all pass, `Contracts: 6 kept, 0 broken`, `OK`, zero warnings.

> **`OK` requires fixing one link that is broken today.** `README.md` points
> at `docs/prd/decisions/0015-availability-is-swept-not-diffed.md`, which was
> renamed to `0015-availability-is-retracted-only-by-a-finished-walk.md`
> during M4 and never updated — found by running this check while writing
> this plan, which is also how the plan-directory exclusion above was found.
> Fix it in this task's commit.

### Placeholder scan

Tasks 1–27 carry complete code for every code step. **Three places are thinner than that and are named rather than hidden:**

1. **Task 28's four integration files** carry property statements, mechanisms and the reason a fake cannot see each, but not assertion bodies. That is a real thinning; the fixtures each one needs already exist in three named files in this repository, so the expansion is mechanical. M4's own self-review recorded the same thinning for six of its tasks.
2. **Task 26's `_ingest_service` / `_watch_service` / `_reconcile_and_watch` / `_worker_pass`** are named as an *extraction* of `usher.cli._build_pipeline` into `src/usher/composition.py` rather than written out — deliberately, because writing them out would create the second copy the extraction exists to prevent.
3. **Task 19's contract-class bodies** are shown as `...` with an explicit instruction that they are a **move** of four complete functions written three pages earlier, not stubs to fill in.

Four places deliberately name something not yet written and say so: `PushHealth`/`redact_query` (Task 4/1, used from Task 6 on), `_replay` (stubbed in Task 18, filled in Task 19), `self._health` in `verify()` (Task 3 writes `None`, Task 9 replaces it), and `can_disable_push`/`disable_push` on `SourceHarness` (introduced in Task 12's prose, implemented by `FakeSourceHarness` only).

Two places name a line that will not survive `ruff` and say so, so the engineer's first run is not spent on it: `FakePushConnection.recv`'s dead `if` (Task 6, with the corrected two-line form given) and the possibly-unused `SourceWatchState` import in `push.py` (Task 5).

**Six mutations are named as expected to survive**, each with its reason, so a sweep that kills one of them has found a change in behaviour rather than a win: `propagate = False` alongside the level (Task 8), `list(self._subscribers)` (Task 18), `partition` vs `rpartition` (Task 19), the `CancelledError` arm (Task 15), the `is ENRICHED` guard (Task 23), and the `stale_after` boundary comparison (Task 7).

### Type consistency

Checked across tasks. `PushHealth` is constructed in Task 4, handed to `EmbyPushChannel` in Task 6, held by `EmbyAdapter` in Task 9, read by `supports_push` in 9 and by `LaneSupervisor.push_snapshots` in 26, and mapped to `telemetry.PushSnapshot` in 16 — one object, one `stale_after`, `reconnects` incremented in exactly one place (Task 16's `record_open`). `PushConnection`'s three methods are defined in 4, implemented by `_WebsocketsConnection` in 8 and by `FakePushConnection` in 6, and `recv(timeout) -> str` raising `TimeoutError` is relied on by the watchdog in 7. `SourceEvent(kind, external_ids, watch_states)` is constructed in 5, asserted in 12, consumed in 14. `PushOutcome.deferred_to_delta` is set in 14 and is the only field 15 branches on. `MergeOutcome(merged, unmatched, rows_written, needing_history)` is returned by 13 and read by 13's own `_flush` and by 14's `_apply_watch_state` — `merged` is targets and `rows_written` is a count, and the two are never interchanged. `ClientEvent(kind, title_id, episode_id, data)` is defined in 17, published in 14 and 21, filtered in 18, replayed in 19, encoded in 20. `SentEvent(id, epoch, event)` is minted in 18 and encoded in 20; `epoch` is a `str` on both sides and is parsed back in 19. `TitleDetail(title, availability, watch_state, promoted)` is returned by 23 and consumed by 24's `TitleResponse.of`. `PushProbe(upgraded, delivering, events, detail)` is defined on the port in 9 and printed by the CLI in 27.

Two inconsistencies found and fixed while reviewing:

- **`SourceHarness.push_stale_after` is declared as a method** (`def push_stale_after(self) -> float`) and Task 12's contract cases call it as `harness.push_stale_after()`, while `FakeSourceAdapter` exposes it as a plain *attribute* (`self.push_stale_after = 90.0`). The harness method wrapping the adapter's attribute is correct; the collision of names on two different objects is not. Rename the adapter's attribute to `push_stale_after_seconds` when writing Task 10.
- **`PushApplyService._publish_watch_states` zips `targets` against `states`** and those two lists are only aligned when every state resolved to a target — which is exactly the case `unmatched > 0` breaks. `apply_states` returns `merged` in the order it built them and skips unmatched, so the zip silently pairs the wrong state with the wrong target for any batch containing one unmatched item. Fix when writing Task 14: have `MergeOutcome.merged` carry `tuple[tuple[str, MediaItemTarget], ...]` — the `external_id` alongside its target — and key the publish off that instead of zipping. The same fix removes the "slightly over-published in one direction" caveat that method's docstring carries.
